# HAVELLIN PALM BEACH

*Concierge Job Playbook · Running a Job End to End · Internal*

---

This is the **how do I run a job** document. It follows one job in order, from the first phone call to the final invoice, and tells you which tab to be on, what to type, and what the app will refuse to let you do until something earlier is done.

It deliberately does not explain how the app is built. Setup, Apps Script URLs, folder IDs, the pricing engine's formulas, the vendor taxonomy and every other configuration matter live in the **Operations Manual** (`manual.html`). If something here says "the app works this out," the manual is where the arithmetic is written down.

> **The Operations Manual is the master document.** It covers everything in this playbook and everything this playbook leaves out. This is the walk-through-it-in-order extract, written for running a job with the app open — where the two disagree, the manual is right and this file needs fixing. Both are reconciled against the app together; the version stamps at the foot of each say when. Plain-text copies for a phone or a printer: `CONCIERGE_GUIDE.md` and `MANUAL.md`.

> **Two things are recorded by hand.** The client's acceptance, and every payment except a bank transfer made through our ACH payment link, which records itself once it settles (Step 9). If you don't record them, the app does not know they happened, and the next step stays locked.
>
> **⚠ The signed agreement used to be the third, and since 2026-09-17 it depends on how you sent it.** Sent through **DocuSign**, the signature comes back on its own and *you cannot record it by hand*. Sent as a **PDF**, you record it exactly as before. Step 6 is where you choose, and Step 8 is what happens next.

## The Job, Start to Finish

```
1. Intake → 2. Build the estimate → 3. Manager approves it → 4. Email it to the client
→ 5. Client says yes → Mark Won → 6. Send the signing packet → 7. Staff the job team
→ 8. Signed copy back → 9. Record the deposit → 10. Work the job: document, log hours, source vendors
→ 11. Midpoint invoice → 12. Final invoice → 13. Close the job
```

> **⚠** **You run the whole job from ONE screen now — the Client Dashboard (changed 2026-09-11).** Open a client and you get a **timeline** of the job from intake to final payment, and above it a brown **band** that names the one step to do next and carries every button for it; the timeline's own rows carry none. **Client Estimate, Agreement and Invoices are gone from the top of the screen.** Nothing they did was removed — it all moved onto the timeline. Anthony asked for this in as many words: *"we are finding ourselves having to go to too many tabs … that way, as we're going through a job, we know what to do next."* **Exactly one step is ever lit up**, and that is the one to do next. If a step is red it is blocked, and the band tells you both why and how to clear it. If you are hunting for a button, open the client and look in the band. A document from an earlier stage is in the strip at the foot of the timeline. The steps below are still the order of the job; they are just no longer the order of the tabs.
>
> **⚠ And since 2026-09-23 there is no Client Intake tab and no Build Estimate tab either.** Both open from the dashboard as a full screen: **+ Add New Client** at the top of the client list for a new client, and the brown **Build estimate** button on the client's own timeline for the estimate. Each has a bar across the top with a **←** button that takes you back. The tabs across the top are now the work on a client on the **left** — Client Dashboard, Job Plan, Job Admin & Inv — and the people we call on the **right** — Contractors, Vendors, Referral Partners. **⚠ There is no Win / Loss tab either, from the same day.** The won and lost figures are a row on the Client Dashboard, straight under Total Jobs. Press **Won** or **Lost** to see the clients behind the number; press a name to open that client.

> **On a phone in the house, press 📱 Field in the header.** The tabs drop to a bottom bar you can reach with a thumb — **Clients · Job Plan · Vendors** — and everything is sized for one hand. The other three tabs are desk work and are hidden, not removed; nothing you type is lost. **Intake and Estimate are not in that bar any more because they are not tabs** — on the phone you reach them exactly as at the desk: **+ Add New Client** when a prospect calls while you are out, **Build estimate** on the client's timeline when you walk in for the walkthrough. **Clients is new there as of 2026-09-11, and its buttons work.** So the three things you actually do standing in somebody's kitchen are all on the timeline, on your phone: **record a cheque** they just handed you, **record the signed agreement** they just handed back *(on a PDF job — a DocuSign one records itself)*, and **mark them won** after the walkthrough conversation. Do them there and then — that is the whole point, and it beats a note to yourself for later. Press it again to come back to the full six tabs. Whatever you were looking at stays put.

> **⚠** **Four of the five documents go out the same way: the estimate and the deposit, midpoint and final invoices.** **✉ Send…** → read the draft in your Gmail and send it → **✓ I've sent it**. The fifth, the signing packet, goes through **DocuSign** (**✉ Send for signature — DocuSign**): DocuSign emails it to the client itself, so the app records it as sent and there is nothing to confirm. Only a client you send it to by hand (**📄 Send as PDF to sign by hand**, Step 6) gets the Gmail route, and a packet you hand over yourself is recorded with **🤝 Handed over in person** (Step 6, since 2026-10-01). The brown band carries the current document's **👁 View**, **🖨 Print** and, once it is filed, **📁 Filed** buttons, each named for its document (*View estimate*, *Filed packet*); an earlier document's are in the strip at the foot of the timeline. Learn it once on the estimate and you know how to send an invoice. **Why you press two buttons on a Gmail send, and not one.** The app builds the email and puts it in *your* Gmail as a draft — it deliberately cannot send it, because every client email gets read by a person first. So it cannot know when you actually sent it. **✉ Send** makes the draft; **✓ I've sent it** is you telling the app it went. Until you press the second one the step reads *"Drafted — read it, send it, then confirm"* (or *"Opened as a plain email — send it from your mail app, then confirm"* when Gmail failed and a plain email opened instead) and stays open. **Do not skip it** — on the estimate, that confirmation is what lets you mark the job Won.

**Five points in that sequence are hard gates.** The app refuses — it does not warn, and there is no override:

| You cannot… | Until… | Why |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Email the estimate | A manager has approved it | The figure isn't final until somebody has signed off on it. |
| Staff the job team, or open the staffing section of the Job Plan | The job is marked **Won** | Holding a specialist for these dates means turning down other work. Never commit crew to a job the client hasn't accepted. |
| Activate the job | The agreement is signed and the deposit is in; on a matter going through probate, the Letters are also *Received* or marked *Not Required*, and on a trust-only matter the successor trustee's **Certification of Trust** (since 2026-10-03, P19) | A job starts under a signed contract with the money in hand, and on probate the Letters are the representative's authority over the property; on a trust the Certification of Trust is the trustee's. |
| Log hours | The job team is confirmed (Step 7) **and** the **deposit** is recorded in full | The deposit is never waived, so this gate is always safe to enforce. |
| Issue the final invoice on an hourly job | Hours are logged | The final bills labour at hours actually worked. No hours means there is nothing behind the number, so no manager can unlock it. A fixed-price final bills the agreed fee, and a fee-only Home Prep final bills the fee on vendor spend, so neither waits on the log — log the hours anyway (Step 10b). |

## Step 1 · Intake — + Add New Client, on the Client Dashboard

Press **+ Add New Client** at the top of the Client Dashboard. Fill in everything marked *. The Job ID (`HVL-YYMM-XXXX`) and the client's Drive folders are created for you on save, and **Save Client drops you straight onto the new client's own dashboard** — its brown button is what to do next. **← Clients** at the top takes you back without saving, and what you typed is still there if you come back to it.

> **On a phone, the form reads straight down in one column — and it was reordered on 2026-09-22 so it reads in the order you actually ask.** First **who and where**: the client, the address, then the house itself — type, square footage, beds and baths, value, the walkthrough and target dates. Then **the conversation**: the two *what's in the house* questions and the tick list, your notes, and on an estate job the representative, the estate details, the attorney and the two documentation questions. Before this, everything about the house sat at the very bottom: on a probate intake you scrolled past the whole estate block, the attorney and the court record before you got to *how big is it*. **Nothing changed about what is saved** — only the order it appears in.

> **⚠** **⚠ Two of these are optional on the form and will stop you dead at the estimate.** **Approximate home value**, on every job, and on a **Home Transition** the **New home sq ft**. The form lets you save without either — and **Build Estimate then refuses to save the walkthrough** until they are there. Ask for both on the call: the value is a Zillow ballpark, and the new home's size is what prices move day. **If you find out later, it is not a disaster** — open the client on the Client Dashboard and press **✎ Edit Client**; both are there from 2026-09-22, and nothing you have scored is lost. Before that date there was nowhere to put either one and the only way out was to re-create the client.

> **On an estate job you are now asked whether the Letters are in hand (new 2026-09-22).** It sits with the authorized representative: **Pending · Received · Not Required**, and it opens on *Pending*, which is the right answer on almost every first call. Say *Received* only if a certified copy is already in hand, and *Not Required* on a matter with no Letters at all. **On probate this is what stops the job being activated**, so it is worth asking rather than leaving; when the copy turns up later you set it under **✎ Edit Client**. Before this date the app did not really ask — it read a field that was not on the form, so every estate job sat at Pending whatever you were told on the call.
>
> **⚠ It is the MATTER that decides, from 2026-09-29, not the service.** An **Estate Settlement whose estate is going through probate** (you answered *Probate* or *Both* to how it is being administered) now gets the **Probate Details** block too — case number, Letters date, the §733.604 deadline — nothing in it required, and it **cannot be activated until the Letters are Received** (or marked Not Required), exactly like a Probate job. Before, that job had nowhere to put its case number and activated with no Letters on file. A trust matter is never asked for the Letters (it has its own paper since 2026-10-03, below). Since 2026-10-02 (P18) the desk's documents print a case number only on a probate matter, so an estate re-answered as a trust never carries an old case number on its paperwork.

> **⚠ A trust has its own paper, and the form names it (new 2026-10-03, P19).** Answer *How is this estate being administered?* and the authority question under the representative changes its name to match: **Letters of Administration** on a probate matter (or *Both*), **Certification of Trust** on a trust — the successor trustee's proof of authority, under Fla. Stat. §736.1017 — and it disappears on *Neither*, where there is no paper. Same three answers, same rule: leave it *Pending* unless a copy is in hand, because **on a trust this is now what stops the job being activated**, exactly as the Letters do on probate. On a trust (and on *Both*) you are also asked the **Trust Details** — the name of the trust, the date it was made and the day the successor trustee accepted, all on the Certification, all optional — and **Property sale required?**, which used to be asked on probate only. **Co-representatives:** if two people serve together (co-executors, co-trustees), press **+ Add a co-representative** under the representative and record each one, name first. Each one approves the releases with the representative and, since 2026-10-05 (P20), signs the agreement beside them: each signs in DocuSign beside the client, so **record each one's email** — a co-representative with none stops the DocuSign send. (Until 2026-10-05 a DocuSign envelope went to the representative alone, and whether the others signed was not decided.)

> **The Move Destination block asks three things, not six (2026-09-22).** Address, city, and the new home's square footage. **Zip, bedrooms and bathrooms are gone** — nothing in the app ever read one of them, so they were three more questions on a call that is already long. If a client volunteers the layout, it goes in *Notes*.

- **Service type** — Home Editing · Home Transition · Home Cleanout · Estate Settlement · Probate Estate Settlement · Contested Probate Estate Settlement · Home Prep for Sale.
- **Square footage** is what drives the sizing. Property type is just a label. Bedrooms, full baths and half baths are separate fields, so "five baths" is never ambiguous.
- **All three decedent services need an authorised representative** — executor, personal representative or administrator, with name, role, phone and email — **and a date of death**. *Power of Attorney* is not one of the roles (since 2026-10-01): a power of attorney ends at death, so it cannot act for an estate. A client recorded with it before then keeps it, shown *(as recorded)* in Edit Client; change it to the role the person actually holds. Probate also needs the case number and, there, the attorney's details are required. On an *Estate Settlement* the attorney fields are offered and the asterisks disappear, because plenty of estate settlements never open probate at all; fill them in whenever there is an attorney, since the Job Plan asks for one by name.
- **Home Cleanout is for a LIVING client. A house whose owner has died is an *Estate Settlement*.** Two different engagements at two different prices — the service type you pick here is what decides how every client document is written, and there is no second question anywhere that lets you correct it.
- **How is this estate being administered?** (every estate job, new 2026-09-21) — *Probate*, *Trust*, *Both* or *Neither*. Ask the attorney or the representative: *"is this going through probate, or is it a trust?"* A pour-over will is usually **Both**. **It has no default and the form will not save without it** (it carries the red * since 2026-09-30, like every other field the form requires), on purpose — this is the one field on the page where guessing is worse than asking, because guessing probate is what puts a court schedule in front of a successor trustee. **On a Probate service, a *Trust* or *Neither* answer brings up an amber box under the question (new 2026-10-05):** there is no court on this matter, and the Probate service prices court work that will not happen, so switch the service to *Estate Settlement* (the *Service type* dropdown at the top) unless Anthony wants the court work priced. It never stops Save Client and nothing switches by itself; it goes when you change the service or the answer. *Contested Probate* is never flagged: a fight over a trust usually ends up in court too. Either way, on a trust or *Neither* the client's documents call the service *Estate Settlement* or *Contested Estate Settlement*, never Probate.
- **Two documentation questions, on every estate job.** *Is a federal estate tax return (Form 706) being filed?* and *Is there a known dispute, contest or elective share?* Ask them; do not guess. **Unknown counts as yes** on the 706, so leaving it alone puts the job in Strict Mode. A dispute also drops the specialist-appraisal threshold from $3,000 to $500 an item. The box under them tells you, in words, what level the job is at and why.
- **Documentation level** — Auto, Standard, or Formal (court & attorney grade). **It is on the right-hand side now** (moved 2026-09-22), directly under the box that explains it, instead of buried in the property details. On an estate it is usually **greyed out with a reason beside it**, because the questions above have already decided it — and on a fresh estate job it always is, since the 706 defaults to *Unknown* and unknown counts as yes. You can always make a job stricter by hand; you can never make it looser here. Formal turns on the appraisal guardrails and makes chain-of-custody mandatory. Separate from the Premium-estate rate — a premium job is not automatically a court-grade one.
- **What are we contracted to produce?** (every estate job, new 2026-09-21 — it replaces *Who builds the inventory?*) — ask the attorney: *do you want us to build the inventory, or does your paralegal? And do you want appraisals arranged?* Four answers, least to most:

  | Pick | What we hand over | What counsel does |
  | --- | --- | --- |
  | **Contents list** | Every item of consequence photographed, described, located and given a condition. **No values.** | Values it, books any appraiser not on our estimate, prepares the filing. |
  | **Inventory with values** | The contents list plus our estimated value at the date of death, with the basis of each value stated. | Books any appraiser not on our estimate, prepares the filing. |
  | **Inventory + appraisals** | The valued inventory plus appraisers engaged, booked, and their reports attached. | Prepares the filing. |
  | **None** | We sort, stage and set aside against counsel's own schedule. No inventory. | Does the whole inventory and the filing. |

  This is **what the agreement promises**, and it also seeds the estimate's *Documentation scope* in Step 2, which prices it. You can still change the scope on the walkthrough; the hint under that dropdown will name the disagreement. **Answer or change it later on ✎ Edit Client and an estimate nobody has approved follows it** (since 2026-09-30): the one open on Build Estimate at once, a saved one when it is next opened. An approved estimate is not repriced; the save tells you how. **You may leave it blank on the call** — the attorney often has not decided — but since 2026-09-22 **the estimate will not save until it is answered**, so it has to be settled before you can quote.

  **An appraiser is ours only when it is on the estimate (2026-09-30).** On any tier, an appraiser you add under Vendors when you build the estimate (Step 2) is priced there and named in the agreement: we coordinate the appraisals Exhibit A lists, and counsel books any other. On *Inventory + appraisals* we arrange every appraisal, so add each appraiser the job needs. No tier requires one and none refuses one.
- **Referral source** — if it came from an attorney, realtor or trust officer, link the actual Referral Partner so they get credit on the leaderboard. Family and friend referrals use the free-text name field. Only what is on screen is saved: pick a partner and then switch to *Family* and the partner is dropped.
- **Assigned concierge is optional here.** Intake usually runs a fortnight ahead of the work and nobody knows who is free. You staff the job properly at step 7.

> **Getting something wrong on the call is fixable on ✎ Edit Client, which follows this form's rules (2026-09-30).** It carries everything asked here (years in home, beds and baths and the referral partner included), it will not let a save clear a required field, and it never quietly blanks something it cannot show: a concierge who has left the roster, a court-appointed role, an old referral source all stay as recorded. Two things it will not do. It will not move a living client onto an estate service or the reverse (*Create a new client for this*: they are different people). And on an estate it does not ask for the deceased's phone or email; every email and DocuSign envelope goes to the representative (and, since 2026-10-05, each co-representative signs in the same envelope). **Notes typed over several lines keep their lines**, on the dashboard and through an Edit Client save (since 2026-09-30), and whatever you type anywhere on the client is shown exactly as typed. **A Probate service on a trust or *Neither*** carries the same amber box under *How is this estate being administered?*, on open and as you change the service or the answer, and Save Changes repeats it in its notice, until the estimate is approved (2026-10-05).
>
> **Dates:** a start after the hard target, or before the walkthrough, is kept and named in red under the dates on both forms. It used to be cleared without a word. **Addresses:** if the street is already on file, the form names the client there; if it is the same person, open them from the dashboard instead. Saving goes ahead either way. **Picked an estate service by mistake?** Switch back: the phone and email you typed come back.

> **⚠** **Get the service type right — it is the most consequential field on this form.** Pick *Home Cleanout* and the app writes to a living owner of their own property; pick *Estate Settlement* and it writes to the **authorised representative** of someone who has died, asks for the deceased's name instead of a client phone and email, and generates a different engagement agreement. There is no tick box and no override. A cleanout booked for a family whose mother died last month, filed under *Home Cleanout*, addresses her as the living owner of her own house — to the people who just buried her — **and under-prices the job**, because the estate services carry the documentation and disposition work a cleanout does not.
>
> **What you CAN fix later:** the service within its own family, living to living or estate to estate — on Build Estimate's service dropdown, right on the walkthrough (from 2026-09-10), or in **✎ Edit Client** (from 2026-09-30). Once the estimate is approved both hold it: press **✎ Edit estimate** to reopen it, which you can do until the signing packet has gone out. What you cannot do anywhere is cross between the two families: that is a different client, so it is **+ Add New Client**. Both pickers offer only the job's own family, and Edit Client's save refuses the switch (*Create a new client for this*).

> **The workbook follows the matter type too, from 2026-09-21.** On a matter recorded *Trust* or *Neither*, the Summary sheet in the client’s own Drive workbook drops *Letters Issued*, the *§733.604 Inventory Deadline* and the *Exempt §732.402* count — all three are things that only exist inside a probate case — and its footer names the trust instrument instead of a court filing. **The valuation half stays**: date of death, the FMV total, what is still awaiting valuation. A successor trustee needs those numbers exactly as much as a personal representative does. **If a trust family’s workbook still shows a court deadline, the matter type is unanswered** — fix it in Edit Client, then make any edit on the client's Job Admin & Inv tab to rewrite the sheet.

> **⚠ The Court Inventory is the PROBATE document, and on a trust matter it is the wrong one.** It lists Probate-track and Exempt-track items only, so on an estate whose contents are held in trust it comes out **empty** — and until 2026-09-21 an empty one printed *FINAL* in green, totalled **$0**, and offered a signature line for a fiduciary to adopt it. Measured on an estate holding $19,000 of furniture. **If you printed one before 2026-09-21 on a trust matter, throw it away and check nobody signed it.** It now refuses: DRAFT, no signature, and on a matter recorded *Trust* or *Neither* a red block at the top saying why. **The trustee's own schedule is built now (2026-09-21) and it is what you hand a trust family:** *Trust Schedule*, under **More** on the Job Admin & Inv tab; since 2026-10-02 (P18) **📧 Send trust package**, on the *Trust Information* card, sends it with the rest of the inventory to the trustee's attorney, or to the trustee (below). Do not send a trust family a court inventory.

> **⚠ *Inventory + appraisals* is a promise to arrange every appraisal, and it turns Strict Mode on.** Pick it and the *Documentation level* dropdown greys out at **Formal** with the reason beside it, and that changes how the job runs: anything flagged has to be appraised or waived before the Court Inventory or the Trust Schedule will come off DRAFT, the appraisal panel turns red and blocks instead of nudging, and chain of custody becomes mandatory — every item shot in place before it moves. That is the standard the client is paying for, so it is right — but do not pick it because it sounds thorough. **Pick it when the attorney wants us to arrange the appraisals**, and when you build the estimate add each appraiser under Vendors (Step 2): since 2026-09-30 the tier prices exactly like *Inventory with values*, and each appraiser is priced by its own line. **Leaving it blank is allowed, and on a first call it is often the honest answer.** There is no default, on purpose. What is not allowed is quoting with it blank: since 2026-09-22 **Save Estimate**, **Submit for approval** and the manager's PIN all refuse until it is answered (Step 2), because the agreement is the thing promising it.

> **⚠ On *Inventory with values*, counsel books the appraisers — unless one is on our estimate.** The agreement says so in bold, and since 2026-09-24 it and the estimate follow the tier rather than the price: on that tier we give the appraiser access, are there for the visit if asked, and hand counsel our list of the items we think need one — but engaging and scheduling the appraisers is theirs. **Since 2026-09-30 an appraiser you add under Vendors is the exception, on any tier:** the agreement then reads *"Havellin will coordinate the professional appraisals listed in Exhibit A (Art Appraiser); any other appraisal remains the responsibility of the Client and the estate attorney."* So if the attorney wants us to handle one specialist, add that appraiser to the estimate; if they want us to arrange them all, the answer is ***Inventory + appraisals***, which puts the job in Strict Mode (above). Either way, make the change (the tier in Edit Client; the appraiser on the estimate, through **✎ Edit estimate** if it is already approved), re-price and re-approve before the agreement goes out. **An agreement or estimate printed on a values job before 2026-09-24 promised appraisal coordination — throw it away and regenerate it.**

> **The 706 answer does one more thing, from 2026-09-22.** On an estate recorded as filing a federal return, the app applies **Treas. Reg. §20.2031-6(a)**: a group of articles may be listed as one lot only where no article in it is worth more than **$100**. You will see it in three places — a line under each collection in *From the Estimate Walkthrough* as you choose *Keep as lot* or *Itemize*, a **Lots to split** chip on Job Admin & Inv, and its own block on the Appraisal Worklist beside the §20.2031-6(b) aggregate. It never blocks anything. **It is not the documentation level that turns it on** — a Strict estate filing no return gets none of it.

> **⚠ Until 2026-09-21 an Estate Settlement was never asked any of this, and it cost real money silently.** The date of death, the inventory question (now *What are we contracted to produce?*), both documentation questions and the estate attorney were on the **probate** form only. So an estate settlement was **quoted at full documentation** — roughly a third of the ticket — even when counsel had told you their paralegal was doing the inventory, sat in **Strict Mode** with the level dropdown greyed out and no explanation on screen, and carried no date of death although every value on that estate is a value *at* that date. **Open any estate settlement you took before then in Edit Client and fill the four in.** If its estimate is already approved and the inventory answer was wrong, the quote is wrong: re-price it and re-approve before you send anything else. And do not guess the 706 answer to make the greyed-out dropdown go away — portability elections mean estates well under the exemption still file. Ask the attorney, put their answer in, and leave it at Unknown until you have one.

### What's in the house — ask these on every call

New 2026-09-10. This replaced the old single *Notes* box as the main thing intake asks about the property. Two questions, in these words, then the checklist:

> **On a Home Prep for Sale call, ask question 2 (safety) and the *Access & security* row** (Q17, 2026-09-30). The app hides the must-find question, the other six rows and the documentation level on prep — nothing leaves the house. Pets, the alarm code, who has keys, a gate, anything a painter or stager needs to know still goes in.

1. **"Is there anything in the house you need us to find? Something valuable, or something that matters to the family, that we absolutely cannot miss or accidentally dispose of?"**
2. **"Is there anything in the house we need to be aware of for safety, or that needs special handling?"**

Then tick what is in the house and **write down what you are told** — the tick on its own is worth almost nothing:

| Tick | Then write |
| --- | --- |
| **Cash** — loose cash, envelopes, hidden spots | Roughly how much, and where. |
| **Valuables** — jewellery, gold/silver, watches, coins, art, collectibles | What, where, who gets it, and whether they want it appraised. |
| **Firearms & ammunition** | How many, where, and *who is authorised to take possession*. |
| **Safes & lockboxes** | Where it is, whether they have the combination or key, whether they know what is inside. |
| **Documents & digital** | Will or trust, deeds, insurance, statements, passports — and the digital ones people forget: crypto wallets, thumb drives, seed phrases, old laptops and phones. What they are looking for, and where it might be. |
| **Sentimental must-finds** — photos, letters, specific heirlooms | Name them. "Sentimental things" is not findable; "the blue photo album from the Navy years" is. |
| **Access & security** | Alarm and codes, cameras, smart locks, who else has keys — caregivers, cleaners, family — and any gate or HOA. Anything we need to disarm. |

> **⚠** **Everything you write here is read out to the crew before Day 1. That is the whole point of asking.** It comes back as a **Standing job flags** panel at the top of the Job Plan, in every room's workspace and on the dashboard, so the two people emptying the house know about the cash in the freezer and the ring in the blue box. If you tick a row and leave the note blank, the crew sees *"Ticked at intake, no detail recorded — ask the client before Day 1"* — which is honest, but it means somebody has to make the call you should have made.
>
> **Question 1: one line per item.** The crew ticks each one off in the house (**Found it**, Step 10a), and a paragraph cannot be ticked. *"Coin collection in the study safe"*, new line, *"the ring in the blue box"*. The box says so.
>
> **Firearms is the red one, and it prints first.** It carries the standing rule with it wherever it appears: *nothing moves without written authority, photograph it where it lies, tell the concierge the same day, and let nobody carry one out — family included.* Get the name of who is authorised to take possession while you have them on the phone; that is the answer that stops the job later if you do not have it.
>
> None of this is a gate. Nothing here blocks a save, and you can correct any of it later on **Edit Client**, which asks the same questions. Do that the moment a client tells you something new — the crew reads the current version, not the one from the call.

> **The plain *Notes* box is still there, at the bottom.** The questions above cover the house; Notes is for everything else — family dynamics, urgency, who actually makes the decisions, which sibling will be difficult on site.

> **Home Prep for Sale runs a shorter version of this whole playbook** — no room scoring, no crew, and hours only if the house needs decluttering as well as trades. Skip to the Home Prep section near the end once intake is saved.

## Step 2 · Build the estimate — the Build estimate button on the client's timeline

Open the client on the Client Dashboard and press the brown **Build estimate** button in the band above the timeline. Once a walkthrough is booked it is the band's main button; before one is booked it sits beside *Set the walkthrough date*. The screen is always for that client — their name is in the bar across the top and on the Job card, and there is no job dropdown to pick from. This is desk or iPad work — the room tables are not built for a phone.

> **⚠** **⚠ Going back without saving.** The **←** button in the top bar (it carries the client's name) takes you back to their dashboard and keeps what you have scored while the page is open — press **Build estimate** again on the *same* client and you are where you left off. **Nothing is saved until you press Save Estimate.** Open a different client's estimate, or reload the page, and the unsaved rooms are gone. **Start over** (where *Clear* used to be) asks first and then does one of two things, and the question tells you which: on a client whose estimate has been **saved** it throws away what is on the screen and reopens the saved estimate; on a client with nothing saved it clears the lot and starts again from the intake answers. **Reset**, at the foot of the screen, also asks first and gives a **blank estimate for the same client**, starting from their intake answers; it never reopens a saved estimate and never changes one — going back to the saved one is **Start over**.

> **Every client's estimate starts clean (since 2026-09-29).** Open a different client and their estimate carries nothing of the last one: no discount, no move styling, no private note, no collections, no vehicles, no vendors, no target date. What it does carry comes off *that* client's record: the home value, Premium Estate, the inventory question and who walked the house. **If the home value or *Walkthrough by* is blank, that client has none on file** — fill it in (the home value on Edit Client), do not borrow one. Until this date the last client's settings rode into the next one, even through a Save; one family's private note turned up in another family's Walkthrough view. If you priced a practice client straight after another before then, open it and check those seven things are its own.

The screen reads top to bottom. **Top card:** *Job* on the left (the client, the **service type — a dropdown, not a label**, target start date, who prepared it), *Schedule & Crew* on the right (concierges and specialists, the concierge hands-on share and, on an estate, *Documentation scope*, the timeline badge, the *client needs it sooner?* planner and the **Rush order** toggle). **Adjustments** is its own card under it: difficult access, multiple heirs, Premium estate. **Middle:** the rooms two across, then collections beside vehicles, then the vendor cards, then moving materials. **Bottom:** the summary, then the pricing reference check beside the discount and the fixed price; the rush premium shows there too, but it is set at the top. Scope decisions at the top, price decisions at the bottom next to the price.

> **What changed on this screen on 2026-09-30 (P12).** An estate estimate opens on **fixed price**. The summary shows what the client is charged on the basis you chose: on a fixed price the fee, then the Services subtotal, the premium and the discount as their own rows. **A room left at its default no longer moves the price**, so tick every room: finishing the walkthrough cannot lower the quote any more. The **Pricing Reference Check** is this house priced at Normal and at Full; read it as information, never as a reason to re-score. A score is 1 to 5, and a blank box stops **Save Estimate**, which names the room.

> **⚠** **If the walkthrough tells you the job is not what intake said it was, change the service type here.** The commonest one: you were called out for Home Prep and the house actually needs editing first — closets, the garage, forty years of a dining room. Pick *Home Editing* in the service dropdown and say yes to the confirmation. The rooms appear, hours start pricing, and **the prep vendors you already entered stay exactly where they are and still earn their 30%**. Everything you have scored is kept.
>
> **⚠ But do this only when the house genuinely needs editing as a job.** If it is a prep job that just needs a few rooms cleared before the painters arrive, **stay on Home Prep and type the hours into *Pre-Prep Declutter*** instead (2026-09-14). Re-typing prices off the whole property's square footage, not the rooms you tick — on a 3,500 sqft house four rooms and twelve, at their starting scores, both come to **$10,650** as Home Editing (only a garage or outbuilding you tick adds to it) — so you would quote a full editing job for half a day of your own time. The test is simple: *is this a decluttering job that also needs trades, or a prep job that also needs a few hours?*
>
> The dropdown only offers the services on the same side of the living/deceased line, so you cannot turn a living client into an estate matter by accident. If you genuinely need to cross it, that is a different client: **+ Add New Client**. Edit Client refuses it too, since 2026-09-30.
>
> It is locked once the **signing packet has gone out** (until 2026-09-29 it stayed open until the signature). Before then it is open, including after the client has seen the estimate — press **✎ Edit estimate** on the timeline first, exactly as you would to give a discount, then re-approve. **If the client is already holding a document that names the old service, re-send it.** *Edit estimate* is not there while the estimate is with the manager (2026-09-29): wait for the PIN or a deny, and it comes back.
>
> **A Probate service on a trust or *Neither* is flagged under the total (2026-10-05).** The same amber box as at intake sits in the Estimate Summary while the estimate is unapproved. Pick *Estate Settlement* in the service dropdown and say yes, and it reprices: measured on a 3,200 sq ft house with five rooms, $24,062.50 as Probate down to $19,560. Leave it and nothing is refused; once a manager approves the estimate the box goes, and the price stays the Probate service's.

### On the walkthrough

- Use **📝** for notes and **🎤** for voice-to-text in each room row. Voice transcribes as you talk. Hit **Save Notes** and it files itself to the job's Walkthrough Notes folder.
- **Private Walkthrough Notes** — the box at the end of the room grid, with its own mic. Internal only: never on the client estimate, never in a shared folder. It's for what you wouldn't say in front of the family — access problems, hoarding, who's actually making the decision. Dictate it in the car on the way out. **Write it properly — somebody reads it back before Day 1.** It comes up on the client dashboard under **🔍 Walkthrough**, at the top of the page, above the rooms.
- **No photos at this stage.** Photography happens after the job is active, not on the estimate visit.
- Room sections open collapsed and run two across. Open each one as you walk that part of the house; use *Expand all / Collapse all / Hide empty* at the top.

> **⚠ To change a price after the client has accepted, open the client and press ✎ Edit estimate.** Until they say yes it sits under the estimate in the band; once they have, it is in the strip at the foot of the timeline. It is there until the signing packet goes out; after that it is a change order (step 10d). It un-approves the estimate and brings you back here with the form live. **If the new price comes out higher than the one they accepted, they are asked again** before the agreement goes out (step 5). *This screen had a job dropdown until 2026-09-23, and an accepted job left it on 2026-09-19; it opens for one client now, so there is no list to find a job in.*

### Scoring a room

Check the room to include it. It starts at 3/3 in most rooms — but foyers, half baths, secondary bathrooms, laundry, mudrooms and utility rooms start at 1/1, and primary baths at 2/2. Wherever it starts, **score what you're looking at**. Then set Volume and Complexity 1–5:

| Score | Volume | Complexity |
| --- | --- | --- |
| 1 | Nearly empty | Simple / standard items |
| 3 | Moderately filled | Mix of standard and care items |
| 5 | Completely full | High-value / fragile / specialty handling |

Tick **Special Items** if the room holds artwork, antiques, or anything needing specialty care. Hours and a timeline appear as you score.

**The hours on a room row are that room's share of the hands-on work** — sorting, documentation, packing and haul-out together — split between the concierge and the specialists. They do not add up to the fee lines: the concierge's off-site coordination and fixed time on site, vendor coordination, move day and the rounding up to the whole hour belong to no room. On a 3,500 sqft Estate Settlement with seventeen rooms scored, the rows carry about 121 of the 162 billed hours (24 of the concierge's 65, and 96 of the specialists' 97; re-measured 2026-10-02 — billed hours are rounded up to the whole hour again, and it was 160 on P17's one day of quarter hours). This changed on 2026-08-03: the rows used to show the *packing step only*, which is about a quarter of the work on an estate settlement, so every room understated itself by roughly **3.7×**. A 2-car garage read *2.4 PS* and was really booking over nine person-hours. If a room looks expensive now, that is the real number — it always was.

> **"How full is this house?" — press one chip instead of moving nineteen sliders.** Above the room grid: **Seasonal · Light · Normal · Full · Packed**. Tick your rooms as normal, then press the one that matches what you walked into. It sets the starting volume for every room in scope at once. *Normal* is what the app did before, so if you ignore the row nothing changes.
>
> It is a *shift*, not a blanket setting — a powder room stays lighter than a living room on every preset. Pressing the same chip twice does nothing new, and pressing a different one re-bases rather than piling on top.
>
> **Press it whenever you like, including before you have ticked a single room.** It is a standing setting: every room you tick from then on opens at that fullness. That is the easy way to run a walkthrough — say what the house is like when you walk in, then just tick rooms as you go through it. *Before 2026-09-10 it only applied to rooms already ticked, so anything you ticked afterwards quietly went back to the middle. If you priced a job that way, the rooms you did later are under-scored.*
>
> **Anything you type by hand is yours and the chip will not touch it.** Type a volume into a room ("the house is full but they already cleared the garage") and later chips re-base every other room around it and leave that one alone — the line under the row tells you how many it held back. To give a room back to the chip, **untick it and tick it again**.
>
> It does not touch complexity. Volume is *how much stuff*; complexity is *how careful you have to be*. Two different questions, and the premium-estate toggle already handles the high-end rate.

Room hours compare across services now. The same walk-in closet reads about 4 hours on a Home Editing job and about twice that on an Estate Settlement, because each row carries that room's share of every hands-on step the service prices. *Until 2026-08-03, when the rows showed packing only, it read the other way round.*

> **⚠** **Score the whole house, not a sample.** Volume and complexity are averaged across the rooms you score, each read against that room's own starting score, and applied to the *entire* square footage. Since 2026-09-30 a room left at its starting score counts as ordinary, so a foyer ticked at 1/1 no longer prices the house as empty: on a 10,000 sqft Estate Settlement the foyer alone now comes to about $43,850 on the hourly basis, the same as every interior room at its default (it used to be $24,100). **The trap now runs the other way: score one room light or heavy and the whole square footage follows it.** The living room alone at 1/1 prices that estate at about $25,600, and at 5/5 at about $75,750. A garage or outbuilding you do not tick adds nothing. A half-finished walkthrough doesn't give you half a price, it gives you a *wrong* price.

The app checks you on this. Under the room grid it compares the bedrooms and baths recorded at intake against the ones you have **accounted for** — scored, *or* deliberately marked out of scope:

| Badge | What to do |
| --- | --- |
| **Green** — room coverage matches intake | Nothing. Every bedroom and bath on record is accounted for. If some are out of scope it says so — "2 scored, 5 out of scope". |
| **Red** — walkthrough looks incomplete | It names the shortfall: "accounted for *2 of 5* bedrooms". Either finish the walkthrough, or mark the rooms we are not touching as out of scope. It won't stop you saving — it's on you to know why you're ignoring it. |

> **⚠ Not doing every room? Say so — don't just leave it blank.** The Scope box beside each room has *three* states, and tapping cycles through them: **blank → ✓ in scope → ✕ out of scope → blank**. Blank means "nobody has looked at this yet". ✕ means "we are deliberately not touching this — the client is handling it". They are different answers and the app treats them differently. On a job that is mostly Home Prep, where the guest wing, the kitchen and the laundry are the client's own, tap those rooms round to **✕**: the red badge clears, they cost nothing, and they still print on the client estimate and the job plan as explicitly *not ours* — which is exactly what you want the client to see. Leave them blank instead and the app cannot tell you from someone who quit halfway.

An ✕ room keeps its **note** (📝 and 🎤 both still work on it) — use the note to record *why* it is out of scope ("client is emptying the garage themselves"). That sentence is what stops the question coming back on job day.

If it says nothing at all, intake never captured the bed and bath counts. Go and fill them in — the check has nothing to compare against without them.

**Guest house or cottage?** They live in **Outbuildings & Guest Quarters** and you score each one as a *whole building* — pick the row matching its bedroom count and set volume and complexity once. There are no separate rows for its kitchen or its bath.

> **⚠** **The pool house and the casita are NOT in that section any more (changed 2026-09-18). Don't go hunting.** **Pool house →** one row, *Pool House — with living quarters*, and it is under **Exterior & Auxiliary**. There is no cabana option: if the pool house is a changing room with nothing in it, tick *Pool / Cabana Half Bath* instead and leave it. **Casita →** there is no casita row at all. A bedroom and a bath is the free-text *Additional Outbuilding Room* row — type *Casita* over the name. Its own living space or kitchenette makes it a *Guest House — 1 bedroom*. Go on what is inside the building, not what the owner calls it.

Outbuildings don't count toward the bedroom and bath numbers from intake — those are the main house. A four-bedroom house with a two-bed guest house is *4* at intake, not 6. Put 6 in and the coverage badge can never clear.

**Looking for a second half bath?** There are five to find: *Half Bath* in **Entry & Living**, in **Kitchen & Utility**, and in both the **First Floor** and **Second Floor Bedrooms & Bathrooms** sections, plus *Pool / Cabana Half Bath* in **Exterior & Auxiliary**. Tick the one that matches where the powder room actually is. Don't reach for *Additional Bathroom(s)* — that counts as a *full* bath, so it pushes the wrong number up and leaves the half-bath shortfall exactly where it was.

### The job settings that change the price

| Setting | What it does to the quote | When to use it |
| --- | --- | --- |
| **Premium Estate** | TC $185 / PS $125 instead of $150 / $100, **and nothing else since 2026-10-01**. It also added a flat 25 hrs of specialty coordination until then; now the auction house, the appraiser and the wine or art logistics are vendor lines you add, each booking its own hours, so the job pays for the channels it uses. | Fine art, antiques, wine — contents that need the extra care. |
| **Crew** — concierges and specialists | Two dropdowns side by side in the *Schedule & Crew* card. The app sizes both itself and keeps adjusting them up *and down* as you score. The moment you set one yourself it stops moving and reports what your choice costs instead. | Leave them alone unless you know something the app doesn't. You're saying *how many*, never *which* — naming people is Job Plan work, step 7. |
| **Client needs it sooner?** | Pick the *date* the client asked for and the planner tells you what crew reaches it — see below. | Whenever a date comes up on site. Read it *before* you tick rush. |
| **Difficult access** | +15% to the physical work, which lengthens the job and so lengthens concierge time automatically. | Stairs, no elevator, long carry. |
| **Multiple heirs** | +20% to off-site coordination — more calls and sign-offs, not more boxes. | Greyed out on contested probate; it's already in those rates. |
| **Documentation scope** (estate jobs only) | *Full*, *Capture only* or *None*, under the concierge hands-on share. New 2026-09-04. It is about a third of an estate quote, so set it before you read the total. *Full* is the inventory with estimated values. **Since 2026-09-30 no scope prices an appraiser: an appraiser is priced only when you add one under Vendors** (below), on any tier, and its fee is billed at cost like any vendor's. The coordination this scope carries was *Appraiser & inventory scheduling* and is *Inventory scheduling* now, so estate quotes came down a little (a 3,500 sqft Estate Settlement from $19,900 to $19,450). *Capture only* means we photograph, describe and locate everything and hand counsel the *list*; they value it and book any appraiser not on the estimate, and it prices half the documentation work and none of its scheduling. *None* means the attorney's office does the whole inventory and we sort, set aside and stage against their schedule. It is saved with the estimate and hidden on home editing, home transition and home-cleanout jobs. | You normally do not set this by hand any more: since 2026-09-21 it opens at whatever **What are we contracted to produce?** says on the intake form (Step 1) — *Contents list* opens it at *Capture only*, *None* at *None*, and either of the two valued tiers at *Full*. Change it here only if the walkthrough says something different from the call; the hint underneath will then name the tier intake recorded. On *Inventory + appraisals* with no appraiser on the estimate, an amber line under the hint reads *This tier promises the specialist appraisals: add each appraiser under Vendors, so its scheduling is priced on its own line* — a reminder, never a refusal. Never change it after approval without re-approving — the client estimate and the agreement both rewrite themselves from it. |
| **Rush order** | A flat **20% expedited-delivery premium** on the Havellin services total, shown to the client as its own named line. Never on vendor costs. Since 2026-09-30 never on the 30% prep fee either, and the toggle says so: *Flat 20% premium on the Havellin services, never on the home prep fee, shown as its own line*. **On a fixed price it is its own line under the fee**: 20% of whatever fee is quoted, a typed one included — and, on an estimate saved from 2026-09-30, on the price of any change order raised on the job (step 10d). | When the client needs it done by a date the natural schedule misses. It buys **priority scheduling**, not people: the estimate names a second concierge or a bigger crew only when you have actually staffed one (Concierges set to 2, or more specialists than the app recommends). Do not tell a client the premium adds crew. |
| **Quote as a fixed price** | **Estates open on it (2026-09-30):** a new Estate Settlement, Probate or Contested Probate estimate starts with it ticked. The fee is the price of the scope; the rush premium and the discount are lines on it. Replaces hourly with a firm flat fee — the hourly basis at two specialists plus a **20% contingency** — prefilled and yours to change — type the figure the client will see, or tap *Round to $X* under the field for the suggestion rounded down to a clean number ($25,275 → $25,000). Amber under it means the estimate moved *after* you set the fee: *Use* takes the new suggestion, *Keep* stands by yours. **On a job with prep vendors the 30% prep fee is not inside it (changed 2026-09-23)** — it sits on top as its own line, charged on what those vendors actually bill, exactly as on an hourly job. The suggestion leaves it out, so do not fold it back in when you round. | **Available on every job** as of 2026-09-20, probate included — it used to be withheld on the two probate services. The contingency is wider there: **25% probate, 35% contested**, against 20% everywhere else. The only job without the toggle is Home Prep, which is billed as a fee on vendor spend. |
| **Preferred Client Discount** | Percentage off Havellin labour only — on a rush job, labour plus the 20% premium charged on it — **max 15%**. On a fixed price it is its own line (2026-09-30): the fee less the materials package inside it, plus the premium on it. Same cap on **Offer discount** on the timeline, where **0 takes a discount off**. Offer discount and **✎ Edit estimate** both go once the signing packet has been sent — after that a price change is a change order. | Sparingly. The cap is the guardrail — the margin panel is no longer shown on this tab. |
| **Multiple heirs** — read this once | It adds a full 20%, but to *off-site coordination* — not to total concierge hours. Coordination is about half the concierge line on Home Editing and Home Transition and about a quarter on Home Cleanout and estate work (the toggle's own line says which, since 2026-09-30), so total concierge hours move about 5–10% and the quote about 2–7% (measured on a 3,500 sqft house: concierge hours +10.6% on Home Editing, +4.6% on an Estate Settlement). | Nothing. It is not a bug; people report it as one. |
| **Moving Materials Package** | Estate tier ($500 / $750 / $1,500) or Home Editing / Transition tier ($200 / $350 / $550). | Or None. |

> **⚠⚠ On an estate job the estimate will not save until two questions are answered on the client (new 2026-09-22).** *What are we contracted to produce?* and *How is this estate being administered?* — both on **Edit Client**. Until they are, **Save**, **Submit for approval** and the manager's PIN all refuse by name, and a line under the total reads *Not a quote yet*.
>
> **Score the house anyway.** It still prices while they are blank — deliberately, because "what do you want us to hand over?" is a conversation you have with the attorney *while looking at what each answer costs*. Tick the rooms, read the number, ring them, then answer it. **Nothing you have scored is lost when it refuses**, and the refusal says so on screen. **Answer it on Edit Client with the walkthrough still open and the estimate follows the answer at once** (since 2026-09-30): press **←** to the dashboard, answer, save — the notice reads *The estimate open on Build Estimate follows it: repriced from Full to Capture only*, or whatever your answer prices — then press **Build estimate** and **Save Estimate**. Until then the answer never reached the estimate on screen, which saved at the top of the scale.
>
> **Why it matters more than it looks:** a blank tier is not "no documentation", it is **the most expensive answer**. Measured on a 3,500 sqft probate estate: blank prices at **$22,750** and *None* at **$14,150**. That is $8,600 of inventory work nobody asked for — and an agreement drawn from it would promise the §733.604 inventory in writing, to the personal representative.

> **⚠** **A bigger crew makes an hourly estimate go DOWN, not up.** The concierge works alongside the crew, so more specialists finish sooner and the concierge is on site for fewer billable hours. That is correct pricing — but it means a crew size left over from a larger job will quietly discount this one. **Never carry a crew size between estimates.** The app flags any crew above its own recommendation; take the flag seriously. **The app's own steps lower it too, and that is decided (2026-09-30).** As you score, the app adds a specialist each time the job would otherwise run past its 10-day target, and each step takes a little off an hourly quote: on a 6,000 sqft Estate Settlement its step from two specialists to three takes the job from 12 working days to 9 and the quote from $32,150 to $29,900. Anthony: *"we don't want jobs stretching over weeks, so we bump up crew size to keep work days down."* An hourly quote bills the hours worked, and nothing floors it. **On a fixed price it does not dip (2026-09-30):** the suggested fee is priced at two specialists, whatever crew the job runs with. The badge under the crew says so on a fixed price since 2026-09-30: *“… so it shortens the calendar without lowering the fee”* (it used to say the bigger crew made the estimate lower).

**If you discount and expedite the same job**, the order is: services total, *plus* 20% on the whole of it, *then* the discount — taken off the labour and the premium charged on that labour — then vendors at cost. Anthony’s example: $100,000 of labour, rushed, is $120,000, and a 10% discount takes $12,000 off it. The premium is always charged on the full services total and the discount is its own line after it. *Corrected 2026-09-23: this paragraph described the order the app used before 2026-09-11 — discount first, then 20% on what was left.* **Since 2026-09-30 the 20% is never charged on the 30% prep fee, and on a fixed price the premium and the discount are lines on the fee.**

### When the client wants it faster — the timeline planner

Under the crew dropdowns there's a date field: *client needs it sooner?* **Pick the date they asked for** — the app works out the days. It shows you what the job delivers as staffed first — "As staffed: 8 working days at 2 specialists — done Aug 19" — then answers the date three ways:

| Answer | What to say on site |
| --- | --- |
| **Green** — "Sep 30 is comfortable" | "Yes, comfortably." **Do not tick rush.** Nothing is being expedited and you can't defend the premium. |
| **Amber** — "Reachable — Aug 14 with 4 specialists", with a button | Offer it. The button sets the crew and, when it really is faster than normal, ticks expedited delivery. The summary underneath shows the price before you commit. |
| **Red** — "Aug 11 is not reachable" | Six specialists is the cap. Counter with the date it gives you, or take scope out. Don't agree to the date. |

> **The amber button comes two ways.** "*Rush it — 4 specialists, done Aug 14, +20%*" means genuinely faster than normal, so the premium applies. "*Set crew to 3 — done Sep 1, no premium*" means the date is reachable only because the crew was set below what the job needs — that's staffing it properly, not expediting, and we don't charge for it.

> **Compressing is good for us, not just for them.** A bigger crew moves hours off the concierge line onto the specialist line, so our cost *falls* while the premium adds revenue — on a 6,000 sqft Home Cleanout, 8 days at 2 specialists against 4 days at 6 with rush went from 52% margin to 65%, and the client got it four days sooner. Say yes to the compression when you can deliver it.

> **⚠** **Don't charge the premium for a schedule you were going to run anyway.** That same job at its natural pace with rush ticked bills *more* than the genuinely expedited version — for nothing expedited. The app now says so out loud: tick rush without compressing and the badge reads *"Rush is on, but nothing is compressed … you are adding $X for no change in delivery."* Untick it, or add the crew.

### Vendors, collections and vehicles

Vendors are picked from **six Category Group cards**, sitting **below Notable Collections and Vehicles & Watercraft** — Asset Liquidation & Valuation, Disposal & Waste, Moving & Logistics, Professional Services, Property Preparation, and End-of-Job Logistics. Each is a category dropdown, an *Est cost* box and a **+**. The lists come straight from the Vendor Directory, so if a category is missing it's missing from the sheet — add it there (see the Operations Manual, §13a) and it appears here. **If the cards have nothing to pick but the End-of-Job Logistics list, which is the app's own, the directory has not loaded**, and since 2026-09-30 an amber line above the cards says so: *Vendor Directory not loaded — add the Vendor Directory URL in ⚙ Settings.* Before that it never showed on a labour job, so the cards just sat empty.

> **A firm that does two things is one row in the directory, not two.** Its trades go in the Category field separated by a semicolon — *Jewelry & Watch Appraiser; Jewelry & Watch Buyer* — and it then shows up under both headings in the Vendors tab while staying one firm with one rating, one status and one contact history. To add a trade to a firm that is already in there, press **Edit** on its card and type the extra trade; **do not add a second row**, and the app will refuse one under a name it already has. The suggestions keep working after the semicolon, and past the first trade they widen beyond the group you picked. One thing it cannot do: a firm still has a single *Category group*, so if its trades genuinely straddle two groups, file it under the one you engage it for most and expect the other trade on that same estimate card.

> **The number you want is the owner's, and you get it standing in the driveway.** A vendor record keeps the firm and the people apart: *Office phone* and *Office email* are the switchboard and the general inbox (*southflorida@navismoving.com*), and each of the **two contact slots** holds a person with their own title, mobile and address (*andy@navismoving.com*). When somebody hands you a card, press **Quick edit** on their vendor and put it in there — the quick card carries both contacts in full, which is the whole reason it exists. Fill in whatever you have: a mobile with no name still counts, and the card will say *name not recorded* so you know to go back for it. **Put a title on them** — *Owner* and *Dispatch* are very different calls at 7am on move day.

> **The Call button says who it rings.** Each party gets its own row on the card: *☎ Office* for the firm, then *☎ Andy* and *✉ Text Andy* for anyone with a mobile on file. **Text only ever appears on a mobile**, because a switchboard cannot receive one — so if a vendor has no Text button, nobody has given you their cell yet.

> **You pick a category, not a vendor.** Finding an appraiser who's actually free that week is office work at kickoff — the estimate says what the job *needs*, the Job Plan says who's doing it. Anything you add on the *End-of-Job Logistics* card carries through to the Job Plan as a line to source, so you're asked once rather than twice. **Anything you do not add is not on the Job Plan at all** (since 2026-09-23) — if the job turns out to need a dumpster or a shredder after all, it is one pick from *+ Add an end-of-job vendor* there.

- **Third-party vendors** — add each with an estimated cost. Havellin charges **no fee** on them; they are billed to the client directly at cost, and your coordination time is billed hourly instead. **Every line you add books that time.** The card footer shows the running total per group. So adding a vendor does raise the quote now, and it should: sourcing, quoting, scheduling and chasing that vendor is real time you spend.
  > **Where those hours come from — count the touches, halve the count.** A *touch* is one thing you actually have to do with that vendor: a call to place, a quote to chase, access to arrange, work to go and look at, a settlement to reconcile. A mover is 6 of them (survey · quote · insurance certificate · pack day · load day · delivery) so it books **3 hours**. An online auction house is 12 → **6 hrs**, the most of anything, because there we list and dispose of the things ourselves; an estate sale company is 3 → **1.5** (call · contract · settlement follow-up), because the company runs its own sale. Anthony set those two on 2026-10-01 (*"a call and some follow up"*; *"handling an online auction ourselves is a real time sink"*); they were 4 → 2 and 8 → 4. An appraiser is 4 → 2 hrs, and since 2026-09-30 that line is the only place an appraiser's scheduling is priced, Premium Estate or not. An auction house is 6 → 3 hrs · a hauler or a dumpster 2 → 1 · shredding 1 → 0.5. Prep trades such as painting carry the 30% fee instead and book no hours (next bullet). **If you think a count is wrong, say so** — that is the whole reason it is written as a count rather than an hours figure somebody once picked.
- **Home Prep as an add-on** — add the trades through the *Property Preparation* card, same as any other vendor. **That's the whole thing** — there's no box to tick and no second Home Prep section; if a line is on the card it's in the estimate. Fill in the **scope note** under each line while you're standing there. **Changed 2026-09-10:** prep vendors carry the **30% Home Sale Preparation Fee wherever they appear** — bundled onto a labour job exactly as on a standalone prep engagement — and they book **no coordination hours** at all any more. The vendors bill the client at cost; the 30% is ours. Before this they were the other way round (at cost with no fee, plus 0.5–2 of your hours per trade), which on a $45,000 package was about $1,200 of hours instead of $13,500 of fee. **Once the job runs, never log the time you spend on those trades** — the 30% is what pays for it (step 10b).
- **Notable Collections** — name, estimated value, quantity, a proposed disposition (appraise, auction house, consignment, estate sale, licensed FFL, gold & silver buyer, and so on), what you expect that partner to charge, and a voice note. *You don't name the auction house here* — you tag a specific partner on the Job Plan when you're calling round with a list, and the disposition you pick is what scopes that picker to the right vendors. **The disposition you pick adds hours**, and the rule is simple: *if the specialist comes to the property, you're standing there; if the items go out to them, your time is phone and paperwork.* An appraisal, a dealer visit or an FFL handoff books real on-site time and lengthens the job. eBay, Replacements.com and consignment book coordination only. A ten-collection estate genuinely is a longer job than a two-collection one.
- **Vehicles & watercraft** — add each by description then fill the panel: type, year, VIN/HIN, mileage, condition, and the two flags — *collector / classic* and *title located*. Ordinary vehicles you value in-house from KBB/NADA as of the date of death; collectors and boats route to an appraiser. Vehicles don't change the labour estimate.

### Reading the timeline badge before you save

| Badge | What to do |
| --- | --- |
| **Green** | Nothing. Either it's inside the 10-day target, or it's longer because the house is big and the crew is already at 6 — no lever left. |
| **Amber** | Over target *with crew headroom*. Adding a specialist would pull it back. This is the one badge you can act on. |
| **Red** | The projection misses the client's hard date. Renegotiate scope or the deadline *before* you send the estimate. |

> **If the app recommends a second concierge**, it's telling you one person can't cover the schedule — more than 10 concierge hours a day. It changes neither the fee nor the duration; it stops somebody working 12-hour days. Contested probate and heavy documentation estates trip this, which is right.

Hit **Save Estimate**. It saves, syncs, and **puts you on the Client Dashboard, on the client you just priced** — with the summary of what you saved (rooms, concierge hours, specialist hours, total) carried across onto that screen. You can reopen it with **✎ Edit estimate** in the band until you submit it for approval; while a manager has it, it is locked, and it opens again after a deny, or after the PIN through ✎ Edit estimate, which un-approves it.

> **⚠** **⚠ If you pressed Save before 2026-09-13 and thought you had lost the form, you had not.** The button was trying to open the Client Estimate tab, which was retired from the nav two days earlier — so it went nowhere, blanked the form on the screen you were still looking at, and replaced your save summary with *"Estimate cleared."* a few seconds later. **Every one of those estimates saved correctly.** Open the client on the Client Dashboard and it is there.

> **⚠** **⚠ If Save says a field is required, go and set it — your walkthrough stays where it is.** Two can stop you: *"Property value is required"* on any job, and *"New home sq ft is required"* on a Home Transition. Both tell you to open the client on the **Client Dashboard** and press **✎ Edit Client**. Do exactly that: press **← <client>** at the top of the screen, press Edit Client, fill it in, save, then press **Build estimate** on the timeline again — **every room you scored is still ticked and still scored**, and Save works. Do not press **Start over** — that is the one thing that would clear the form. Until 2026-09-22 both messages said to fix it in *Client Intake*, which only creates new clients, so there was no way to finish the estimate at all; if you hit that and abandoned a walkthrough, it will work now.

## Step 3 · Get it approved — Client Dashboard

> **⚠** **The buttons that used to sit across the top of the Client Dashboard are gone (changed 2026-09-11).** There were seven: *Estimate · Submit for Approval · Client Accepted — Mark Won · Change Order · Edit Client · Drive · Activate Job*. **Five of them were already further down the same screen.** Nothing was removed — every one of them is where it belongs now: the four job steps are rows on the **timeline**, *Change Order* has its **+ New** in the Change Orders card, and the ones that are not steps sit on the **Job Timeline & Payments** heading: **✎ Edit Client**, **🔍 Walkthrough** and **📁 Drive**. **If you are hunting for a button, look in the brown band above the timeline.** That is the whole design — one screen, one lit step, and its buttons in the band.


> **The document you need is in the brown band at the top, not down the page (new 2026-09-13).** The band reads **NEXT** and the step, then names the **document this stage is about** — *Client Estimate* — and under that name sits **one row of buttons**: the document's **👁 View**, **🖨 Print** and **✎ Edit** first, then the one brown button that is the thing to do, then anything else the step offers. It advances with the job: the estimate, then the signing packet, then each invoice in turn. Consult the document, then act — that is the order it is laid out in. **The strip at the very foot of the timeline is the job's archive**, for going back to a document from an earlier stage; on a job whose estimate is still a draft it is empty, and that is correct. **On a finished job the band reads COMPLETE and its big buttons are the final invoice** — the last thing sent — so the final is at the top and the strip holds the four before it. The big buttons are always the most current stage.

> **They are all on one line now (changed 2026-09-20).** The document's buttons and the step's used to be two separate rows, so a band offering four of them rendered **two over two** on a row that fits eight. Anthony: *"let's make the four … move horizontally rather than stacked two over two to save a little vertical scrolling."* Nothing was wrapping — they were two containers, so no window width would ever have fixed it. **Nothing moved out of the band and nothing was removed**; the order is the same and the band is shorter. On a phone the row still wraps onto a second line, which is what it should do.

> **⚠** **⚠ A draft estimate has View and Edit but NO Print, on purpose.** The tray titles it *Client Estimate — DRAFT*. Read it on screen all you like; it must not reach paper or a client until a manager has approved it. **And until 2026-09-13 the foot of the timeline offered you "View deposit invoice" on a job with no approved estimate at all** — which opened a complete, printable bill priced off the draft. If anyone has a printed invoice from a job that was never approved, bin it.

Open the client; the band reads *Get the estimate approved*. **👁 View estimate** reads the client-facing document — do that first, for accuracy. Then **Submit for approval** → a manager presses **Manager approval** in the band and types their PIN (or **Deny**, with a reason) → the *Estimate approved* step goes green with their name and the date, and the tray drops *DRAFT* and gains **🖨 Print estimate**.

> **⚠ Submitting opens an email asking for the PIN, and it now comes from your Havellin Gmail (fixed 2026-09-22).** Before this it was handed to **whatever your computer has set as its default mail program** — and on the first test run that was iCloud Mail, so the approval request for a priced estimate went out **from a personal iCloud address**. It now drafts in the Havellin mailbox you are signed in to, the same way the client estimate does. Read it and press send. **The manager's answer comes back the same way (since 2026-09-30):** approving or denying with the PIN drafts the email to you in the Havellin mailbox on the manager's device. It used to open their computer's own mail program.
>
> **If a badge says "CHECK THE FROM ADDRESS before you send", stop and look at the From line.** That is the app telling you it could not reach Gmail and fell back to your computer's mail program — usually because Gmail is not set up on that device, or you closed the Google sign-in. The email still works; it may just be leaving from the wrong account.

**The document now opens with a full job plan (new 2026-08-03).** Ahead of the money there is *How We Work*, *Spaces In Scope* grouped the way the Build Estimate grid groups them, and *How The Work Runs* — every stage of the job with what you will do, what you need from the client, and what finishes it. **Read it before you submit.** It is written out of the estimate, so a room filed in the wrong section or a vendor you forgot shows up here in plain English, where a number would not have caught your eye.

**Every vendor you entered is named in the stage it actually happens in** — appraisers in the sorting stage (they value while everything is still in place), haulers and auction houses in disposition, cleaners in close-out. So read the stages as a check on your vendor list: *if a vendor you entered is not named anywhere in the sequence, you have filed it under the wrong category.* What the client receives at the end — photographs, the inventory, vendor invoices, donation receipts, the final invoice against logged hours — is listed inside the Close-Out stage.

> **⚠** **Which version of this document you get is decided by the SERVICE TYPE, and nothing else.** *Estate Settlement*, *Probate* and *Contested Probate* are written to the representative of someone who has died. *Home Editing*, *Home Transition*, *Home Cleanout* and *Home Prep* are written to a living owner. There is no field to override it — which is the point: the two documents and the price always agree about who the client is.
>
> **If this document has the wrong voice, the job is on the wrong side of the living/deceased line**, and that cannot be changed on this client: create the right one with **+ Add New Client**. Do not rewrite the wording — it is regenerated every time anyone renders the document, and the price is wrong too.

> **⚠** **On an estate or probate job the document reads completely differently, and it has to.** Those stages are written for the **representative** — the attorney, executor or trustee — not for a family standing in the house. Stage 2 is *Sorting, Documentation & Inventory*: we catalogue and **nothing is sold, donated or removed at that stage**, and the document tells them plainly they do not need to be on site. Stage 3 is *Distribution & Disposition* and will not start until the representative has read the inventory and authorised disposal **in writing**. On a home editing or home transition job the old decision-paced language stays, because there it is true.
>
> Two things in that copy are commitments *you* have to keep on the job: **releases against signed receipts**, and **nothing goes out on a verbal request**. If an heir asks you on site for something, the answer is that it goes through the representative in writing — be pleasant about it, and do not make an exception. **Since 2026-10-03 (P19) the desk keeps the first one with you**: on an estate it prints each person's receipt and files the signed copy back (step 10e).

> **⚠** **Never ask a client for the will.** Ask for a certified copy of the **Letters** (on a trust-only matter, since 2026-10-03, the successor trustee's **Certification of Trust** instead, and never the trust instrument itself; on a matter with no court and no trust, written confirmation of who directs the work; the client estimate asks for the same), and for the *list* of items designated to a named person — the representative or their attorney produces that list. Since 2026-10-03 (P19) that list goes on the desk's **Beneficiaries & specific bequests** card, in their words (step 10e). If you find a will, codicil, deed or title in the house, it is **sequestered and handed over against a signed receipt**: an original will to the estate attorney the same day, recorded on the Job Plan's *Found on site* card (step 10g, since 2026-10-03), everything else to the representative and counsel (step 10a). We do not read it and we do not interpret it. The client document says so in as many words, and you should too.

> **⚠** **Check the sorting stage against what you priced (new 2026-09-04).** If the estimate's *Documentation scope* is *Capture only* the stage reads *Sorting, Photography & Listing* and says once that valuation is counsel's; at *None* it reads *Sorting & Set-Aside* and says once that counsel is inventorying. If it still reads *Sorting, Documentation & Inventory* and promises an itemised inventory with values, you are promising work that is not in the price — go back to Build Estimate, set the scope, and re-submit. The rules about authority do not move with it: nothing is sold or removed at that stage, nothing leaves without written authority.

Two things about it worth knowing before a client asks. **There are no dates or durations in the stages**, on purpose — the pace is set by how quickly they make decisions, and a date there is a promise about their calendar rather than ours.
