The only dates are in the header — the target start, a projected completion and the anticipated duration in working days — and the document does not explain the absence in the stages either, so if a client asks, answer from those and say plainly that it depends on how fast they decide. And the stages come from the service type: a probate job shows documentation and court-filing stages, a home cleanout shows neither, a move shows Move Day. You cannot add or remove one by hand, and you should not try to — they are generated from the same thing that priced the job.

**Two things to check on the document itself (changed 2026-08-03).** Every vehicle you logged now appears in a *Vehicles & Watercraft* table — each one reads *Flagged for specialist appraisal* if you ticked Collector / classic, otherwise *Flagged for disposition*, and any vehicle without *Title located* ticked prints *Title to be located* in front of the client. That is deliberate — no title means no transfer, and they should hear it now rather than at closeout — but tick the box if you did find the title. The document never names a buyer or an appraiser for a vehicle; that gets decided on the job and recorded in the inventory.

On a **fixed-price** quote the document reads differently on purpose: one *Fixed Project Fee* line replacing the hourly rows, and **no hour counts anywhere** — including on the vendor and home-prep footnotes, which on an hourly quote tell the client how many concierge hours the vendor coordination took. A fixed-price document that quotes hours invites exactly the argument the fixed price exists to avoid. The fee printed is the one you typed — $25,000 if that is what you set — never the suggestion behind it.

> **The 30% prep fee is a row in the Havellin Services table now, and the rows add up to the total (moved 2026-09-23).** Anthony, off a Home Transition estimate carrying a painter, a pressure washer and a pool clean: *“the $250 in GC fees don’t appear to be added to the Havellin Services total. where’s the error?”* The fee was in the total — $225 on $750 of prep vendors — but it was printed underneath, under the prep vendors, so the rows above the total did not reach it. It is the last row of the services table now, *Home Prep for Sale — Home Sale Preparation Fee*, and the Home Prep section lists the vendors alone and points up at it. **On a fixed-price quote it is the line under the Fixed Project Fee**: the flat fee and the prep fee, then the expedited-delivery premium and the preferred-client discount as their own lines when there are any (2026-09-30), adding to the total. **Add the rows up before you submit.** If a client is holding an estimate with prep vendors on it from before today, re-send it.

> **⚠** **We are insured and bonded. We are NOT licensed.** Every client document said *"Licensed, Insured & Bonded"* until 2026-08-03. It is corrected everywhere in the app, but if you see the word *licensed* describing Havellin on anything that reaches a client — a document, an email, a proposal you have written yourself — take it out. *Licensed FFL* on a firearms disposition is a different thing and is correct: that is the vendor's licence, not ours.

> **The vendor numbers are flagged as estimates on the document itself (new 2026-09-10).** Both vendor sections now open with a line before the table saying these are good-faith figures from the walkthrough, that each vendor sets its own final price, and that because every vendor invoices the client *directly* they will see the real bill. **Say the same thing out loud when you walk them through it.** The vendor lines are the part of your estimate most likely to move, and a client who was told that up front reads a higher painter's invoice as a painter pricing a paint job — not as Havellin missing its own number.

**If a client asks whether we value things from photographs** — the sorting stage says we do, and why. We photograph a piece and send it to the specialist first so an on-site appraisal is only booked where the item warrants one, which keeps them from paying a call-out fee on something that does not. What we do *not* do is put a value on anything of consequence from a photograph: that is done in person, on site, while the contents are still in place. *Until 2026-09-10 the document claimed the opposite outright — that nothing is ever assessed from a photograph — so if you sent an estimate before then, that is the sentence they read.*

The tagline on every client document is **"Havellin handles the work no family should face alone."**

> **⚠** Once approved, the estimate is **locked**. The one way back in is **✎ Edit estimate** on the timeline: it un-approves the estimate and withdraws the agreement's approval, so it has to be approved again. It is there until the signing packet has gone out; after that a change of scope is a **Change Order** (step 10d). It also takes away **📁 Filed estimate**, the link to the old copy in Drive, until the edited estimate is approved, sent and filed again (since 2026-09-30; the link went on opening the pre-edit copy as if it were current). While the estimate is approved, Edit Client holds the inputs that price the hours: service type, square footage, years in home, Premium Estate and the new home's square footage. Touch one and its alert names the same route, **✎ Edit estimate**, then change it on Edit Client (since 2026-09-30; it used to say *raise a change order* before the packet had gone out, when that was not the route).

**You do not have to save the estimate to Drive — approving it does that.** When a manager approves it with the PIN, the client estimate files itself into the job's Drive *Estimates* folder, and **📁 Filed estimate** opens it — in the band, or in the strip at the foot of the timeline once the job has moved on. If you edit an approved estimate, re-approving re-files the corrected version over the old one, and sending it files it again. **📁 File estimate to Drive** appears only on an estimate that went out without a filed copy — press it then, and it goes away once it works.

> **The PDF is named for the client now (new 2026-09-08).** Print / Save PDF gives you *Havellin Service Estimate - 1234 Ocean Blvd - Sep 8 2026.pdf* instead of *Ellsworth-HVL-0007.pdf*. The email attachment uses the same name.

> **⚠** **If you estimated a client before 2026-09-08, look in their Drive *Estimate* folder.** Two different documents used to be saved under one filename — the client estimate, and our internal worksheet with the room scores, the hours and your walkthrough notes on it — and whichever finished uploading last is the one that stayed. **If the file in there is a table of rooms and hours, it is the internal one.** To put the client version back, press **📁 File estimate to Drive** where the band offers it; where it does not, print the estimate to PDF (**🖨 Print estimate**) and put it in the folder by hand. *The Save to Drive button this used to name went with the Client Estimate tab.* They are two separate files from now on, and the internal one says *Internal worksheet — not a client document* across the top.

No client signature is wanted on the estimate. It's informational.

## Step 4 · Send it — Client Dashboard

**✉ Send estimate** (in the band when it reads *Send the estimate to the client*) builds a proper formatted email — Havellin masthead, the property, the cost summary, the stages — with the estimate PDF attached, and leaves it as a **draft in your own Gmail**. It opens the draft for you. Read it, add a line of your own, and send it yourself.

Google will ask which account the first time you use it on a device, and stay quiet after that. **The draft is yours**: it sends from your address, lands in your Sent folder, and the client's reply comes back to you. The app can create the draft and cannot send it — that is deliberate, and it is why you always get to read it first.

Then come back and hit **✓ I've sent it** so the app records the delivery. The row will not go green until you do, and **Mark Won is waiting on it**.

> **⚠** **If the price goes up after you sent it, this row lights again (new 2026-09-29).** The app remembers the price on the estimate you sent. When a manager approves it at a **higher** figure — an edit re-approved — the band reads **✉ Send revised estimate**. Send it and press *I've sent it* before anything else: the client should never be asked to accept a figure they have not seen. A discount does not do this; the client holds a higher figure, not a lower one.

> **⚠** An unapproved estimate **cannot be emailed**. If the button refuses it prints the reason on the dashboard, right where you pressed it — go back to step 3.

> **⚠** **Change the price after the draft is made, and that draft is dead (2026-09-29).** A discount or *Edit estimate* does not reach into Gmail, so the draft still has the old price in it. The app stops offering **✓ I’ve sent it** for it and names it on the row, with the day and the Gmail account: **delete that draft, do not send it**, and send a fresh one once the manager re-approves. The app never deletes it for you. If the old one already reached the client, tell them a revised one is coming.

> **If the Google sign-in is cancelled or fails, ✉ Send estimate opens a plain email in your normal mail app instead, and says so.** That one carries **no attachment** — print the estimate to PDF (**🖨 Print estimate**) and attach it yourself. **Since 2026-09-30 the app records it:** the row reads *"Opened as a plain email — send it from your mail app, then confirm"*, the estimate is filed to Drive, and the band offers **✓ I've sent it**. Send it from your mail app with the PDF attached, then press it. (Until then the app recorded nothing for a plain email, the band stayed on *Send the estimate to the client*, and there was no tap to press.) Where you can, still sort out the sign-in — a Gmail draft carries the PDF for you. *The ✉ Plain email button that used to sit beside Send went with the Client Estimate tab.*

Then wait. Do not send an agreement, do not book anybody.

## Step 5 · The client says yes — Client Dashboard

Open the client; when the band reads *Get the client's decision*, press **✓ Client accepted — mark won**. Acceptance is informal — nothing is signed until the agreement — so the app records *how you know*:

- **Method** — email reply · phone call · text · in person
- **Date** they accepted
- **What they said** — paste the email, or write down what was said on the call

> A phone call or in-person acceptance **with no note** gets challenged before it's accepted. There's no email to fall back on, so your note is the only record of what the client actually agreed to. Write it while you remember it.

*Approved* and *Won* are different things and the difference is the whole point: approved means a manager signed off on our figure; won means the client said yes. Marking Won is what unlocks staffing.

> **⚠** **If the price goes up after they said yes, they are asked again (new 2026-09-29).** The app remembers the price the client accepted. When a manager approves the estimate at a **higher** figure, the job stays **Won** — staffing and the Job Plan carry on — but it reads *Won · Awaiting Re-acceptance*, and the agreement, the deposit invoice and the payment link wait. Do it in this order:
>
> 1. Send them the revised estimate (step 4 — the band says *Send revised estimate*), and press *I've sent it*.
> 2. Get their yes to the **new** figure, the same way as the first time.
> 3. Press **✓ Client accepted the revised price**. The pop-up shows the figure they accepted and the new one; record how you know, and **Record Acceptance** keeps their earlier yes on file beside it.
>
> They can say no to a higher price — **Mark lost** is right beside it. A **discount** never asks them again, and nothing is asked once the signing packet has gone out. A client who walked away and came back gets the ordinary *Client accepted — mark won*: their old yes was withdrawn when they left. **A job from before 2026-09-29 has no figures on record and is asked nothing** — if you raise one of those, send the revised estimate anyway and get their yes before the agreement goes.

## Step 6 · Agreement — Client Dashboard

> **⚠** **Know what the agreement says if the client walks away (fixed price new 2026-09-20; hourly 2026-09-30).** You will be asked. **On a fixed price** the fee is earned in stages: **the deposit is earned the moment they sign and does not come back**, 75% is earned once the job reaches its midpoint, and the whole fee is earned at completion. Whatever stage has been reached is owed, plus any vendor booking we cannot cancel without a charge. **Hours do not enter into it** — a flat fee does not move with the time we spend, on the way out any more than on the way in.
>
> The one exception is if *we* are in material breach and do not fix it within seven days of being told so in writing. Then the unearned part comes back. “The client changed their mind” and “the client is unhappy” are **not** that, and the agreement is written so they cannot be dressed up as it.
>
> **On an hourly job the deposit does not come back either (since 2026-09-30).** They owe the hours worked and the materials used up to the day they stop; the deposit counts toward that, and where it is more than the work done, the rest of it is still earned. Until then the agreement handed back the unused part of the deposit (the living-client form) or kept it only “after project start” (the estate form). The one exception is the same: our own breach, unfixed seven days after they told us in writing — then anything we hold above the hours and materials comes back. **Money they paid beyond the deposit and the work done** — a midpoint payment larger than the work — goes back to them (since 2026-10-01; until then it was Anthony's call): when you close the job out the app shows the *Refund due*, and the refund is recorded on the card once Anthony has sent it (Step 13).
>
> **If the job has prep vendors, the agreement states two figures (2026-09-23):** the fixed price, and the home sale preparation fee at 30% of what those vendors actually invoice, charged *in addition* to it. On the way out the prep fee is owed on the prep work already done or booked, not in the fixed fee’s stages — and the deposit still does not come back.
>
> **Do not improvise on this in a driveway.** If a client starts asking what happens if they stop the job, read them the clause or call Anthony. Do not offer a refund of the deposit; it is not yours to offer.

> **⚠** **The packet cannot go out until the job is marked Won (enforced 2026-09-08).** Until the client's acceptance is recorded (step 5) you can read it and nothing more: print, send and file all wait, and there is no override.
>
> **The reason is where the agreement goes.** The first print or send approves the agreement and files the signing packet to the client's Drive *Agreement* folder, so no packet lands in the folder of somebody who never said yes. Reading it costs nothing. **Since 2026-09-29 that includes the signing packet itself:** once the estimate is approved, **👁 View packet** — in the strip at the foot of the timeline — opens it titled *Signing Packet — PREVIEW*, with no Print button, so you can walk the client through the contract they are deciding on. Print and Send come back when you mark them Won.

> **⚠** **If you edit the estimate, this agreement's approval is withdrawn.** The agreement attaches the estimate as Exhibit A, so it cannot stay approved against a version you are changing. The *Signing packet sent* step says so. Once the manager re-approves the estimate, the next print or send approves the agreement again, and the packet is filed over the old one — you do not need to delete anything. **A discount does the same (since 2026-09-29)**: offering, changing or removing one withdraws the approval, and the *Signing packet sent* step says a discount changed the price. Once the manager re-approves, it says only *send a fresh packet* (2026-09-30). **Once the packet has gone out, *Offer discount* and *Edit estimate* both disappear** — the client is being asked to sign that price, so a change after that is a change order (step 10d). **If the edit put the price up after they said yes**, the status reads *Won · Awaiting Re-acceptance* and the packet waits for their yes to the new figure (step 5); you can still view it, marked PREVIEW.

> **⚠** **You no longer need a manager to approve the agreement (changed 2026-09-10).** There used to be a second PIN here. It is gone, and it is not coming back — Anthony: *"once an estimate is approved by a manager and accepted by a client, a TC should be able to send an agreement without further manager approval … there is literally no way to amend an agreement that comes out of the system."* That is the reason it was safe to drop: **you cannot change a word of the agreement.** Its commercial terms *are* the estimate a manager already approved, attached as Exhibit A, and everything else is generated. There was nothing for a second manager to read that the first had not. **The two conditions have not changed** — the estimate must be approved and the client must be marked Won. You still cannot get past those. What changed is that meeting them approves the agreement *as you send it*, instead of being a separate thing to chase somebody for.

1. Open the client. When the band reads *Send the signing packet*, read it first (**👁 View packet**).
2. Press **✉ Send for signature — DocuSign**. That one press approves the agreement, files the signing packet to the client's Drive *Agreement* folder and sends the envelope: the client signs first — on an estate with every co-representative recorded beside them, each on their own block (since 2026-10-05) — Anthony countersigns, and agreements@ is copied. The step records itself as sent — there is no *I've sent it*. The notice names everyone it went to: *Sent to Ruth Adler and Daniel Adler for signature through DocuSign*.
3. For a client who will not sign on a screen, press **📄 Send as PDF to sign by hand** instead. It makes a Gmail draft with the packet attached, CC'd to agreements@. Read it, add a line, send it, then tap **✓ I've sent it**. On an estate with co-representatives the email already says *"Each co-representative named on the signature page signs it too."* (since 2026-10-05).
4. Handing it over yourself? Print it (**🖨 Print packet**), give it to them, then press **🤝 Handed over in person** on the same step (since 2026-10-01). It asks first, then records the packet as sent, in person, today; the step reads *Handed over in person* and the band moves on to getting it signed. Nothing is emailed and there is no *I've sent it*.

> **⚠** **A co-representative with no email stops the DocuSign send (since 2026-10-05).** DocuSign emails each co-representative their own part of the envelope, so it cannot go without their address. The press is refused before anything is sent, naming everyone missing at once: *"Daniel Adler has no email recorded. Add it on Edit Client, or send the PDF to sign by hand."* Add the email on **✎ Edit Client** and press again, or send the PDF to sign by hand, in which case they sign the printed page and you file it once the client has signed (Step 8).

> **⚠** **Two signers at one email address go out, and the notice says so (since 2026-10-05).** If a co-representative is recorded with the client's email, or another's (a couple sharing an inbox, or a slip), the envelope still goes and the notice turns amber: *"Ruth Adler and Daniel Adler are on the envelope at one email address (ruth@adler.example): make sure each of them signs for themselves."* If it was a slip, correct the email on **✎ Edit Client** and call them both; if they share an inbox, make sure each signs as themselves. Anthony is testing how DocuSign handles it, and the app may refuse it later.

> **Handing it over in person?** Print it (**🖨 Print packet**) and press **🤝 Handed over in person**. Printing files the packet but does not record it as sent, so without that press the *Agreement signed* step, and its recorder, never light. (Until 2026-10-01 the only way to record it was to send the PDF route's email as well and tap *I've sent it*.)

> **⚠** **PDF route only. Sent the packet from Gmail and forgot the tap? Do it before anybody touches the price (2026-09-29).** Until you tap **✓ I’ve sent it**, the app thinks the packet is still a draft, so *Offer discount* is still there. Press it and the pop-up names the draft and asks: *“Already sent it? Cancel and tap ‘I’ve sent it’ instead”* — because once the packet is in the client’s hands, a price change is a change order (step 10d). **If it really was never sent**, apply the discount, delete the old draft in Gmail (the row names it), and send a fresh packet once the manager re-approves. The fresh send tells you which of the two drafts to delete.

> **⚠** **There are two agreement forms and you do not choose between them.** The app picks on the **service type**, the same way the estimate does (step 3): Estate Settlement and both probates get the **estate form**, written to a representative signing in a fiduciary capacity; Home Editing, Home Transition, Home Cleanout and Home Prep get the standard form, written to an owner contracting for their own property. **Read the first paragraph before you send it.** If it addresses the wrong kind of client, the job is on the wrong side of the living/deceased line: that is a new client (**+ Add New Client**), not a correction. Never edit the agreement text by hand.

> **On a trust or *Neither* the agreement never says Probate (new 2026-10-05).** Its title and the sentence under it name the service *Estate Settlement* on a Probate service and *Contested Estate Settlement* on a Contested Probate one, and so do Exhibit A, the invoices and the emails; the price is still the service's own. If a trust family's agreement says *Probate Estate Settlement*, the matter type is wrong or unanswered: fix it on **✎ Edit Client** and regenerate. The trust's date reads in full, *dated March 3, 2015*.

> **⚠** **The estate agreement rewrites its inventory clauses from the estimate's Documentation scope (new 2026-09-04).** Scope of Services, the §5.2 probate-compliance list, the appraisal row in §5.3 and the midpoint-payment trigger all follow it. At *None* the agreement says the inventory and valuation are counsel's; at *Capture only* it sells the photographed list without valuation. **Its appraisal wording follows who arranges the appraisals (2026-09-30):** on *Inventory + appraisals* at full scope Havellin arranges every one; on any other tier an appraiser on the estimate is named — *"Havellin will coordinate the professional appraisals listed in Exhibit A (Art Appraiser); any other appraisal remains the responsibility of the Client and the estate attorney"* (*the Client and their counsel* on a trust-only matter, *the Client and their advisers* on *Neither*, since 2026-10-03) — and the §5.3 row reads *Arrange the appraisals listed in Exhibit A*; with none on the estimate, counsel arranges them all. Changing the scope means **✎ Edit estimate**, which withdraws the agreement's approval; once the manager re-approves the estimate, the next print or send files the fresh packet over the old one. Read §2 and §5.2 before it goes out — that is where it would contradict what the attorney told you.

> **⚠** **Read the estate agreement's first page against the matter before it goes out (new 2026-10-03).** On a **trust-only** matter it names the trust as the party (*The &lt;name&gt;, dated &lt;date&gt;*, from Edit Client; lines to complete where either is not recorded) with the decedent beside it, a *Certification of Trust* row where the Letters were, the *Trustee's Attorney*, and no case number or court, and the signature page is headed *Client / successor trustee*. On *Neither* it names no paper and no court, and the client signs as *Client*. *Both* keeps the probate form and adds the trust beside the estate. **If a trust matter's agreement names an estate, Letters or a case, the matter type is wrong or unanswered** (an unanswered matter signs the probate form on purpose): fix it on **✎ Edit Client** and regenerate. **Record every co-trustee or co-executor before you send.** Each one recorded gets a signature block of their own, named, and §5.1 says each of them has signed or the client may bind them. **Since 2026-10-05 (P20) each signs in DocuSign beside the client**, on their own block, emailed by DocuSign, so record each one's email first. On the sign-by-hand routes they sign the printed page, and you file it from the timeline (Step 8). (Until 2026-10-05 they always signed a printed copy, DocuSign carried the client and Anthony only, and you put their pages in the Drive folder yourself.) **§5.4 is on every estate agreement now**: nobody at Havellin, and nobody on our crew, buys anything from the house or takes a cut of a sale. If a representative or a family member offers, the answer is no.

> **agreements@ gets a copy either way.** DocuSign copies it on the envelope and mails it the executed contract once both have signed, and the PDF route's Gmail draft is CC'd to it. Both carry the whole **signing packet**, so the client never receives an agreement whose Exhibit A is missing. *Until 2026-09-08 the agreement's Email to Client button did nothing, so agreements@ has no copy of anything sent to a client before then.*

> **One document goes into their Drive folder, and it is the packet (changed 2026-09-11).** It used to file two — the packet *and* the agreement on its own. They sat side by side under almost the same name, and only one of them is signable: the bare agreement is the terms with **Exhibit A missing**, which both forms say makes it invalid. Nothing is lost by filing one, because the packet *contains* the agreement as its first page. **If you are looking in a folder from before that date you will see both.** The one to keep is the one whose name reads **Havellin Services Agreement**. The one ending *_Agreement.html* is the half-document — do not send it to anybody.

> **⚠ If a representative asks whether the crew sign NDAs, the answer is yes — and it was signed long before this job (changed 2026-09-20).** Every person who works a Havellin engagement, staff and 1099 contractors alike, is under a **signed written agreement carrying confidentiality obligations** before they work any job at all. There is nothing to collect on the day and **nothing for anyone to sign on site** — do not offer it. **Until 2026-09-20 §7 of the estate agreement promised something else**: that Havellin would require a Non-Disclosure Agreement from every team member and contractor *before accessing the property*. We never took one, so the contract promised a step the firm does not perform. Anthony: *"we do not require NDAs for staff on all jobs. confidentiality is baked into our 1099 employment agreements."* §7 now states the standing obligation instead, and the matching *NDA* box came off Before Day 1 on the Job Plan the same day. **An agreement signed before that date is fine and does not need re-issuing.** What the crew is actually bound by is broader than what that clause described. Say what is true; do not read the old clause back to them.

The buttons then appear one at a time, each in its own turn, each recording who did it and when. None of them will run out of order — you cannot mark a signature on an agreement that was never sent.

| Button | Press it when |
| --- | --- |
| **🖨 Print packet** | One PDF: the agreement, then the approved estimate as Exhibit A on a new page. **This is what goes out for signature** — the agreement alone refers to an exhibit the client does not have. It is filed to the client's Drive *Agreement* folder, on its own, the first time it is printed or sent. **Printing does not record it as sent.** |
| **✉ Send for signature — DocuSign** | **The normal route, on every device.** DocuSign emails the packet to the client and it comes back signed by itself. Nothing to confirm afterwards, and **no "I've sent it" button appears** — DocuSign did the sending. **The button says which route it takes**, so read it before you press. |
| **📄 Send as PDF to sign by hand** | **The old-school route, per job.** A Gmail draft with the packet attached, for you to read and send. Use it for a client who will not sign on a screen. It changes nothing for any other job. |
| **🤝 Handed over in person** | **A printed packet you give the client yourself** (since 2026-10-01). It records the packet as sent, in person, and the step moves on to the signature. Offered beside the two send buttons, on the same step, until the packet has gone. |
| **✓ I've sent it** | *PDF route only.* You have emailed it. |
| **✓ Record the signed agreement** | *PDF route, or handed over in person.* The signed copy has come back. It asks **who signed it** — see step 8. |
| **✓ Record payment** | The money is in your hand. This is what lets work begin. |

> **⚠** **Pick the route before you press, because it is fixed from that moment.** Both send buttons sit side by side until one is pressed; afterwards **only the one you chose is offered**. An agreement already sitting in a client's DocuSign inbox must not also be recorded as signed by hand — that is two records of one contract disagreeing. **If you send the wrong way, do not try to work around it: tell Anthony.** **A job you papered before DocuSign was switched on keeps its old buttons**, permanently. Nothing in flight was stranded. **There is no longer a Settings switch for this and you do not need to look for one.** Until 2026-09-18 it was a per-device dropdown that defaulted to the old email route — so an agreement sent from a browser nobody had configured went out the old way, which is exactly what happened once. DocuSign is now the firm's route on every device. The per-job PDF button is unaffected.

> **What the agreement now promises about the record you are about to build (new 2026-09-21).** Both forms define **Project Records** — the written list of what was documented, where each item went and **who took it** — and hold it exactly like the photographs: in the client’s own Drive folder, seen only by the crew on the job and anyone the client names in writing, kept seven years, never sold, and **never used for marketing** whatever the opt-out box says. Two things follow for you. **The client gets a copy at the end**, so it is a deliverable rather than an internal note. And the contract says in terms that it is **not an appraisal and not proof that anything changed hands in law** — so do not tell a client it is, and do not let one treat it as a tax record. If they need a valuation or a receipt, that is theirs to obtain.

> **A client can ask for a name to be left off.** On the living-client form only, the agreement lets the client ask in writing that a recipient not be named; record the disposition without the name if they do. On an estate there is no such option — a personal representative cannot keep a beneficiary’s name out of the fiduciary record.

## Step 7 · Staff the job team — Job Plan tab

Do this in the wait between sending the agreement and the deposit landing. You know the job is happening; now find out who is actually free.

1. On the client's **Job Plan**, open **Hours & daily close**; the **Job Team & Hours** roster is inside it. It shows the crew the approved estimate was priced for — not a blank six slots — plus a concierge row and an optional second concierge row.
2. Confirm each person's availability, then name them. Any empty planned slot is flagged **"needs a name."**
3. Need somebody beyond what was quoted? **+ Add a specialist beyond plan**. Those rows are tagged *beyond plan*, because crew above plan eats margin against a fixed quote and the projection will say so.
4. **Save & Confirm Job Team →**

- **Placeholders are fine.** *Contractor TBD* and *Contractor — TC* mean a confirmed need with an unconfirmed person. They cost at a placeholder rate, so the app says out loud that margin is an estimate until you name them.
- **Confirming locks the named people.** Empty slots stay open so you can add mid-job.
- **Reload before you change the team on a client somebody else has touched today** — the Job Plan's refresh does not bring another device's team changes. Until the 2026-09-30 Apps Script redeploy is live, a change made on an older copy takes the whole client (see the red box in Step 10c); once it is, the team merges part by part (concierge, second concierge, specialists, sign-off), but reload anyway.
- **Revise team** reopens the roster — but anyone who has already logged hours stays locked. You can add to a team mid-job; you can never retroactively remove somebody who worked. Hours stay shut until you re-confirm.
- **One person, one slot.** A name taken disappears from the other selects, and picking somebody who already holds another slot is refused on the spot, naming the slot they are in (2026-09-23). The confirm refuses it too. Only *Contractor TBD* and *Contractor — TC* may appear twice.
- **A second concierge the estimate staffs needs a name before the team confirms** (2026-09-29). If the estimate's **Concierges** control was set to 2, the *Transition Concierge 2* row reads *needs a name* and **Save & Confirm Job Team** refuses until it has one — pick somebody, or *Contractor — TC* if you have not picked yet. On a rush job the estimate *promised* that second person, so the slot cannot quietly go empty.
- **The concierge row starts on whoever was assigned at intake**, not whoever did the walkthrough (2026-09-23). Reassign the job in **✎ Edit Client** before the team is confirmed and the plan follows; once you pick a concierge on the plan or confirm the team, that choice stands.

> **The chip row at the top of the plan tells you whether the team is done (new 2026-09-23).** *Job team confirmed* sits beside *Vendors lined up*: red until you press **Save & Confirm Job Team**, green the moment you do. **It goes red again if one person is in two slots** — press **Revise team** and give one of them to somebody else.

> **⚠** **Confirming the team does not start the clock.** Hours stay locked until the deposit is in. A confirmed team on an unfunded job keeps its confirmation and tells you plainly that the deposit is what's missing. Staffing ahead of payment is deliberate; starting work is not.

## Step 8 · Signed copy back

**What you do here depends entirely on how you sent it in Step 6.**

### If it went out through DocuSign — one button, and you rarely need it

The *Agreement signed* step reads **DocuSign is watching for it**. When the envelope completes — the client (and on an estate each co-representative, who signs beside them since 2026-10-05), then Anthony's countersignature — the app marks it signed by itself, **names the person who actually signed** (and each co-representative, with their own day), on the day *they* signed and with the email address DocuSign checked (since 2026-09-30; it used to show the day the envelope completed, which is Anthony's countersignature), and files the executed copy and its certificate of completion into the client's **Agreement** folder in Drive. The one button in the band is **↻ Check DocuSign now** — for the moment you have watched the envelope complete and do not want to wait for the next time the app asks. This is the route in normal use: Anthony has sent envelopes through it that worked (reported 2026-09-30).

> **⚠** **⚠⚠ NOTHING RUNS ON A TIMER.** The app asks DocuSign when it loads (and when the client list reloads after a change on another device), when somebody opens the client, and when you press **↻ Check DocuSign now** in the band. **Whichever asks, it asks at most once every twenty minutes per agreement**, so opening it six times in a row asks once and the other five show you what it last heard. That limit is DocuSign's, not ours, and **the penalty for breaking it is losing API access for the whole firm**. That is why the button is safe to press: inside the window it **sends nothing** and tells you the time it can ask again, so it cannot be hammered past the limit. **If the client says they signed and the app still says waiting:** press the button and read what it says back — either a time to come back at, or that DocuSign does not report the envelope complete, which means it is genuinely still out. **Do not try to record it by hand** — the app will refuse, deliberately, so that two records of one contract cannot disagree.

> **Havellin signs second, and it is not done until we do.** The client signs first; the agreement then comes to Havellin to countersign. The job does **not** read as signed in between, because a half-executed contract is not a contract. **An agreement that seems stuck right after the client signed is usually waiting on us** — check the DocuSign inbox before chasing the client.

> **If the check fails it tells you, and tells you what it does not mean.** You will see the reason and the words *"It may already be signed; this is not a statement that it is not."* Open the client again later. A failed check never marks anything unsigned.

> **Two files land in Drive, not one** — the executed agreement, and the **certificate of completion**, which is DocuSign's record of who signed, from where, and when. On a probate matter that is the evidence of who bound the estate. **If either could not be filed you get an amber notice, and the *Agreement signed* row offers 📁 File signed copy** in the strip under the timeline (new 2026-10-01): press it and the app fetches both from DocuSign and files them. Do not let the certificate slide on an estate job. A press within 20 minutes of the last request, the automatic one included, is refused — DocuSign limits how often it can be asked — and the notice says when it can go again. The button goes once both are in Drive. (Until 2026-10-01 this note said to download both from DocuSign by hand.)

> **The countersignature goes to Anthony, and `agreements@` is copied on the finished contract.** Anthony signs on behalf of Havellin on every agreement — one named person, so the certificate of completion records who actually signed rather than whoever opened a shared inbox first. Once every signature is on it (the client's, on an estate each co-representative's, then Anthony's), DocuSign mails the executed copy to the client, to Anthony, and to `agreements@` for the file.

### What the client has to do — two things, and one box they can ignore

Worth knowing before they phone you about it. On the signing screen they are asked for their **signature** and the **date**. That is all that is required. There is also one optional tick box about marketing at the end of §10.2 — or §7.2 on an estate agreement, which carries the same box. **No initials anywhere, and no role tick box** — both were removed on 2026-09-18. A co-representative signing beside the client has no box: the client's choice speaks for the matter, and a co-representative who wants out says so in writing (§7.2; Anthony, 2026-10-05).

> **⚠** **The marketing box is an OPT-OUT. Leaving it alone is a yes.** One box, reading *I DO NOT AUTHORIZE any marketing or promotional use*. They tick it to decline; they leave it blank to authorize. **Ticking it changes nothing** about the work, the fee, or any other term — say so if they hesitate. **⚠ They can also decline in writing at any time, ticked box or not**, and withdraw an authorization later the same way. That is in §10.2 and it is the answer to give if a client rings after signing and says they have changed their mind. Withdrawing is forward-looking — it does not require pulling down anything already posted. **⚠ If a client asks whether we will post about their house:** yes, unless they tick the box or tell us in writing — and **never their address, never their name, never a face**, on any answer. **⚠ AN ESTATE OR PROBATE AGREEMENT HAS THE SAME BOX, in §7.2.** For a few hours on 2026-09-18 it was a flat prohibition with nothing to tick; that was reversed the same evening. A representative gets the same answer as anyone else, with the same restrictions behind it.

> **⚠** **⚠⚠ BUT TWO RULES ARE YOURS TO FOLLOW AND ARE NOT IN THE AGREEMENT. A signed box does not make either of these OK.** **1. Nothing goes out until the house has closed or been cleared.** An empty estate in Palm Beach whose contents you posted last week is a target, and the people who would use it that way read the same feeds clients do. Wait. **2. Never post a contested matter at all.** Not anonymised, not later, not with permission. A representative's decisions are exactly what the other side attacks, and a photograph of the house on our feed hands them something for free. If you are unsure whether a matter is contested, it is contested — ask Anthony. Neither of these is something the app or the contract will stop you doing. That is why they are here.

> **⚠ It changed on 2026-09-18 and the version before that lasted a day.** For a few hours the agreement asked them to pick *I AUTHORIZE* or *I DO NOT AUTHORIZE* and sign for it. DocuSign did not render those fields on the first real envelope, so it is one simple tick box now. **If you are looking at an agreement that shows two boxes and a second signature line, it was generated before the fix — regenerate it.**

> **Photographing the house is not one of the choices, and we do not ask permission for it.** That is how the inventory, the appraisal support and the chain of custody get built — it is the work they are paying for. The agreement says where the media is kept, who can see it, that it is never sold or licensed, and that it is not published except as the marketing section allows. If they ask, that is the answer; the marketing consent is a separate question and they answer that one themselves.


### If it went out as a PDF — record it the day it arrives

**✓ Record the signed agreement** — in the band when it reads *Get the agreement signed*. Do it the day it arrives — the deposit step will not open until you have.

> **⚠** **It asks WHO SIGNED, and it means the client (new 2026-09-11).** Not you. Not the manager who approved the price. **The name of the person who signed the contract.** Until this build the app recorded the Havellin manager who approved the *estimate* and displayed that as the signer — so the dashboard read *"Agreement signed · Anthony Graziano"* over a contract Anthony had not signed, and **nowhere did the app hold the name of whoever actually signed it**. On a probate matter that is the first thing counsel asks: who bound the estate to this? It prefills the authorised representative (or the client on a living-owner job) and **will not accept a blank**. It also asks how it came back — *Signed in person* or *Signed copy returned* — the date *they* signed rather than the date you typed it, and a note (where the original is held is the useful thing to put there). **A job signed before 2026-09-11 reads as signed with no signer named**, and says so. That is correct and there is nothing to fix: we genuinely did not record it. Do not go back and type a name in from memory.

> **⚠** **Co-representatives sign the printed page too, and the app asks for it (since 2026-10-05, P20).** On an estate with co-representatives recorded, once you record the client's signature the notice and the *Agreement signed* step say whose signature is not on record yet, in amber: *Not on record: the signature of Daniel Adler. File the page they signed, under the timeline*. Get the signature page they signed (their own *Co-Signer* block), then under the timeline, in *Co-representatives on the agreement*, press **File the page signed by Daniel Adler** and pick the PDF or a photograph of it. It is filed to the client's *Signed Records* folder, the flag clears and the step names them. Filed the wrong page? Press **Void** beside its link, give the reason, and file the right one. It is a flag, not a block — the deposit and the activation go ahead without it — but do not leave it. The same applies when a DocuSign envelope went without them (an Apps Script deployment older than 2026-10-05: tell Anthony). The agreement says as much: since 2026-10-05 its signature page reads that no work begins until the client and Havellin have signed and the deposit is in (it said *both signatures*).

## Step 9 · Record the deposit — Client Dashboard

The deposit is **50% of the approved total** and it is **never waived or varied**. The same button records all three payments; a *Which payment is this?* picker at the top opens on the first unsatisfied stage, so in normal use you never touch it. Press **✓ Record payment** on a timeline row and it opens on that row's stage, with the stage in its title and on its button: *Record Deposit*, *Record Midpoint Payment*, *Record Final Payment* (*Record Second Payment* on Home Prep).

Recording a payment captures evidence, not a tick:

| Field | What to put |
| --- | --- |
| **Amount** | Prefilled with what is still outstanding on that stage — the deposit at 50% less anything already in, the midpoint and the final at **the figure their own invoice asks for** (2026-09-29; they used to open blank). A second cheque needs no arithmetic. **Check it against the cheque in your hand**, and if the cheque is different, type what arrived. |
| **Date received** | The day it reached your hand, not the day it clears. It opens on today — your today: until 2026-09-29, after 8pm it opened on tomorrow. |
| **Method & reference** | Cheque number, wire confirmation, Stripe id. Pick **Bank transfer (ACH)** only for a transfer *not* sent through our payment link — one sent through the link records itself. **There is no Card** (since 2026-09-30): Havellin takes no card payments, and the app refuses one. |
| **Paid by** | The trust, estate account, law firm or the client. In estate work the payer is often not the client, and this is what shows the estate is funding the engagement properly. It also settles the argument if heirs later dispute who paid for what. |
| **Photo of the cheque** | Take it *before the cheque leaves your hands.* Five seconds, and it's what makes a hand-recorded payment a document rather than an assertion. It files to Drive against the payment. |

**Partial payments are normal.** Two cheques, or the trust sending part and the family the rest, all work. The job stays part-paid and unfunded until the running total hits the target, the bar shows what's outstanding, and the button stays available for the next one. A short total asks before it's accepted; a second cheque that completes the deposit goes through without a warning.

> **Work starts on *received*, not *cleared*.** Waiting for cheques to clear costs three to five days on every job. Wires and cash are recorded as cleared. A cheque, or a bank transfer you record by hand, is recorded as *uncleared*, and the recorder lists it that way. **Once the bank shows it as final, press ✓ Mark cleared** beside it on the payments list under the timeline (since 2026-09-30; until then nothing in the app marked it cleared). A transfer through our payment link is recorded only once it has settled, so it arrives cleared.

> **⚠ A CLIENT CAN PAY THE DEPOSIT BY BANK TRANSFER, AND THAT ONE RECORDS ITSELF.** The band carries **🏦 ACH payment link** beside **✉ Send deposit invoice**, and beside **✓ Record payment** until the deposit is in. Press it, send the link the app shows you along with the invoice, and when the transfer lands the payment writes itself — amount, date, payer, and *Stripe* as who recorded it. **Do not also record it by hand.** If you did anyway — a bank transfer at the same stage for the same amount — the app now matches Stripe's report to your entry and clears it instead of adding a second, and says so (since 2026-09-30; until then the deposit went on the job twice).
>
> **It takes about four business days, and that is the normal state of it.** The client authorises the transfer on day one and the money arrives on day four or five. In between, the client will tell you they have paid and the app will show nothing. They are both right. **Do not record it by hand to make the screen agree with them** — that is the app claiming money is in the account when it is not.
>
> **The check runs when the app loads and when you open the client.** There is no timer. If you are waiting on a transfer, open the client.
>
> **After a part cheque the link asks only for the balance** (2026-09-29), and the Stripe page says *balance* so the smaller figure does not look like a mistake. **If the app says a link it already made "asks for $…, but only $… is outstanding … Do not send it"**, it was made before the cheque came in: a link cannot be edited, so switch it off in the Stripe Dashboard under Payment links and take the balance by cheque or wire.
>
> **Cards are not accepted.** Not an oversight and not a setting to change: a card on a $25,715 job costs Havellin $747 against $16.50 on a bank transfer, and the "3% convenience fee" that would cover it is only legal on tickets under about $300. If a client insists on a card, the answer is a cheque or a wire. The payment recorder has no *Card* option either (since 2026-09-30).

### A payment already recorded: Mark cleared, or Void (new 2026-09-30)

Every payment on the client is listed under the timeline on the Client Dashboard, under **Payments recorded**, with its state: *Uncleared*, *Cleared* (with the day and who), or *Void* (with the day, who and why).

| Button | When to press it |
|---|---|
| **✓ Mark cleared** | The bank shows the cheque or transfer as final. It asks first, then records today. It changes no total: the job was funded when the money was received. |
| **Void** | A cheque came back unpaid, a payment was typed twice, or it went on the wrong client. The dialog tells you **what the void does to the money before you confirm** — on a deposit it says when the job stops being funded, which stops hours being logged, and that the timeline will show *Deposit voided: record the replacement payment*. **A reason is required.** The payment is not deleted: it stays on the list, struck through, with your reason, and counts toward nothing. Then record the money the way it really arrived. |

> **There is no un-void, and a recorded payment is never edited — decided with Anthony, 2026-10-01.** If you voided the wrong payment, record it again. If a payment went in with the wrong amount, date or method, void it with the reason and record it again the right way. Nobody can delete or change a payment: the record of what was received, and what was taken back and why, is the point.

> **The cheque that paid the deposit bounced, and the job is already running? (since 2026-10-01)** Void it as above. The timeline's deposit step turns **red** and the band reads ***Deposit voided: record the replacement payment***, with what was voided and why, and the fix under it. **The job stays active** and nothing is undone, but no hours can be logged until the deposit is whole again. When the replacement comes in, press **✓ Record payment** on that red band; the step goes green and the band moves on. A payment typed twice and voided does not do this, because the deposit is still whole.

**Refunds are listed here too (since 2026-10-01)**, as *Refund* with how it went, to whom and the day it was sent, and the heading reads *Payments and refunds recorded*. A refund has no **Mark cleared**; one recorded by mistake is voided like a payment (Step 13, *If the job dies instead*).

### Deposits over $10,000

Above $10,000 a personal cheque is no longer the accepted instrument: **wire preferred, cashier's cheque accepted.** Recording a large personal cheque anyway is allowed — the money is already in hand — but it is flagged on the payment record as a policy exception.

Two things worth knowing when you ask for one:

- A **certified** cheque is not a **cashier's** cheque. Certified is drawn on the client's account with the bank only secondarily liable. A cashier's cheque is drawn on the bank's own account with the money already taken. Ask for the second one, by name.
- Neither is bounce-proof — counterfeit cashier's cheques are a common fraud and a bank can reverse weeks later. On a large one, phone the *issuing* bank on a number you look up yourself, never the number printed on the cheque. A wire beats both: it's final on receipt, and it's what trust officers and law firms do routinely anyway.

### What you're collecting, and when

| Milestone | Amount | Timing |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Deposit | 50% of the estimate | On signing |
| Midpoint *(Second payment on Home Prep)* | Brings the cumulative to 75%, with vendor and prep fees trued to the actual quotes you've logged | At the halfway point — the calendar halfway, half the work done or every room locked, whichever comes first (Home Prep: once the vendor schedule is booked) |
| Final | The balance. Hourly: labour at the hours logged (change-order hours included, since the crew logs them) and fees on actuals. Fixed price: the rest of the fixed fee, plus the price of each accepted change order and the prep fee on the actual vendor invoices. Home Prep: the fee on actual vendor spend, plus any logged declutter hours. | Within 7 days of the final invoice |

> Only the deposit gates anything. Recording a midpoint or final payment captures the money so the job's paid total is complete — it doesn't unlock anything, and a midpoint cheque of any size will never fund a job. Those two stages open on what their own invoice asks for and challenge nothing: check the figure against the cheque.

## Step 10 · Work the job

On the day the crew starts, press **▶ Activate job** on the client's timeline. The job is now **Active**, and the app takes you straight to **that client's Job Plan** (since 2026-09-23). Back on the dashboard the *Job active* step shows that day and the job's assigned concierge (2026-09-30). Three things run in parallel.

> **⚠ Activate on the day work starts, not before.** Activating is what fixes the job's start date, and every date on the schedule line is counted from it. Press it ahead of the target start and the app asks *Activate this job today?* with both dates on it: **press Cancel unless the crew really is starting today.** The date is stamped once and cannot be changed afterwards.

### The plan, top to bottom (rebuilt 2026-09-19, opened up 2026-09-20)

The Job Plan runs in the order you work a job, and the stages are named, not numbered. Two tools fold at the top; everything under them is the job itself, open, on one thread with a node per stage — green behind you, a bronze **NOW** where you are, grey ahead:

1. **Where you stand** — the schedule strip under the client line. Before the job starts: the target start, how many working days, halfway and the target end. From the day it starts: the day you actually started, today, *Working day 3 of 6*, how many working days are left, halfway and the planned end — every date counted from the day you started. The same line the Client Dashboard shows (see *Where you are against the schedule*, below).
2. **Gates** — one row of chips: agreement signed, deposit received, on probate the Letters and the §733.604 deadline, on a trust the Certification of Trust (new 2026-10-03), the attorney, the Form 706 date on an estate whose 706 is not answered *No* (new 2026-10-03), vendors lined up, and the job team (new 2026-09-23). Green or red, read off the record, nothing to tick. A red one says underneath what clears it.
3. **Vendors & partners** — a tool, folded, with *2 of 4 confirmed* on the bar. It opens by itself while the job is not yet active and anything on it is unconfirmed. Step 10c.
4. **Hours & daily close** — a tool, folded, with today's hours on the bar; you open it to log the day. Step 10b.
5. **Before Day 1** — the pre-job call and its notes, materials on site, keys tested, crew briefed, COI; on probate the PR's authority confirmed, and on a trust the Certification of Trust read, with any limits on the trustee's powers noted (new 2026-10-03). Open, ticks counted in the heading.
   ⚠ **The NDA box went on 2026-09-20.** Havellin does not take a per-job NDA — confidentiality is in the contractor agreement every crew member signs before they work an engagement at all, so there is nothing to collect on the day and nothing to tick.
6. **In the house** — the room cards, and the in-house boxes under them: four on an estate job (firearms photographed where they lie, each checked for NFA, the papers sequestered, valuables logged), and under them the **Found on site** card for an original will or cash (new 2026-10-03, Step 10g); one on a living-client job (every room photographed and every item of consequence logged before it is moved), plus the two firearms boxes when intake flagged firearms. Step 10a.
7. **Midpoint & pickups** — the midpoint invoice lines, release authority, receipts, pickups, shredding. Open.
8. **Move day** — Home Transition only.
9. **Close-out** — the final walk, the empty-house check, the handoff, then the **Close-out card**: satisfaction call, Google review email, referral ask, and the vendor scorecard, which is required (Step 13). Open.

> **Nothing in the job folds, and the stage you are in says NOW.** Before Day 1 until the job is active; In the house while it is; Midpoint & pickups once every room is locked; Close-out once the midpoint is paid and the rooms are cleared, or the moment the job is closed. Move day is never marked NOW — it reads done once Close-out does. The only bar that opens by itself is *Vendors & partners*, while you are still lining them up; tap either bar to open or close it and it stays that way until you reload. The phase numbers are gone — Phase 3 was Move day, which most jobs never have, so the old plan counted 0, 1, 2, 4.

> **The brown Next band is on the Job Plan now (2026-09-22)**, under the client line, with the *Deposit & Start* half of the timeline under it — exactly what the Client Dashboard shows, same buttons. It is how you get reminded to send the midpoint invoice without leaving the plan. Job Admin & Inv carries the same band.

### Before Day 1 — read the walkthrough back

Open the client and press **🔍 Walkthrough**, on the same line as *Edit Client* and *Drive*. It opens the whole walkthrough over the dashboard: **your private notes first**, then every room you ticked with its fullness and complexity scores and whatever you wrote in it, the collections and their dispositions, the vehicles, and any prep scope notes. This is the one to do the night before, or sitting outside in the car.

> **⚠ IT IS AN INTERNAL PAGE. DO NOT SHOW IT TO ANYONE IN THE HOUSE.** It carries your private notes word for word — who is hoarding, which son disagrees with which daughter, the access problem you did not raise in front of them. There is deliberately **no Print button and no way to send or file it**, so it cannot end up on a kitchen counter or in a shared folder. Read it off the screen and close it.

> **If the estimate was approved you are reading the frozen copy**, and the page says *Locked &lt;date&gt; by &lt;name&gt;* at the top. If it was not, it says so in amber — those scores can still move. A room reading *Out of scope* is one the client is handling; that is an answer you recorded, not a room you missed.

### Where you are against the schedule — on the Job Plan header and the Client Dashboard

The line under the client card on the Job Plan (new 2026-09-20) and the line above the timeline on the Client Dashboard are the same line: *Started Sep 22, 2026 · Today Sep 24, 2026 · **Working day 3 of 6** · 3 working days to go · 36% of the work done · 50% of the estimated hours logged · Halfway Sep 24 · Planned end Sep 29*, with the hard target or the court deadline beside it. Before the job starts it reads *Target start · 6 working days · Halfway · Target end* instead. Anthony asked for it on the plan — *"projected six days, today is the 24th, three more days … that lets you know where you stand in the job"* — and on 2026-09-23 the target dates came off it once the job has started: *"do away with the old ones like the target start date and target midpoint because it's very confusing."*

> **⚠** **⚠ THOSE TWO PERCENTAGES ARE DIFFERENT QUESTIONS AND THE GAP BETWEEN THEM IS THE POINT (new 2026-09-13).** **Work done** comes from the room statuses you set on the Job Plan, weighted by the hours the estimate priced each room at. **Hours logged** comes from your timesheet. *70% spent against 70% done* is a job going fine. *70% spent against 30% done* is a job in trouble — and one number could never have told you which you were looking at. **Look at the pair, not at either one.**

> **⚠** **⚠ THE WORK FIGURE ONLY EXISTS IF SOMEBODY SETS THE ROOM STATUSES.** If hours are going in and no room is marked done, the app **will not print a percentage** — it says *"36 hours logged and no room marked done yet, so there is no progress reading"* and points you at the Job Plan. That is deliberate: a crew that has not touched the statuses and a crew that has done nothing look identical from the dashboard, and it will not assert the second. **Set each room to *Locked* once it is sorted and shot, and *Cleared* once the specialists have emptied it — two taps in the room's workspace, as you go.** Two minutes a room, and it is what makes the whole schedule reading work — for you, and for whoever is reading the job from the desk.

| What the line says | What to do |
|---|---|
| **red** — *N% of the work is done on working day X of Y. Tracking to &lt;date&gt;, Z working days past the planned end.* | At the rate you are actually going, this job finishes late. Ring the client and re-plan, or raise a change order if the scope grew. **Do not wait for the planned end to arrive to have that conversation.** |
| **red** — *Working day X of Y — Z working days past the proposed length.* | You are already over the length that was quoted. Same conversation, more urgently. If the scope grew and the client signs a change order for it, the plan lengthens by its hours the moment they accept, and the line says *incl. 2 days by change order* (2026-09-25). |
| **amber** — *Past the halfway point and the midpoint invoice has not gone out.* | This one is about **money**, not about the work. Send it from the band above. |
| **grey** — *N hours logged and no room marked done yet.* | Not a telling-off. Go and set the room statuses. |
| **red** — *ends N working days past the hard target / court deadline* | The **plan itself** does not reach the date we committed to — separate from how the job is running. On probate that date is statutory. Raise it now. |
| *Form 706 due &lt;date&gt;* — **red** inside 30 days (*in N days*), and once it has passed | New 2026-10-03 (P19). The estate's federal tax return, due nine months after the death. **It is not ours to file**: counsel or the estate's accountant files it or an extension. Red means ring the attorney and make sure it is in hand; grey is only a reminder. *If a return is filed* means nobody has answered the 706 question — ask, and record the answer on Edit Client. |

> **It stays quiet for the first day or so, and that is right.** It will not call a job behind until at least one crew-day of hours is logged and a fifth of the work is done — on the first morning, one room of twelve finished projects to a thirteen-day job. The percentages still show; it just does not draw a conclusion from them yet. **And it never tells you you are ahead** — the percentage is right there beside the day count, so a flag saying so would be telling you something you can already see.

> **"Halfway" is the middle working day of the plan, and it is when the midpoint invoice falls due (2026-09-29).** Until then — or until half the work is done or every room is locked, if either comes first — the band says *Do the work* and the midpoint invoice waits beside it as an outline button reading *due around <date>* (Step 11). The strip itself still never prints the word *midpoint* next to the date.

> **⚠ Once the job starts, the line counts from the day it started, and the target dates go (changed 2026-09-23).** It used to keep the target start and add a second end date beside it — *target was &lt;date&gt;* and *now ending &lt;date&gt;* on one line. Now there is one set of dates at a time: the target ones until you activate, the real ones after. **The client's estimate and agreement still carry the dates they signed against**, so if the planned end moved and it matters to them, tell them. A hard target the job no longer reaches turns the line red the day it starts.

> **Home Prep for Sale has no day count and says so.** That engagement is scheduled around the vendors, and even with declutter hours quoted it books no specialist hours and runs on the vendors' calendar, so its line reads *"scheduled around vendor availability"* rather than inventing a number.


> **⚠ The Job Plan keeps itself current while it is open (new 2026-09-12).** Two people work this tab at once — one in the house, one at the desk — and until this date it only read the plan **when the page loaded**. So a room locked in the house did not appear at the desk until somebody reloaded. It now re-reads roughly **every 20 seconds**, and again the moment you open the tab. Two things it will not do, both on purpose: it **never refreshes while you are typing in the plan** (a refresh redraws the whole tab and would take your half-written note with it), and it **stands down entirely while the red unsaved-changes chip is showing** — your own work has not reached the sheet yet, so pulling the sheet in would undo the room you just locked. In both cases it simply waits. **It does not bring vendors or the team** — those are saved on the client, not on the plan — so for those, reload (see the red box in *c. Source the vendors*). Nothing else keeps a client current mid-job; for a current **Client Dashboard**, reload the page.

### a. Shoot the rooms — Job Plan tab

The rooms are a block of **cards, three across on a desk** (two on the iPad, one on a phone), the *In the house* card on the Job Plan, right under *Before Day 1*, once the job is **Won** — one card per room you priced, in walkthrough order, with the status pill beside the name, the shot counts (*2 found · 5 items · 1 after*) under it and the walkthrough note on one line. The rooms the client is keeping are named in one line underneath rather than listed. **Tap a room** and it opens as a **pop-up over the plan** (the whole screen on a phone) — you never leave the Job Plan; *← Rooms*, a tap outside it or Esc closes it. Inside: three camera buttons, the two status taps, and a strip of everything shot in it so far. Above the cameras is the intake brief — every must-find with a **Found it** tick, the safety answer, the cash and the safes — so you read it in the room, not from memory. Rebuilt 2026-09-19 — the old room cards, with a typed name, a category and seven chips before every shot, are gone.

> **⚠** **Two passes through every room, in this order, and you type nothing in either.**
>
> **1 · 📷 As found.** Before anything moves: the room from the door, then **every drawer, cabinet, closet and box opened and shot as it sits**. Do not tidy, do not pull things out. These are the evidence of what the house held on Day 1 and they never become inventory lines. When every drawer, cabinet and closet has been opened and shot, tick **As-found pass complete** under the cameras — it records the date and the job's concierge, and the As-Found Record prints it. **On an estate or probate job the room cannot be locked without at least one as-found shot and that tick** — Lock is greyed out and the room says which is missing (*Shoot the room as found first*, then *Confirm the as-found pass first*). On a living-client job it lets you through and flags either one in amber.
>
> **2 · 📷 Items.** Sort the room, then shoot it: one shot per object, or per obvious lot. Pick a chip — **Keep · New home · To a person · Donate · Sell · Remove · Undecided** (*New home* only on a Home Transition or a job with a new address on file; both new 2026-09-21, and at the desk they read *Move* and *Distribute*) — and it stays lit shot after shot until you change it, so a shelf going to Sell is one tap for the whole shelf. **Undecided is the default**: anything you do not decide in the house lands at the top of the Job Admin & Inv tab that evening, which is where it belongs. No name, no category, no value — that is desk work.

- **⚑ Appraise** — tap it *before* the shot you want a specialist to see. It clears itself after every shot, so it is asked again for the next object: a judgement about one thing, never a setting for the room.
- **Detail of last** — a maker's mark, a signature, the damage, the label inside the lid. Tap it and the next shot files under the *previous* item instead of starting a new one. It is greyed out until you have shot an item to be a detail of.
- **🎤 Hold to talk** — hold the button, say it, let go; the words ride the last shot as a note (*"signed on the base, promised to Karen"*) and are saved the moment you let go. **Or type it** in the box beside it — always there, and the only way where the phone cannot dictate. A typed note saves itself when you press Enter, take the next shot or press **Done**; there is no Add button any more (2026-09-20). It stays on the shot it was typed about, even if you shoot the next one before you finish. An item's note shows on its row on the Job Admin & Inv tab, where you can correct it in the item panel; an as-found note prints on the As-Found Record.
- **The camera stays open until you press Done.** Shoot the whole room in one go — the count in the corner ticks up, the last shot sits as a thumbnail in the *Last* panel under the picture, with a 🗑 to delete it, and every shot is filed to the client's Drive folder as you go — the **As found** pass into **As-Found Record**, everything else into **Estate Inventory** (new 2026-09-20; before that they shared one folder, and a job that started before the change keeps sharing it). Counsel is given both in one action, so nothing about how you share changes. The phone's own camera app is not used unless the browser has no in-page camera or the site is refused camera access, in which case the same screen falls back to it one shot at a time.

**Then the two taps.** **Lock** when the room is sorted and shot — the decisions are made, and once every room is locked the plan moves on to *Midpoint & pickups* and **the midpoint invoice is due**: the Job Plan puts up a red *you're at the project midpoint* banner and the Client Dashboard makes *Send midpoint invoice* its filled button. It can fall due sooner, at the plan's halfway day or once half the work is done (Step 11). **Cleared** when the specialists have finished and the room is empty: shoot **📷 After** first (it flags you if you have not), then tap it. Cleared is not offered until the room is locked, and both taps are on the same screen as the camera, so there is nothing to find. A room you marked *packed* or *complete* on a build before 2026-09-19 reads *Cleared* now; nothing was lost.

> **Check the card after you shoot.** The counts on the room card are every shot taken in that room, saved or not; a red **⚠ not saved** beside them means at least one did not reach Drive. Open the room and press **Retry** on that shot in the strip. Retry works across sessions, so a photo that failed yesterday can still be sent today. If Retry tells you the image can no longer be recovered, the device did not have room to hold it — take the shot again.
>
> **To delete a photo, press the 🗑 on its thumbnail** in the room's strip, or on the *Last* shot in the camera (new 2026-09-20; before that only a failed shot could be thrown away, with a ✕ that is gone). It asks first. The shot comes off the job and out of the client's Drive folder — Google keeps it in the Drive bin for 30 days — and an item shot takes its detail shots and any lines split out of it with it; the question names them. A detail shot that reached Drive has no tile of its own: delete it in the camera straight after you take it, or it goes with its item. One that did **not** save has a tile under *Detail shots not saved*, naming its item, with **Retry** and the 🗑 (since 2026-09-30; until then it had neither once the camera moved on, and the card's *⚠ not saved* could never clear). A shot that never reached Drive is simply thrown away. **An as-found shot cannot be deleted**, saved or not: it is the record of the room before anything was touched, and a poor frame does no harm in it — shoot another.
>
> Rooms run in **walkthrough order**, matching the estimate, so you can work down the list the way you walked the house.

> **⚠** **Press ⚑ Appraise for anything you think is worth more than it looks. Nothing else in the house can tell.** In the field the app knows nothing about the object — no name, no category — so the automatic rule that routes art, jewellery, silver, antiques, rugs, collectibles, firearms, wine and instruments to the worklist fires *at the desk*, once the line has a category. What no rule will ever see is the period side table, the unmarked bronze, the thing that looks like furniture and is not. **That judgement is yours standing in the room**, and the toggle is how you record it. On a living-client job there is no automatic rule at all — nothing is valued there — so ⚑ is the only way a line reaches the desk's *To a specialist* pile.
>
> It is asked again for every shot, deliberately — the chip stays put between shots, this does not. If you meant it for the whole shelf, press it on each one.
>
> Missed it in the house? Tick **Needs Appraisal** in the item's panel (▾ on its row) that evening on the Job Admin & Inv tab. Nothing is lost by doing it later.

> **⚠** **If intake flagged firearms, a red banner is the first thing on the Job Plan and a red line sits at the top of every room's workspace.** It carries what intake wrote down (*"Two in the hall safe, PR has the code"*), the standing rule — nothing moves without written authority, photograph it where it lies, tell the concierge the same day, nobody carries one out — and the link to the protocol. It is the only place firearms shows on the plan: the Standing job flags panel under it carries the other flags and does not repeat it (since 2026-09-20), and if firearms was the only thing flagged there is no panel at all — the banner is the brief. If it reads *Ticked at intake, no detail recorded*, ring the client before Day 1. Read it to the crew in the driveway. **The two firearms boxes under the room grid stay on the plan for exactly this job** (photographed where they lie; each one checked for NFA) — and on every estate and probate job whether or not intake flagged anything.

> **⚠** **Read the Standing job flags aloud to the crew before anyone starts.** The panel at the top of the Job Plan is what intake was told about this house — cash and safes (firearms is the red banner above it), the thing the family cannot lose, the dog and the rotten step. *Before Day 1* has a checkbox for exactly this. Open the Job Plan on your phone and read it off the screen, in the driveway, before a door is opened.
>
> **⚠ There is no printed plan any more, and no printed flags panel with it.** *Print Job Plan* was retired on 2026-09-11 — it ran to **nine pages** of room cards whose status and Take Photo buttons do nothing on paper. So **throw away any plan printed before then**, and do not go looking for the button. The screen is the brief, and it is on every phone that can open the job.
>
> If a row reads *"Ticked at intake, no detail recorded"*, that is a question nobody finished asking — ring the client before Day 1 rather than finding out with a crew standing in the driveway. If something turns up that intake never mentioned, put it on the record through **Edit Client** so the next person reading the plan has it too.

> **⚠** **Found the ring? Press *Found it* on its line, in the room.** Every must-find is its own line on the brief — on the Job Plan header, in every room's workspace and above Job Admin on the Job Admin & Inv tab — with a **Found it** button. The line goes green, drops under the ones still missing and reads *Found Sep 19, 2026 · Ashley Jerome*: the date, and the concierge assigned to the job. **Undo** if it was a false alarm. It saves to the job like everything else, so the desk sees it too. Do not leave it for the evening — a must-find nobody ticked reads as still missing to the next person in, and they go looking for it.

> **⚠** **Found an original will, or cash? Record it on the *Found on site* card, today (new 2026-10-03).** It is under the in-house boxes on every estate job. An original will goes to the estate attorney the same day against a signed receipt, unopened and uncopied; cash is counted by two of you, sealed in a numbered bag, and the fiduciary signs for the bag the same day. Step 10g has the procedure.

> **⚠ You never type a name in the house, and now you never type one at the desk either unless you are correcting it.** Shoot the shelf once rather than each bottle on it — the agent splits the frame and names what is in it that evening (Step 10e). What is still worth your five seconds in the room: *Detail of last* on a maker's mark, a signature or a hallmark, because those close-ups are read and they turn a guess into a reading.

### b. Log hours — Job Plan tab, every single day

The log is the **Hours & daily close** bar at the top of the plan, under *Vendors & partners* and above the job (moved there 2026-09-20 — it spent one evening folded under the rooms). The bar reads *today 3 hrs · 36 of 63 logged*; on an active job with nothing logged for today it reads **no hours logged today** in amber. Tap the bar and the same form, summary, projection and history open under it. From the Client Dashboard, **+ Log Hours Today** opens the Job Plan on that client with this bar already open (2026-09-23).

One entry per working day: the **date**, an **activity summary**, and **hours against each named crew member**. The roles come from the roster you confirmed — concierge rows log as TC, specialist rows as PS.

> **⚠** **Hours go in as half hours (since 2026-10-02).** Anthony: *“maybe make it thirty minutes for logging hours (so a TC can bill 1hr, 1.5hrs or 2hrs)”*. Type 1, 1.5 or 2 — never 1.25. That goes for you, a second concierge and every crew row. Save refuses any row that is not a half hour, names it (*Not a half hour: Anthony Graziano Jr (1.25)*) and saves nothing, the good rows included, so correct it and save again. The final bills exactly what you logged, to the cent: nothing is rounded up. An entry from before today in quarter hours (they were quarters for one day, 2026-10-01) or tenths stays as it was and bills as logged. The client's agreement says the same: time is recorded and billed in half-hour increments, as worked.

> **⚠** **Log daily. Do not batch at end of job.** The hours drive the forward-variance projection *and* the final invoice — the timesheet *is* the invoice. On an hourly job, a final invoice cannot issue with no logged hours at all, and nobody can unlock it for you.

> **⚠** **Every job, fixed price included — no exceptions (Anthony, 2026-09-20).** A flat fee changes how the hours are billed, never whether they are logged. On a fixed-price job the final issues off the fee with nothing gated on the log, which is exactly why the log matters there: it is the only record of whether the fee and the timeline were right, and a fixed job that runs over its days is *our* margin going, not the client’s.
