Logging the day is part of the job for the transition concierge, whatever the billing basis, and the amber *no hours logged today* on the fold is meant to nag on a fixed-price job too.

> **⚠** **On a fixed-price job, going more than 15% over the hours is a margin warning, not a stop (2026-09-25).** The hours summary and the projection read *“⚠ Over the estimated hours — margin at risk”*, not *STOP*, and nobody has to ring the client: the fee does not move with the hours, so the overrun comes out of our margin, not their bill. Keep working and tighten the plan for the rest of the job. The one question worth asking is whether the extra time is work **beyond what we agreed** — if it is, raise a change order before doing more of it (d, below). On an hourly job the old rule stands: past 15%, the client is told in writing before work continues.

> **⚠** **Never log the time you spend on the prep vendors (2026-09-23).** Phoning the painter, letting the pool service in, checking the pressure washing — that time is what the **30% Home Sale Preparation Fee** on those vendors pays for. **On an hourly job, logging it bills the client twice**: once in the fee and again in your hours, because the final invoice bills what is logged. On a fixed-price job it bills nothing extra, but it makes the log say the flat fee took longer than it did — and the log is how we learn whether the fee was right. Log the job itself and leave the prep trades out of the day’s total. On a standalone Home Prep job the declutter hours go in the log and the trades never do.

Log *your own* hours as well as the crew's. You are on site for every crew hour; if the log shows crew time with no concierge time against it, the invoice warns you in dollars about the time it thinks you've missed.

> **Your hours are half of the schedule reading on the Client Dashboard.** The line above the timeline shows *hours logged* against *work done*, and the second half comes from the room statuses. Log daily and set the statuses as rooms finish, and anyone opening the client sees where the job really is. Skip either and the dashboard goes quiet rather than guessing. **This is true on a fixed-price job too** — a flat fee changes how the hours are billed, never whether they are recorded.

> **Once the client accepts a change order, its hours join the estimate on every hours figure (2026-09-25).** The bars on the Client Dashboard, the hours table and the projection on the Job Plan, the Hours bar and the schedule line all measure against the estimate *plus* the accepted change orders, and each says so under the figure — *incl. +40.0 hrs by change order*. So a job the client has signed extra hours for no longer reads *OVERAGE* or *STOP* over them. Hours past that still raise the same alarms, and a change order nobody has accepted moves nothing.

### c. Source the vendors — Job Plan tab

It is the **first bar on the plan** — *Vendors & partners*, a tool folded above the job — because these calls are made before the job starts. It opens by itself while the job is not yet active and anything on it is unconfirmed, and its fold counts the lines (*2 of 4 confirmed*); the *Vendors lined up* gate chip at the top of the plan goes green when they all are.

Assign a directory vendor to each estimate line, set its status, and **record the actual quote**. Actual quotes on **Home Prep** lines are what the 30% fee is charged on at the midpoint and final — on a standalone prep job and, since 2026-09-10, on prep bundled into any other job too — **fixed-price jobs included since 2026-09-23**, where the fee sits on top of the flat fee rather than inside it. Every other vendor line carries nothing for Havellin; those are recorded so the client's costs and the job's real margin are known. You will notice prep lines have **no *Coord hrs* box** — prep books no *coordination* hours, so there is nothing to compare against. (Declutter hours are a different thing and are logged in the hours log, not here.) A line with no logged quote falls back to the estimate and gets tagged "est." on the client's invoice.

Only **Active** vendors appear in the picker. An assigned vendor's phone is a tap-to-dial link, because you're usually standing in the house when you need them.

**End-of-job vendors are the ones on the estimate, and only those (since 2026-09-23).** A dumpster, a hauler, the move-out clean, the donation pickup or the shredder shows up here only if the estimate carried it. If the job turns out mid-way to need one, pick it from **+ Add an end-of-job vendor…** under the list: it becomes a line like any other and counts toward *Vendors lined up*. **Remove** takes one off again, and asks first if it has a vendor or a quote on it: the vendor comes off the job, and the quote comes off the vendor costs the invoices list (the client pays a vendor directly; until 2026-09-30 the question said the quote was *no longer billed*). **Each line with a vendor on it says who to call** (since 2026-09-30): the firm, its office number marked *office*, then each named contact beside their own mobile, and every number dials. It used to put the first contact's name beside the office number, which rang the switchboard.

**On a Home Prep job, a vendor a change order adds joins the list once the client accepts it (since 2026-09-30).** A trade the house turns out to need mid-job — a painter nobody quoted — goes on a change order (d, below). The moment the client accepts it, the vendor appears with the other prep lines under *Home Prep Vendors — Sourcing & Status*, budgeted at the estimated cost the change order carried, and in *Budget & Fee*: assign it, set its status and type its **Quote $** like any other line, and the 30% fee is charged on that quote. Until they accept, it is not on the plan. **On a job with prep bundled into the labour the same happens since 2026-09-30**: the vendor joins the *Home Prep for Sale* lines of the sourcing list.

> **⚠** **Two of you on one client: reload before you change a vendor or the team (2026-09-29, 2026-09-30).** Vendors and the team are saved on the *client*, not on the plan, so the Job Plan’s 20-second refresh does not bring the other device’s changes to them. Until 2026-09-29 the other device’s next save — of anything, on any client — could put back what you had just set: a confirmed painter, a dumpster, a whole confirmed team, gone, with nothing on either screen saying so. That is fixed; and since 2026-09-30, opening the client on a device that has sat since the morning, while the agreement is out for DocuSign signature or a payment link is out, no longer takes it over either. Once the Apps Script redeploy of 2026-09-30 is live, each vendor line and each part of the team merges on its own, so two people changing *different* ones both land. Until then — the out-of-date banner says so — a change you make on a copy of the client loaded *before* the other device changed it **takes the whole client and theirs is lost**. **So reload first**, every time, on a client somebody else has been working today.

> **Coord hrs — fill it in if it's easy, skip it if it isn't.** Next to each quote is a *Coord hrs* box showing what the estimate assumed (*est 3.0*). Roughly how long that vendor actually took you. **It bills nothing** — your logged hours already include the time you spent on the phone to them, so this is just labelling time that's already been charged, not adding any. It's there because the touch counts behind the quote are our best guess and nothing else in the app can check them; a handful of real jobs and we'll know whether a mover really is three hours. Nothing is gated on it and no invoice reads it. Once you've entered a few, a line at the bottom of the section totals estimated against recorded.

### d. Change Orders — Client Dashboard

Scope changed after approval? Do *not* edit the estimate — it's locked. On the client’s dashboard, press **+ New** on the **Change Orders** card → description, a reason, and **the extra concierge and specialist hours**, in whole hours (since 2026-10-02 a part hour such as 2.5 is refused and nothing is saved; for one day, 2026-10-01, they were quarters) → **Create Change Order**. **No manager PIN is involved** — a change order is agreed with the client, not approved internally.

> **⚠** **A change order carries HOURS. There is no dollar box, and on an ordinary job there is no price on the client's copy.** (Changed 2026-09-11 — it used to ask for a dollar impact. A fixed-price job is different — see the next box.) Type the extra concierge and specialist hours you expect; the screen tells you what that does to the job as you type — *“140.0 hrs on the estimate becomes 180.0 (+28.6%) — Past the 15% threshold”* in amber, or *“+2.9% — inside the 15% the client already agreed to”* in blue. **It will not save with no hours on it** (on Home Prep, with neither hours nor a vendor — see the Home Prep boxes below).
>
> **You are not billing them for the change order itself.** On an ordinary job you work the extra scope, log the hours like any other hours, and the final invoice picks them up. The change order is the client’s written authority, not a second charge. On a *fixed-price* job it is the other way round — a flat fee never looks at your timesheet, so there the hours are converted at the estimate’s rates (plus the 20% expedited-delivery premium on an expedited job, since 2026-09-30) and added to the fee, and the change order says so on its face. The invoice says which.
>
> **Holding a printed change order from before 11 Sep? Throw it away and print it again** — the old ones carried a dollar figure that is no longer how any of this works.

> **When the client accepts, the app files the accepted copy — their name and the date on it — to the client’s Change Orders folder in Drive (new 2026-10-01).** The row then shows **📁 Filed copy**. If it shows **📁 File to Drive**, the filing did not land: press it.

> **⚠** **On a fixed-price job the question is scope, never the 15% — and the change order carries its price (2026-09-25).** A flat fee does not move with the hours. If the job simply takes longer than planned *on the scope we agreed*, that is ours to absorb: **no change order, and no call to the client about it.** If the client asks for something that was **not** in the agreed scope — another room, the storage unit, a second property — raise a change order *before* you do the work, however small it is. Type the extra hours and the screen gives you the price as you type (*“+ $5,000 on the fixed project fee, which goes from $24,000 to $29,000”*). The printed change order shows that price, the client signs under it, and it is exactly what the final invoice adds — whatever the work actually takes.
>
> **On an expedited job the price carries the 20% premium (since 2026-09-30).** The added scope is expedited like the rest of the job, so the readout adds *plus the 20% expedited-delivery premium, as this job is expedited*, and the same $5,000 of hours comes to *+ $6,000*. The premium is fixed on the change order when you create it: one raised before that date, or on a fixed fee saved before it (which carries its premium inside the fee), keeps its plain-rate price. The preferred-client discount never applies to a change order.
>
> **Printed a fixed-price change order before 25 Sep? Print it again** — the old one says it creates no charge, and the invoice charges it.

It lands as its own row on that card, and the row carries its buttons:

1. **PDF** — the printable change order, on every row, before and after the client agrees. On an ordinary job: hours on the approved estimate, the scope change, revised estimated hours, and **no dollar figure on it anywhere**. On a fixed-price job: the fee in the agreement, this change order’s price with the hours and rates behind it (and the 20% premium, on an expedited job), and the revised fee. On a Home Prep job: the concierge hours at their rate, and any vendor it adds with its estimated cost and the 30% fee at that cost.
2. **Get Acceptance** — the client types their name against *✓ I Accept This Change Order.* Hand them the iPad, or record it yourself off their email or call. On a fixed-price job the screen they sign under shows the price; on a Home Prep job, the rate and any vendor it adds. **Only there while it is waiting on the client** — once accepted, the row reads *Accepted* with their name, and keeps its PDF.

> **⚠** **An unaccepted Change Order is never billed.** The final invoice counts accepted change orders and silently ignores the rest. A change order created, printed, agreed on the phone and never marked accepted is work you will do and not charge for. **Take the acceptance at the moment the client agrees.**

> **⚠** **Raised a change order before 29 Sep? The client has never accepted it, because until then there was no button to do it with.** The **PDF** and **Get Acceptance** buttons sat in a hidden row the app never showed, so the card read *Awaiting acceptance* with nothing to press. An unaccepted change order does nothing: it is not on the final invoice, its hours do not lengthen the plan or clear the overrun warnings, and on a Home Prep job it does not open your hours log or add its vendor to the plan. **Open the job, press PDF on the row, walk the client through it, and take Get Acceptance** — or record it off their email or call if they already agreed.
>
> The message after you press *Create Change Order* or *I Accept This Change Order* now shows on the dashboard, and the row appears straight away. It used to show on a screen you could not see, so pressing Create looked like nothing had happened. **If you pressed it twice because of that, print and accept only one** — a change order cannot be deleted, and the one left unaccepted moves nothing and is never billed.

> **If the client asks whether their rush premium or their discount applies to the extra hours (2026-09-29, changed 2026-09-30):** on an hourly job, **yes** — the extra hours are billed like every other hour on the job, premium and discount included, and the printed change order says so in one line. On a fixed-price job the discount, **no**, and the premium, **yes** since 2026-09-30 (Anthony): the change is priced at the hourly rates printed on it plus the 20% premium, and the page says *“It is priced at the hourly rates shown plus the 20% expedited-delivery premium, as this engagement is expedited”*, adding that the preferred-client discount does not apply where the job has one. A change order raised before that date, or on a fixed fee saved before it, stays at the plain hourly rates and says so. A job with neither prints no line.

> **The second change order starts from where the first left the job (2026-09-25).** Its screen reads *“180.0 hrs on the estimate and 1 accepted change order becomes 200.0”*, and its printed copy carries a *Change orders already accepted* line, so the revised total is the real one. It used to start from the estimate alone and print a total short by every hour the first change order had added.

> **⚠** **On a Home Prep job, extra concierge hours go on a change order, and the change order carries the rate (2026-09-25).** A prep job signed with vendors only had no way to add your time — the half-day clearing a garage before the painters arrive — so this box used to say don’t promise it. **Now you raise a change order before you do the work:** concierge hours only (the specialist box is greyed out on prep), and the screen reads it back as you type — *“+6.0 concierge hrs at $150 an hour — about $900 at the estimated hours, billed as they are worked on the final invoice, on top of the 30% Home Sale Preparation Fee”*.
>
> **Get the acceptance before you start.** Their agreement names your rate (§3.3, from 2026-09-25) and the printed change order states it again beside the hours — so print it, walk them through the hours and the rate, and take their name on *Get Acceptance*. Until they accept, nothing opens and nothing is billed.
>
> **Then the Job Plan opens an hours log**, your row only. Confirm the team in it, then **log the hands-on hours as you work them** — never the time on the vendors, which the fee covers (step 10b). The final bills what you log, at the rate, on top of the fee; log none and it bills the fee alone, and the timeline’s *Final invoice sent* step says so before you send it. A change in an existing vendor’s work still needs no change order: the fee follows what the vendors invoice. A *new* vendor does — next box.

> **⚠** **On a Home Prep job, a new vendor goes on a change order too (2026-09-30).** A trade the house turns out to need — a painter nobody quoted — had no way onto the job: the plan, the budget and the fee all read the approved estimate. Now, under the hours, pick it in **Add a prep vendor** (the Vendor Directory’s *Property Preparation* trades) and type **Its estimated cost**. A change order can carry concierge hours, a vendor, or both; it will not save with neither (*“Enter the additional concierge hours this change is expected to take, or the prep vendor it adds — or both.”*), or with a vendor and no cost. The screen reads it back — *“Adds Painting to the preparation vendors. It bills the client directly at cost, and the 30% Home Sale Preparation Fee applies to what it actually charges — about $1,350 at the estimated $4,500.”* — and the printed change order and the screen the client signs under name the vendor, its estimated cost and the fee at that cost. The card reads *adds Painting (est. $4,500)*.
>
> **Get the acceptance before you book them.** Once the client accepts, the vendor joins the prep vendors on the Job Plan — book it and type its quote there (c, above) — and the final invoice charges the 30% on that quote. Until they accept, it is not on the plan and nothing is billed. **Since 2026-09-30 a job with prep bundled into another service can do the same** — a Home Transition, a Cleanout or an estate whose estimate carries a painter or a stager: the vendor box is under the hours there too, the change order can carry hours, the vendor, or both, and the client accepts the vendor, its estimated cost and the 30% on it (on a fixed price, on top of the flat fee). The vendor joins the *Home Prep for Sale* lines on the Job Plan's sourcing list. There is no box on a job whose estimate has no prep vendor with a cost, or on a fixed price from before 2026-09-23 whose fee carries the prep fee inside it.

### e. Job Admin & Inventory — the desk tab

**This is your end-of-day tab.** The Job Plan is what you use in the house; the Job Admin & Inv tab is what you open that evening at home or in the office to work through what you shot. Rebuilt 2026-09-01 around exactly that.

The manifest assembles from three places: the shots you took on the Items pass in the house (they arrive with no name — see *Unnamed shots* below), **+ Add line item** for an asset with no photo (cash, an account, a vehicle), and a **From the Estimate Walkthrough** panel that pulls in the collections and vehicles you flagged at estimate time — as a lot or itemised. Nothing can be imported twice. Until they are brought in, the Job Plan's banner and the Appraisal Worklist say so and send you to that panel on this tab (since 2026-09-30; the banner named a tab called *Inventory*, and the worklist sent you to the Job Plan, which has no import).

> **The must-find list is above Job Admin (new 2026-09-19).** *From intake — must find · safety*: each must-find with its **Found it** tick, and the safety answer. Name the evening's shots against it — if the ring is among them, press *Found it* here. Cash, safes and the rest of the brief stay on the Job Plan.

> **Job Admin sits folded near the top of this tab (new 2026-09-19)**, under the client line, the brown Next band and the must-find list. It is the desk paperwork that used to be checkboxes on the Job Plan — the financial close, the §733.604 court list (on a trust, since 2026-10-03, the trustee's own list), the seven-year archive — with a count on the fold (*0 of 3 ticked · 3 derived items still open* on a cleanout with nothing done yet). The lines the app can answer for itself (an original will or cash found on site, first and in red while we hold it, since 2026-10-03; rooms cleared, hours logged, change orders accepted, final invoice sent, and since 2026-10-03 donation receipts, sale proceeds reconciled and, on an estate, the signed receipts for property released to a person and the Disposition Ledger signed) are read off the job and are never ticked; the rest are boxes. It is here rather than on the Client Dashboard because when you are in the middle of a job this tab is where you already are.

> **⚠** **The court list is withheld until somebody answers the matter type (new 2026-09-21).** The *Florida court & legal compliance* boxes used to appear on a Probate job and on nothing else. They now follow what was recorded about the estate, not what was sold: **the matter type** (probate · trust · both · neither) and **what we are contracted to produce**. Two lines on the Job Admin card, the last of the ones read off the job, tell you where you stand — *Matter type recorded* and *Contracted to produce*. If either reads open, fix it on **Edit Client**; the tier has to be answered before the agreement goes out anyway. **Three things follow from it, and the first two are new work you would otherwise never be asked for:** an Estate Settlement administering a probate estate now gets the whole list (it used to get none); a probate matter contracted at *None* no longer asks you to verify a value or attach an appraisal counsel is producing; and a matter recorded as a **trust** gets no court list at all — that is correct: nothing is filed with a court on a trust. Since 2026-10-03 it gets its own list instead, under *Trust administration*: the *Trust Schedule* (under *More ▾*, below) verified where we state values, the appraisals attached where they are ours or one is on the job, property the trust does not hold left off it, the schedule delivered to the successor trustee or their counsel, and our disposition records handed over for the trustee's accounting. Neither list has a sign-off box any more: the signed Disposition Ledger is the sign-off. A matter recorded *Neither* gets neither list.

> **What the Summary shows depends on what we were hired to produce (new 2026-09-22).** On an estate contracted at *Contents list* or *None* the agreement says counsel does the valuing, so the Summary **counts** rather than prices: no *Valuation Basis* control, no *Value as of*, no *Total Estimated FMV*, no *Items Awaiting Valuation*, and *Items by Category* in place of *FMV by Category*. The client’s Drive workbook matches. Everything else is untouched — the dates, the court rows, the bequest and dispute counts, the proceeds, and the MAIV line, which is a federal filing question for the estate whoever answers it. **Missing figures you expected? Check the tier on Edit Client** — an unanswered tier keeps the valuation, so this only changes once somebody has said *Contents list* or *None*.

> **The tab is called *Job Admin & Inv* now, and it follows the Job Plan (new 2026-09-20).** Same tab, same place on the bar — the name just says what was already on it. **You only pick the client once:** set the job on the Job Plan and this tab opens on the same client, and the other way round. It lasts the session; reload the page and you pick again.

> **⚠⚠ You do this on every labour job now, not just estate work (new 2026-09-21).** Home Editing, Home Transition and Home Cleanout keep the same room-by-room record as an estate job — Anthony: *“what’s going to the new home, what’s going to be donated, what’s going to be sent to the kids … the inventory is not just for the jobs that we’re doing for dead people.”* Nothing about the price changes: no living-client estimate moved by a dollar, and you are **not valuing anything** on these jobs. What you are doing is recording where each thing went and who took it.

> **⚠⚠ Start here: *Name N shots* (new 2026-09-22).** First button on the bar. It reads every unnamed shot that has reached Drive — from any day, not only today's; one still marked *not saved* waits for Retry — and fills in a name and a category on every unnamed line — that is the whole reason you shoot without typing in the house. A shelf of books comes back as one line with a quantity; a bar console holding four things comes back as four lines sharing the one photograph. It names things as fully as the picture allows, so expect *"Bang & Olufsen Beolab 8000 speakers, pair"* rather than *"speakers"*, and expect a hedge — *"appears to be"*, *"likely a reproduction"* — when it is reading the shape rather than a mark.

> **⚠ It never values anything and never decides where anything goes.** No FMV, no disposition, no NFA flag, no bequest or dispute. It says *what each thing is* and stops. If a row comes back with a value on it, something is wrong — tell Anthony.

> **⚠ Every row it names is marked unchecked, and checking them is your job.** A row reads **agent** in bronze, or **agent · unsure** where it was guessing; hover it for why it named what it named. Edit the name or the category and the badge clears. Client documents keep printing *IN PROGRESS — N of M items reviewed* until you have been through them, so nothing is hidden from the client and nothing is claimed either. **Work the unsure ones first** — that is what the badge is for.

> **⚠⚠ One Items shot per object or shelf. Shoot the same credenza twice and you get two of everything.** Anthony asked this outright: *"we take two pictures of that bar … it's not going to think it's more of the same items is it"*. It does. **Every photograph is a separate look and the agent has no memory of the last one**, so it cannot know it has already named that console. Four objects in the frame, shot twice, come back as eight lines.
>
> **The second angle is what *Detail of last* is for, and it is a different button.** Shoot the object, press *Detail of last*, shoot the mark or the back or the damage — that close-up goes to the agent *with* the object and never becomes a line. Miss the toggle and it is an ordinary Items shot, which is the same double again. **The toggle resets after every shot**, so it is one tap per close-up, every time.
>
> This is the cheapest place to get it right. Fixing it in the room is one tap; fixing it at the desk is reading two photographs and deleting a line.

> **⚠ Photograph the maker's mark and it gets read.** Use *Detail of last* on the signature, the hallmark, the stamp on the back of the china — those close-ups go to the agent **with** the object they belong to, which is the whole reason to take them. It is the difference between *"appears to be"* and a flat reading. Takes five seconds in the room and saves an argument later.

> **It also tells you things that are not about naming.** Above the rows you may see **Must find** — it checks every item shot it names against what the family asked you to find (it never sees the as-found pass, by design: read the as-found shots against the list yourself), and says when it spots the item or somewhere one would be kept, like a safe or a locked drawer. And **Firearm** or **Possible NFA item** — a suppressor looks like a plain metal tube and the real risk is nobody recognising it. **Go and look.** Now and then it raises an amber **Look at this** as well — anything else in a frame it thinks a person should see. A notice is read off a photograph, not off the house, so it is a prompt and never an all-clear — and it never ticks *Found it* for you.

> **A photograph with nothing in it comes back blank, on purpose.** A wall, an empty room, a shot that did not come out. It stays on the *Unnamed shots* count for you to deal with rather than having a line invented for it.

**Where it went is two answers, and both go on the row.** The disposition says *what happened to it*; **Channel / Recipient** says *who has it*, and it now sits on the row itself instead of three sections deep in the item panel.

- **Move** — it is going to the client’s new home. **Leave the recipient blank**; the destination is the new address already on the job, and nothing is being released to anybody.
- **Distribute** — a named person is taking it. **Put their name in Channel / Recipient.** This is the daughter, the grandson, the neighbour.
- **Donate / Sell / Consign / Auction / Junk** — put the charity, the buyer, the saleroom or the hauler in the same field.

> **⚠** **If it left the property, somebody’s name goes in Channel / Recipient. A blank there is the one gap this record exists to close.** Six months later the family asks where the dining table went, and “Donate” on its own does not answer it. The Contents Record counts the blanks for you and prints them at the top, so fill them in as you go rather than reconstructing them from memory in December.

> **It says *Distribute*, not *Gift*, and that is not fussiness.** On an estate the same button is handing a beneficiary something the will or the trust leaves them — a **distribution**, not a gift — and the two are different things to an accountant. One word has to be right on both sides, so it is this one.

**The document a living-client family gets is the Contents Record.** It is the button where the estate documents sit on a decedent job: *what stayed, what went, where each piece went and who took it*, grouped by destination and then by room. It carries **no values** — but on any line where money actually came back from a sale, a consignment or an auction it shows the **net received**, after that partner's fees, because that is a real figure and the family will ask.

> **Print it mid-job too.** It stamps *IN PROGRESS* until every line is reviewed and *REVIEWED* after, so an early copy cannot be mistaken for the final one. Printing it on a Tuesday is the fastest way to see what is still sitting in *Not yet decided*.

> **⚠** **The estate documents are not offered on a living job, and you should not go looking for them.** Until 2026-09-21 they were — and one *Donate* row on a Home Editing job was enough to produce a *Release Approval Request* addressed *“To Personal Representative”*, over a *“Fair Market Value as of date of death”* line, on a client who is alive and standing next to you. The Court Inventory, the §733.604 schedule and the date-of-death valuation are all decedent-only now. (The Disposition Ledger was too, until 2026-10-03: it is on every job now, as the close-out summary the client signs, with the proceeds net to the client on a living job.) **If you have a client copy of any of those printed before that date for a living client, bin it.**

#### How to work it

1. It opens on **Today** if you shot anything there today, and on **All** if not; press **All** and it stays on All (2026-09-30 — until then, on a day with a capture, it snapped back to Today every time the tab redrew). Each client's desk starts clean: a search, a count or a room picked on one client no longer follows you to the next. The **Needs you** counts across the top are also filters, and which ones you see depends on the job: *Unnamed shots · Not yet decided · No value yet · Lots to split · Needs appraiser · No recipient · Firearms held · Disputed / Hold* on an estate (*Lots to split* only where a Form 706 is being filed); on a living-client job there is no *No value yet*, and *Needs appraiser* reads *To a specialist*.
2. Start at the top. **Unnamed shots** is every line nobody has named yet — once *Name N shots* has run, only what it could not name: a blank frame, or a shot still waiting on Retry. Each has an empty bronze name box, with your spoken note under it and its detail shots counted beside it. Type the name in the box, correct the category in the item panel if *General/Household* is wrong, and it drops off the count. **Not yet decided** is everything you left on the Undecided chip, and that is the second worklist. Rooms inside each group run in walkthrough order, so you are retracing your own day.
3. Put the value in on the row and pick the disposition on the row. When you change a disposition the item **moves** to that group — that is working, not a glitch.
4. Anything you need the rest of the record for — condition, flags, custody, appraiser, proceeds — press the **▾** and the item opens. On a firearm it also says whether it may be carried, and holds the dealer route for one going to a named person (Step 10f). **Remove line** is there too (2026-09-30): it takes one line off — photographed, split or typed — and never the photograph, which another line may share; it asks first, and *Removed items* at the foot of the tab gives the line back. Until then only a typed line or an agent's possible duplicate could be removed at the desk.
5. For the repetitive stuff, **tick the boxes down the left** and a bar comes up from the bottom: set category, disposition, valuation source (on an estate: a living job's bar does not offer it since 2026-09-30, because the field is not on its record) or room across the whole selection at once, name who took them (**Set recipient**), **Mark reviewed**, or print an **Approval request** for just those lines and **Record approval** when it comes back signed. Forty household items to Junk is one action, not forty.
6. Tick **Reviewed** on each line as you finish it. The bar at the top tracks you.
7. **Work *Needs appraiser* before you print a release request.** That filter is everything flagged for a specialist — by your ⚑ or, on an estate, by its category and value — and on an estate a line on it with no appraiser linked and no waiver prints *NOT YET APPRAISED* on the request. An unvalued line on a signed release is property that left the estate with no independent record of what it was worth. A living client's request prints no valuation notice at all (until 2026-09-30 it could print the estate's *not yet valued … reported under oath*).

> **Reviewing does not lock anything and does not unlock anything.** You can print the client's schedule at any point in a job — we show clients work in progress on purpose. Until every line is ticked the document prints with an **IN PROGRESS — 18 of 42 items reviewed** stamp and a line saying unreviewed values may still change. Don't apologise for it; that stamp is the honest thing on the page.

> **The As-Found Record, under *More ▾* (new 2026-09-20).** A room-by-room index of every as-found photograph on the job — the room, the date, the time it was taken and a link to the file. **Print it and read it before you hand the estate back.** Until now the as-found pass reached no document at all: the shots were in Drive and the representative had no way to find one. This is what answers *"there was a gold Rolex in my father's desk drawer"* six months from now.
>
> **Read the red block at the top first — it is the point of the page.** Any room in scope with no as-found photograph on it is named there, in red, over the sentence *"This record is not a complete account of the property."* If a room is named and you know it *was* shot, the photographs did not save: go back to that room on the Job Plan and press Retry. If it genuinely was not shot, the record says so and will keep saying so — there is no tidying that away.
>
> It also names any room that was photographed but never **confirmed complete**, which is the tick you press in the room. That line is saying the drawers and closets in it may not all have been opened. Same index is a tab in the workbook the representative gets, so it is one spreadsheet and not a second file to explain.

> **⚠ Do not use the As-Found Record as an inventory, and do not let anyone else.** Nothing on it is valued, dispositioned or proposed for release, and it says so on its own face. It is a record of the state of the house on the morning you walked in — that is all, and that is what makes it worth having. The schedule of what the estate owns is the **Estate Inventory PDF**.

> **⚠⚠ One photo, several items — you do not have to shoot each object (new 2026-09-20).** Anthony's own words about a bar console: *"this same picture is going to serve as reference to multiple items — four bottles of booze, a Banksy, a hutch."* Shoot the shelf once in the house. At the desk, open that line and press **➕ Another item in this photo** for each additional object; each becomes its own line with its own number, name, value and disposition, all sharing the one picture. The row says *1 of 4 in this photo* so you can see at a glance why four rows look the same. The new line appears in place under the photograph's other lines, without the tab redrawing around you (2026-09-30).
>
> **Shoot a second photograph only when the object earns one** — something valuable enough to want front and back, a signature to zoom in on, a maker's mark on the underside of a plate. That is what **Detail of last** is for in the house, and it is a different thing: a detail is another picture of ONE object and never becomes a line of its own.

> **⚠⚠ *Possible duplicates* — the desk's catch for the credenza shot twice (new 2026-09-22).** Two agent-named lines in the same room with the same name, **off different photographs**, are flagged in an amber block above the rows with both pictures side by side, and each row wears a bronze *possible duplicate* chip so you see it while scrolling too. **Open both photographs before you press anything.**

| Press | What happens |
| --- | --- |
| **Remove** (on the line you do not want) | That line comes off the inventory. **The photograph stays in Google Drive** — only the line goes — and *Removed items* at the foot of the tab gives it back whole if you were wrong |
| **Not duplicates** | They really are two objects. Clears the pair and stops flagging them |

> Correcting either name, or ticking the row reviewed, settles it on its own — the flag is only ever asking about a name nobody has looked at yet.

> **⚠ Two matching nightstands are not a duplicate, and the app will never guess which you have.** It flags and it never merges, on purpose: *Nightstand* twice in one bedroom is either one nightstand shot twice or a real pair, and only somebody who can see both photographs can say. Merging automatically would quietly delete a real object off a schedule a court may read. **And two lines split out of ONE picture are never flagged** — that is the *1 of 2 in this photo* case and it is correct; the block only ever pairs lines that came off different frames.

> **⚠ Deleting the photograph deletes every line you split out of it.** The 🗑 is on the photograph in the room, on the Job Plan, and its question names the lines that go with it — read it. If you split a shelf into six objects and then delete the picture, all six go and the picture goes to the client's Drive bin. **Restore** under *Removed items* on this tab brings the six lines back (and, since P22, any detail shots the bin took with the item; press **Show all** there if the line is older than the twelve most recent), but not the picture: each reads *photo in the Drive bin*, and the photograph has to be restored from Google Drive's own bin, which keeps it for 30 days. It is easier not to. And a shot that has not finished uploading cannot be split at all: press Retry on it first, or the lines would have no picture and never get one.

> **Where the columns went.** This tab used to be a 29-column spreadsheet with four buttons above it that swapped which columns you could see. Both are gone. Every one of those fields is still there — in the item panel, in the CSV and in the client's workbook.

> **⚠** **The release approval request now tells the representative when a line has not been valued — and before 2026-09-11 it did not.** An object the app had itself flagged for a specialist, with no value on record, printed with *Auction* proposed and an initial box beside it and the document said nothing about it. It is badged *NOT YET APPRAISED* now, named with its item number in a notice above the table.
>
> **It does not stop you, and it should not.** Selling before a formal appraisal is often the right call — a dealer's offer may be the market test, and not everything warrants a formal valuation. The representative decides. Your job is to have asked the question first, and to be able to answer it when they ask what the piece was worth.
>
> **⚠ Any release approval request you printed before 2026-09-11 is worth re-reading against the manifest.**

- **Value it against something.** Put the number in the row, then set **Valuation Source** and write what you used in **Valuation Basis / Comps** in the item panel — which app, which comparables, what the range was. A line with a value and no source prints **not stated** in red on the client's schedule, because we promise on our own website that the source is stated.
- **Appraisers** — build the roster per estate: name, firm, credential (ISA / ASA / AAA / USPAP / GIA), independence, the effective and report dates, and **Due back** — the turnaround we promise to tell the client. An item valued by appraisal links to a roster appraiser instead of free text. **Who arranges the appraisals follows the contract (since 2026-09-30):** at *Inventory + appraisals*, Havellin arranges every one; on any other tier, only the appraisers on the approved estimate (its Exhibit A) are ours to schedule, and the estate attorney — or, on a trust, the trustee or their counsel — arranges any other; with none on the estimate, counsel arranges them all. If counsel wants us to arrange one the estimate does not list, that is a change to what was signed, and it goes to Anthony.

  > **⚠** **An appraiser who also BUYS is not independent, and the app now says so.** Pick a firm from the directory dropdown and, if the directory lists it as a buyer, an auction house or an estate sale company, the option reads *⚠ also Jewelry & Watch Buyer* and the *Independent* tick comes off by itself with the reason printed beside it. A firm that might end up buying the property, or earning a commission on what it sells for, cannot give a defensible opinion of its value — that is the entire point of the word. You can tick it back, and sometimes you should: a family that wants the jeweller who has known them thirty years to look at the jewellery is making their own decision. But do it deliberately. **If you linked an appraiser from the directory before 9 September 2026, open the roster and check that row** — the old picker hid the buying trade and left *Independent* ticked.
- **The $3,000 guardrail** ($500 on a disputed or contested estate) — flagged items with no appraiser attached raise an amber nudge on a Standard job and a **red block on a Formal one**. Per-item **Waive** logs a reason. On a Formal job the Court Inventory and the Trust Schedule print *DRAFT* until every flagged item is appraised or waived, and the DRAFT line names who arranges the outstanding appraisals, by the rule under *Appraisers* above.
- **Needs Appraisal** (new 2026-09-11) — the tick box in the item panel's Valuation section, and the desk half of the **⚑ Appraise** toggle you press in the house. Tick it for anything the category cannot see; untick it to take a line back off the worklist. *Untick is not the same as Waive* — waiving records that the requirement applied and somebody decided against it, with a reason that prints. And if the item routes automatically on its category, unticking does nothing: link an appraiser or waive it.
- **MAIV §20.2031-6** and **MAIV Class** — whether an article counts toward the estate's *marked artistic or intrinsic value* total. It fires by itself on art, antiques, jewellery, silver, rugs, collectibles, firearms, wine and instruments; leave it on *Auto* unless you have a reason. Set it to **No** for something that is plainly ordinary in a fancy category — a $40 mass-produced print in Art & Décor. Set it to **Yes** for a **fur coat** or a **rare book library**, which the regulation names and the app has no category for, then pick the class.
- **Valuation basis and date** — Fair Market Value by default, valued as of the date of death. If the estate is taxable and counsel elects the §2032 alternate date (six months after death), toggle it and the schedule follows.
- **Asset track** — Probate / Trust / Non-probate / Homestead / Exempt. Probate and Exempt reach the **Court Inventory**; Trust reaches the **Trust Schedule**. **The default follows the matter type** — *Trust* on a trust matter, *Probate* on everything else — so on a trust job you no longer set it line by line. Non-probate and Homestead are on neither list on purpose; each document names what it left out.
- **Chain of custody** per item, in the item panel (▾) — released / received / transferred / returned, with party, date, method and receipt. Mandatory on Formal, on any probate job and, since 2026-10-03, on any trust administration.
- **Snapshot** — a labelled point-in-time copy of the manifest. This is your amended-inventory trail; print any snapshot as an as-of schedule. **You cannot delete one (since 2026-10-03).** A snapshot taken in error gets **Void**: say why, and it stays on the list marked *VOIDED* with your reason, and still prints. **Compare with now** or **Compare with previous** prints what changed, item number by item number: added, removed, renamed, revalued, a new disposition or track.

**What you hand out.** Three buttons sit on the header; everything else is under **More ▾**.

- **Contents List** — *on an estate contracted at **Contents list**, this is the button in the primary slot and the Estate Inventory PDF is not offered.* A photo, the description, the quantity and the condition for every item, **room by room in the order you walked the house**, and **no values at all**. That is the document the agreement promises counsel on that tier: they value it, they file it.
- **Estate Inventory PDF** — the client and attorney document. A photo, description, room, quantity, condition, date-of-death value and the valuation source on every line, grouped by disposition. Homestead, exempt and non-probate property print in their own schedule at the end, plus the appraisal flag list showing who is engaged on what and when it is due back.
- **CSV** — every column as a spreadsheet file, for attaching to an email to an executor or attorney.
- **Approval Request** — see the red block below. This is the one that matters.

Under **More**: the *As-Found Record* (above), *Court Inventory* (the §733.604-style schedule counsel files — Havellin does not file it; since 2026-10-03 one named adoption line per personal representative once a co-representative is recorded), *Trust Schedule* (the Chapter 736 one a successor trustee gets), *Appraisal Worklist* (a per-specialist packet to hand each appraiser), *Snapshot*, and **Share w/ Counsel**, which grants the attorney or trust officer read-only access by name — never a public link — to both photograph folders, *Estate Inventory* and *As-Found Record* (one folder on a job started before 2026-09-20). **Revoke** takes both back. Financial folders stay private either way. *Show summary & rollups* opens the totals panel at the bottom of the tab. On a living-client job the list is shorter: As-Found Record, Appraisal Worklist, *Share photographs*, Revoke and the summary. The *Disposition Ledger* is no longer here (it was, on estates, until 2026-10-03): it has its own card under the inventory, on every job, below.

**The close-out records, under the inventory (new 2026-10-03).** Below the manifest and above the snapshots, on every job but Home Prep, once the inventory has a line:

- **Disposition Ledger** — where each item went and what it brought, line by line under its item number, with the proceeds net to whoever they belong to: the estate, the trust, or the client on a living job. It is **the close-out summary the client signs**. **Print** it, have the representative sign it (the trustee on a trust, the client on a living job), scan or photograph the signed page and press **File signed copy**. **File to Drive** files the page itself at any time; closing the job does it for you. What is still missing is listed above the table — a line with no disposition, an item that left with no recipient, a sale with nothing received, a statement that does not agree — and **while any line has no disposition there is no signature block at all**: decide every line first.
- **Proceeds statements** (cleanouts and estates) — when an auction house, a consignment shop or an estate sale company settles, press **+ Record a statement**, tick the lines it covers and type its gross, fees and net paid exactly as printed. The app checks it against what those lines record, to the cent, and where they differ it says by how much, with both figures: fix whichever is wrong (the line’s *Gross Proceeds* and *Fees*, or the statement). Then **File the statement** on its row. Recorded the wrong one? **Void** it, with the reason; it is never deleted.
- **Donations** — one row per charity. **Print Donation Record** gives the charity and the donor a list of what went; when the charity’s receipt comes back, press **File charity receipt** on its row. **Every donated line needs its receipt now.** Lines with no charity named are listed apart: name it in *Channel / Recipient* first.

> **⚠** **A tick box will not clear these any more.** *Sale proceeds reconciled*, *Donation receipts on file* and, on an estate, *Disposition Ledger signed by the Personal Representative* (the trustee on a trust) are lines the app reads off those records — on the Job Admin card, and the last two on the Job Plan too. They go green when every statement agrees and is filed and every sold line is on one, when every donated line is receipted, and when a signed ledger is filed. On a living client's job with an inventory the client signs the ledger, and since P22 *Disposition Ledger signed by the client* asks for that copy in the same places. Until 2026-10-03 they were boxes, and the donation one passed on a single receipt.

> **⚠** **⚠ A TRUST MATTER GETS THE *TRUST SCHEDULE*, AND YOU MUST NEVER CALL IT AN ACCOUNTING.** It is the Chapter 736 version of the Court Inventory: the same table, the same DRAFT rules, and the property held by the trust is what is *on* it. The trustee **receives** it for the trust's records — they do not adopt it and they do not file it anywhere. The document says on its own face that it **supports** the accounting the trustee owes under §736.08135 and **is not one**, and it lists what an accounting has that this does not: receipts, disbursements, gains and losses on sale, and who the beneficiaries are. **If a trustee or their lawyer asks Havellin to prepare the accounting, the answer is no and it goes to Anthony.** That is fiduciary work and we are not the fiduciary. **Since 2026-10-03 it names the trust** under the decedent (*The &lt;name&gt;, dated &lt;date&gt;*, from Edit Client), and once a co-trustee is recorded each trustee gets a signature line of their own, named.

> **⚠** **⚠ Non-probate and Homestead property is on NEITHER schedule, and that is right.** The two lists do not add up to everything and were never meant to. A joint account's contents and a payable-on-death item go straight to the named person; homestead passes to the heirs. Each document names what it left off so nobody reads one as covering the rest. **Do not move something to Trust just to make it appear somewhere.**

> **⚠** **Three documents are not on the strip on every job, and that is the contract rather than a fault.** The **Estate Inventory PDF**, the **Court Inventory** and the **Trust Schedule** all state a value on every line — a carrying value is still a value — so they appear only where the engagement says we state values. At *Contents list* you hand over the **Contents List**; at *None* counsel does the whole inventory and the document we issue is the **Approval Request** — which is why it is the primary button there (drawn once; until 2026-09-30 a second copy sat beside it). A line under the buttons says which it is. **Do not go hunting for a missing button and do not send a document from another job.** If you think the tier is wrong, fix it on *Edit Client*; if the client wants a valued inventory, that is a change to what was signed and it goes to Anthony.

> **⚠** **Check the tier before you tell an attorney what they are getting.** On a *Contents list* engagement we photograph, describe, locate and condition every item and **we do not value anything** — counsel does, and counsel files. The app prints that on the face of the document, so do not contradict it on the phone. If an attorney asks you for values on that job, the answer is *"that is in your office’s hands under the agreement — I can give you the photographed list today"*, and then tell Anthony, because moving to a valued inventory is a change to what was signed.

> **⚠** **Sending the estate attorney the inventory is one button: 📧 Send probate package, at the foot of the Probate card on the Client Dashboard (new 2026-10-01).** It makes a Gmail draft to the attorney, copying the representative and agreements@, with the Court Inventory (and the Trust Schedule on a pour-over will), the Estate Inventory PDF, the Disposition Ledger (since 2026-10-03) and the Appraisal Worklist attached (on a *Contents list* engagement, the Contents List in place of the first two), and links to the appraisal reports, both photograph folders and a record of the signed release approvals and custody events. It shares both photograph folders with the attorney as it goes. Send it whenever counsel needs a copy: an unfinished document goes out marked as unfinished, never as final. Read the draft, send it from Gmail, then press **✓ I’ve sent it** on the same row. If the row says the inventory changed since the draft, delete that draft and send a fresh one. With no attorney email on the client it refuses and says so — add it with Edit Client. (Until 2026-10-01 *Send Package* only ticked a box; nothing was sent.) **On a trust-only matter it is 📧 Send trust package, at the foot of the *Trust Information* card — the same card, renamed (new 2026-10-02, P18).** The same package, with the Trust Schedule where the Court Inventory would be (it never carries a Court Inventory), goes to the trustee's attorney, copying the trustee and agreements@ — or, when no attorney's email is on the client, to the trustee, copying agreements@ — and shares both photograph folders with whoever it goes to. It says nothing about a court or a filing, and the card has no case number or court deadline, because a trust matter has none. (Since 2026-10-03, P19, it does carry the trust's own paper, the **Certification of Trust**, with its red chip until it is received, the trust's details, the property sale and any co-trustees.) With neither email on the client it refuses and says where each is entered. A matter recorded *Neither* has no card and no package: send its documents one at a time from the desk.

> **⚠** **Nothing of value leaves the property without a signed approval request. Verbal approval is never accepted — not from the executor, not from a beneficiary, not on the phone.** Press **Approval Request** and the app builds the itemised list: photo, reference number, room, value, proposed disposition, and a box for them to initial each line. Only lines leaving the property without an approval from every fiduciary are on it — Keep, Move and Hold are left off (nothing is leaving), and so is anything not yet decided. Send it, get it back signed, then select those items and press **Record approval**, which stamps who signed and when across all of them at once (since 2026-10-03, P19, a dialog with a tick for each fiduciary and the date: below). Do not type approvals in row by row; that is how a signed approval ends up recorded against three items out of twenty.

> **⚠** **If a line on that request is badged *SPECIFIC BEQUEST* or *DISPUTED*, do not chase the signature on it.** A specific bequest is property the will or the trust designates for a named person, and every estate estimate we issue says we set those aside. A dispute means beneficiaries disagree and we hold. Both can still properly be released — but that is the representative's call **on counsel's advice**, and it is not yours to argue either way. The request names those lines above the table and says so; let them ask counsel. If they want it off the request instead, set the item's disposition to **Keep** or **Hold** and it drops off by definition. Since 2026-10-03 (P19) a line matched to a bequest on the desk's list is badged *BEQUEST TO* and the person's name instead, whenever it is proposed to go anywhere but to them, and the same holds.

> **Catch it earlier at the bulk bar.** When you set a disposition across a selection, the confirmation now names any flagged items it swept in — "2 of them are flagged specific bequest — #2 Sargent portrait, #4 locket." That is the moment to fix it, not when the representative is holding the paper. Since 2026-10-03 (P19) a line matched to a bequest on the desk's list is named with its person: "1 of them is flagged bequest going elsewhere — #1 Sargent portrait (bequest to Mary Smith; proposed: Auction)."

> **⚠** **Every fiduciary signs a release (new 2026-10-03, P19).** Where the client records more than one representative — co-executors, co-trustees — the release approval request goes to all of them by name, prints a signature line for each and says every one must sign. **Record approval** now opens a dialog: tick each one who signed and enter the date on the paper. **Since 2026-10-05 (P20) it records whoever has signed so far**: one co-trustee signing a week before the other is fine. Tick the one who signed, and before you press it the dialog tells you who has not signed yet and that those lines stay on the next request until they do; when the other's signature comes back, select the same lines and record it the same way. Each keeps their own date, and each tick says who has already signed the lines you selected. (Until 2026-10-05 it refused until everyone was ticked, so the first signature could only be typed into the item's record.) A line only some of them have signed is not approved: its row reads *approval incomplete* (hover it for who), it comes back on the next request saying who has signed so far and when, a firearm on it stays held, and the Job Plan's release-authority line names who is still to sign. A co-trustee recorded on the client after an approval does the same to every line approved before them, and a line that has already left is ratified (next). Once it is recorded, press **File the signed request** in the same dialog and choose the PDF or a photograph of the signed pages: it is filed to the client's Drive against the lines it covers.

> **⚠** **Something that left before every fiduciary signed is ratified, never undone (new 2026-10-05, P20).** A line that has already gone — a date of disposition, a *Released* custody event or a filed receipt — while not every fiduciary has signed for it (usually a co-trustee added to the client after the hand-over) reads *ratification owed* on its row, and the next **Approval Request** lists it apart, after the lines asked for, under *Already released: for ratification*, for the missing signature: the signature ratifies the release, and nothing is undone. Nobody goes to get the item back. Send the request, get it signed, and record the signature with **Record approval** like any other; once everyone has signed, the line drops off. The Job Plan's release-authority line counts these apart from the lines still waiting on approval.

> **⚠** **Put the list of designated items on the desk (new 2026-10-03, P19).** On an estate the desk has a **Beneficiaries & specific bequests** card under the review bar. When the representative or their attorney gives you the beneficiaries and the list of designated items, enter them there: each beneficiary with their phone and email, and each bequest in *their words* — never yours, and never from the will — with the person it names and the item numbers it covers (**Match** adds a line later). Matching a line marks it a specific bequest. The card then names three things: a line flagged as a bequest that is not on their list, a bequest with no line found yet (*not yet found*), and a matched line proposed to go anywhere but to its person. That last is badged *BEQUEST TO* and the name on the approval request, and its row reads *⚠ bequest to … · going elsewhere*. Nothing is refused: a designated item can properly be sold, and that is the representative's call on counsel's advice. Usually it is a mis-sort, and you fix the disposition. When you type who is taking a line, the beneficiaries are offered as you type; you can still type anyone.

> **⚠** **A signed receipt for everything released to a person (new 2026-10-03, P19).** The estate agreement and the estimate both promise one, and until 2026-10-03 the app had no receipt to print. On an estate the **Releases & signed papers** card on the desk lists each person receiving property. A line going *To a person* owes a receipt once every fiduciary has approved it, or once it is recorded as gone. Press **Print receipt** for that person; they sign it when they take the items and you sign as witness; then press **File signed receipt** and choose a PDF or a photograph of the signed page. Only a filed receipt counts, and only for the person who signed it: a note typed in *Receipt / Doc* is shown as a typed reference and does not clear the line, and a line you re-route to somebody else needs their receipt. A receipt someone signed stays on the card after their lines are re-routed or removed, under their name with *No line on the inventory goes to them now* (since P22; until then it dropped off the card, though it stayed in Drive). Two spellings that differ only in capitals or spaces are one person. A line released with none filed reads *no signed receipt* on its row and in the *No signed receipt* chip, and Job Admin counts them (*Signed receipt for every item released to a person*, N of M). Nothing refuses a release for it: the flag is there so you close the gap. A living client's job has no receipt.

> **⚠** **Havellin's people never buy or receive estate property (new 2026-10-03, P19).** It was the firearms rule; since 2026-10-03 it is the rule for everything in an estate (Anthony: anything else is a conflict of interest). No concierge, crew member or manager buys estate property or is given any of it, at any price or as a favour. On an estate the app refuses a sale (*Auction*, *Consign*, *Sell*) or a release *To a person* whose recipient names one of us — on the row, in the item's record and on the bulk bar — says why, and changes nothing. Every estate approval request now says it in print. If a family offers you something, the answer is no, kindly. **On a living client's job it is flagged, never refused (since 2026-10-05, P20):** the client's own things are theirs to sell or give to whom they like, but the conflict is ours, so it goes on the record. The row reads *⚠ Ashley Jerome works with Havellin*, the bulk bar names it when you set it, and the approval request names the line and the person above the table, so the client signs knowing. **An estate line already going to one of us** (written before the rule, or naming someone who joined since) is flagged the same way since P22, and its approval request asks the representative not to initial it: change where it goes.

> **The signed papers are on the desk too (new 2026-10-03, P19).** **Releases & signed papers** lists every signing act on the job (since 2026-10-05, P20, each day someone signed is its own row, and the copy filed for one co-trustee's signature stays listed after the other's completes the lines), with **File signed copy** until its signed request is in Drive; the receipts; and, where the engagement states values, the *Court Inventory once the representative has adopted it* on a probate matter (**File the adopted copy**) and the *Trust Schedule once the trustee has signed it as received* on a trust (**File the received copy**). Each copy filed is listed as a link with who signed and when. Filed one against the wrong paper? Press *Void* on it, say why, and file the right one; the voided copy stays in Drive.

> Everything in the inventory is documentation support, not a legal or appraisal opinion. The estate attorney and a credentialed appraiser remain the authority.

> **There are two $3,000 tests and they are not the same test.** The *per-item* one is the ⚑ flag — $3,000, or $500 on a disputed or contested estate: is this object worth enough to send to a specialist. The *aggregate* one asks whether the estate's art, jewellery, silver, antiques, rugs and collections **added together** come to more than $3,000 — and if the estate files a federal estate tax return, that means an expert appraisal under oath has to be filed with it.
>
> **An empty Appraisal Worklist does not mean nothing needs appraising.** Thirty $500 pieces of silver: nothing is flagged, because nothing is near $3,000 on its own — and the estate owes an appraisal on $15,000 of silverware. The worklist now prints the aggregate in its own box whether or not anything was flagged. **Read that box before you tell anyone the estate is clear.**

> **Value the MAIV articles before you call the total done.** While any of them has a blank value the app will say "at least $X — cannot be tested yet" rather than giving you a verdict, and it is right to. A partial total that happens to land under $3,000 is not an estate under $3,000.

> **⚠** **Your inventory is no longer only on your own phone (2026-08-24).** Until this change, every valuation, disposition, custody entry and flag lived in one browser and nothing ever read it back — so you and Ashley could each hold a different inventory for the same estate, and whichever saved last quietly overwrote the other. Clearing your browser data destroyed the record outright: the photographs were safe in Drive, but what they were, what they were worth and where they went was gone. It now saves to the shared sheet and merges item by item, so two people working the same job keep both sets of edits.

> **⚠** **⚠ One thing that merge got wrong until 2026-09-11, and it is the chain of custody.** Within an item the newer record won *whole* — so if you logged a release on your phone and Ashley corrected that item's value on her laptop without having synced, her record was newer and **your custody entry was deleted**. Silently, on a court record. Custody events now merge as a union: everyone's entries survive, and they are listed in the order the events happened rather than the order they synced. **If you worked an estate from two devices before that date, open the custody logs and check nothing is missing.**

> **Removing a custody event marks it removed rather than erasing it** — otherwise the next sync from the other device simply puts it back. It disappears from the list and stops counting, and the removal holds everywhere.

**What that means in practice.** Open a job on any device and it pulls whatever anyone else has entered. You do not have to be the person who took the photos to see the valuations. If you remove a manual line item it stays removed everywhere — and its item number is never handed to anything else, so a gap in the numbering is the record that something was taken out.

> **Photos on the inventory rows.** Every line shows a thumbnail of the item, pulled back from Drive. If you see a little category symbol instead — a box, a ring, a chair — that photo has not come down to this device yet. It is not lost: it is in the estate's Drive folder, and the client document tells you how many are missing rather than printing empty squares. If *every* photo is a symbol, the app will say why at the top of the tab — usually the Apps Script needs redeploying, which is Anthony's job, not yours.

> If a message flashes up reading *Inventory not saved — still on this device only … will retry*, that save did not reach the sheet — usually no connection. It is held on this device and sent again by itself while the app stays open, and the red *N unsaved changes — retrying…* chip bottom-left stays until it lands. Until then nobody else can see it, and clearing your browser would lose it: don't clear site data while that chip is showing.

### f. Firearms — and what NFA actually means

Firearms come up more often than people expect on Palm Beach estates, and the rule for a crew is short: **nobody on a Havellin crew touches one. Ever.** Not to move it out of the way, not to make a room safe to work in, not to put it somewhere sensible. If a firearm is in the way, the room waits. **One named person moves firearms and it is Anthony** — not Ashley, not a specialist, not a contractor, however sensible it looks at the time.

> **⚠** **What to do, in order.** Photograph it where it lies, before anything in that room is moved — the whole object, both sides, and a legible close-up of the **serial number**. Leave it exactly where it is and secure the room or the container. Tell your Transition Concierge the same day — not at the end of the week. The TC notifies the representative and the estate attorney in writing within 24 hours. Nothing else happens until the representative has authorised the transfer *to a named licensed dealer*, in writing.

> **The full procedure is one tap away, in the app.** On the Job Plan, the red firearms banner carries **Read the firearms protocol →** (on the client dashboard it is on the red *Firearms* row of the standing-flags panel); the transport block on the Appraisal Worklist carries the same link. It opens in a new tab, works on a phone with a poor signal, and needs no sign-in — which is the whole reason it is there rather than in Drive. It runs the six stages from the intake call to closing the record, the NFA recognition check, the seven nevers and the kit. **It is still a draft for counsel**, so treat the operative wording as provisional and the practice as firm.

### Getting them to the dealer — what changed on 2026-09-13

The old rule in this guide said *"Havellin does not transport firearms and does not ride along with them"*, full stop. **That has changed, and it has changed in one direction only.** Where the representative authorises it *in writing, naming each firearm by serial and naming the receiving dealer*, Anthony may drive an ordinary firearm straight to that dealer for purchase or consignment. An **NFA item is never carried by anybody at Havellin**, under any authority, and that has not moved an inch.

Title stays with the estate the whole way. We are a courier holding for the representative, delivering to a licensed dealer, paid for our hours — that is what makes it work.

**Going to a family member — through a dealer (new 2026-09-30).** A firearm the representative directs to a named person — a son, a daughter, a named heir — still goes to a licensed dealer, and the dealer makes the transfer to that person, with the background check and the paperwork. We never hand it over. In the app the line keeps *To a person* (**Distribute**) with the person in **Channel / Recipient**; open it (▾), and its *Firearm* block has **Through a licensed dealer…** — press it and name the dealer. The record keeps who recorded the route and when, the row reads *via* that dealer, the approval request prints the route on the line, and the worklist names it on the cleared line. The rest is the same as any firearm: written authority naming it by serial and the dealer, the serial on the record, Anthony alone in the car. **Take the route off** undoes it. An **NFA** item is offered no route: the dealer collects it, whoever it is going to. Until this date nothing could clear a firearm marked *Distribute*, so one left to a family member could never be driven to the dealer at all.

> **⚠** **The two things that would actually get us in trouble, and neither of them is the drive.** **Never buy an estate firearm** — not once, not at fair value, not as a favour to a family that wants it gone. **Never take a percentage of firearm proceeds** — bill the hours like everything else. Buying and reselling, or earning on the sale, is the exact fact pattern that turns a concierge into an unlicensed dealer. If anybody ever proposes "we'll just take a cut on the guns," the answer is no. **Since 2026-10-03 (P19) the first holds for everything in an estate, not firearms alone**: Havellin's people never buy or receive estate property (step 10e).

**Before the car moves**, on every trip: locked hard case in the trunk or cargo area, never on your person; ammunition in a separate locked container and ideally not on the same trip; every item verified unloaded with the action open and a chamber flag in it; direct route, no errands, no showings, no lunch, no school pickup; the vehicle never left unattended; and never across a state line. At the counter you get an **itemised receipt, serial by serial**, signed and dated, in your hand before you walk out, and you photograph it there rather than in the car.

**What the app will and won't let you do.** Each firearm has to pass five checks before it reads as cleared to carry, and the Appraisal Worklist prints the first one it fails:

| It says | What to do |
| --- | --- |
| *NFA item — never transported by Havellin* | Nothing. This one does not clear and is not meant to. The dealer collects it from the property. |
| *Going to a named person — never handed over directly* | Record its dealer route: open the line (▾) and press **Through a licensed dealer…** in its *Firearm* block, naming the dealer (above; since 2026-09-30). No written authority clears this one. A family member receives a firearm through a licensed dealer, who does the background check and the paperwork — never from us. Until 2026-09-30 nothing you could record cleared it while the line read **Distribute**. |
| *No written authority on file* | Record **Authorized By** and **Approval Date** in the item panel (▾) — or select the lines and press **Record approval** on the bulk bar. Both fields — half an authority is not an authority. |
| *Written authority is not complete. … has not approved it, and every co-trustee must* (new 2026-10-03) | The client records more than one representative and the approval does not name them all — or one was added to the client after it was signed. Get the missing signature, then select the line and press **Record approval**, ticking whoever signed (since 2026-10-05 one at a time is fine: each signature is kept, and this reason clears once all are in). Half the fiduciaries is not an authority either. |
| *No serial number recorded* | Fill in **Serial Number**. The authorisation names each gun by serial, so an item without one was never named in the document that makes carrying it lawful. |
| *No dealer named* | Put the receiving dealer in **Channel / Recipient**. On a firearm going to a named person the dealer is on its route, and Channel / Recipient keeps the person. |

> **Serial numbers are new (2026-09-13) and they are the identity of the object, not our item number.** The dealer's receipt, an insurance claim and any police report are all written serial by serial; our *Item #* means nothing to any of them. Photograph the serial in the house — once the gun has left the property nobody can go back and read it. The field is in every item's panel (▾), not just a firearm's, so use it for a vehicle's VIN and for watches and instruments too.

### NFA items — the ones that don't look like guns

"NFA" is the National Firearms Act. It covers a short list of items that the federal government tracks individually, by serial number, on a registry — and they move on a completely different path from an ordinary shotgun. **It is also the one thing on a firearms job the app will not let anybody override.** (A firearm going to a named person was the other until 2026-09-30; it clears now, once its dealer route is recorded.)

**What you are looking for:**

- **Suppressors** (also called silencers or cans) — a metal tube, usually six to ten inches, often with threading at one end, sometimes loose in a sock or a padded case. *It looks like plumbing.* This is the one people miss. Any serial number on a bare tube means NFA.
- **Short-barrelled rifles and shotguns** — a rifle with a barrel under 16 inches or a shotgun under 18. Reads as "a small gun" unless you are measuring.
- **Machine guns** — a selector with any position beyond safe and semi-automatic. Rare, but they exist in collections here.
- **Any Other Weapon** — pen guns, cane guns, disguised firearms. Rare, and they turn up in exactly the kind of collection we work in.
- **A tax stamp** — a sheet of paper with an ATF seal, often framed on the wall or folded in the safe beside the item. If there is a stamp, there is an NFA item.
- **Anything in a gun safe you cannot identify.** Flag it and let the dealer say what it is.

> **⚠** **The risk with an NFA item is not that you mishandle it. It is that nobody recognises it as a firearm in the first place.** A suppressor sitting in a drawer gets logged as a metal tube, or as nothing — and then every gate the app has stays shut and **it rides to the dealer in the trunk anyway**, which is the actual disaster. If you find something in a gun safe, a gun case or a locked box and you are not certain what it is — photograph it, do not touch it, flag it as a firearm and tick NFA. Being wrong that way costs the desk a phone call. Being wrong the other way is a federal offence for somebody, and *26 U.S.C. § 5861* carries up to ten years.

**In the app:** set the item's category to *Firearms* and tick **NFA Item** in the item panel (▾). The tick is live only on a firearms line. It changes nothing about the authority rule — every firearm waits for written authority either way — and from 2026-09-13 it also **hard-blocks transport**, so nobody can decide on the day to put one in the car. It puts the item on a separate notice on the Appraisal Worklist so the desk can warn the dealer before they drive out.

Why the desk cares: not every licensed dealer is licensed for NFA items, so an unannounced one can mean a wasted collection trip. **Do not quote a timeline to a client.** This guide used to say a Form 5 transfer takes months; in 2026 it clears in about *two days* electronically. A Form 4 to a dealer is the slow one. Tell the representative the item is on a separate federal path and let the dealer give the date.

> **What you never do:** tell a family member what a gun is worth, agree to "just hold onto it," hand one to a beneficiary who asks for it — including the named heir, including one standing there asking — take one home for safekeeping, or move one between rooms. If a beneficiary presses, the line is: *"I'm not able to handle firearms — that goes through the representative and a licensed dealer. Let me get you the right person."*

> **What we say to a client, and it is worth leading with the second half:** firearms are documented in place and dispositioned through a licensed dealer; where the representative authorises it in writing, Havellin transports non-NFA firearms directly to that dealer. **Havellin never purchases estate firearms and takes no share of firearm proceeds — our fee is our time, so the estate's interest and ours are the same one.** That is the sentence a trust officer is listening for. Do not market firearms handling as a service line; it is something we do inside an estate engagement, not something we sell. And do not use the word *licensed* anywhere near Havellin — the dealer is licensed, we are insured and bonded.
