> Havellin is not a firearms expert and this is not legal advice to a client. If anything about a firearm on a job is unclear, it stops and goes to the representative and counsel. That is always the correct answer and nobody will ever be criticised for it.

### g. Found on site — an original will, or cash (new 2026-10-03)

Two things turn up in an estate's house that Havellin must never hold, and each has a procedure Anthony set. The **Found on site** card records it: it is on the Job Plan's *In the house* stage, under the in-house boxes, on every estate job. Record it the day you find it, on the phone in the house if you can.

> **⚠** **An original will: never open it, read it, copy it or photograph its contents, and hand it to the estate attorney today, against a signed receipt.** We never keep one, not overnight and not "until the family decides".

1. Press **Record an original will**. Fill in where it was, who found it, and what it looks like *from the outside* (*"sealed white envelope marked Last Will, unopened"*). The app refuses it without the day, the finder or that description. *Handed to* is filled in with the estate attorney (or the representative if no attorney is recorded); leave its date blank while you still have the will.
2. Until it is handed over the card says so in red — *Original will held by Havellin: hand it to … today* — and the same line leads the Job Admin card at the desk.
3. Press **Print Receipt for Original Will** and take it with you. It states that we have not opened, read or copied it and keep no copy, and it has a line for the person receiving it and one for you.
4. When you hand it over, press **Record the hand-over**: the attorney and today are filled in, and any name can be typed. A day other than the day it was found is recorded and flagged on the card.
5. Then **File signed receipt**: choose the PDF, or a photo of the signed receipt. It goes to the client's *Signed Records* folder in Drive and is linked on the card.
6. Whoever holds the will has **ten days** to deposit it with the clerk of court (§732.901). The card shows the date and turns red the day after. Ask the attorney; when they confirm it, press **Confirm the deposit with the clerk** with the day and who confirmed it. Every line then reads green.

> **⚠** **Cash: two of you count it together, it goes in a sealed numbered bag, and the fiduciary signs for the bag today.**

1. Press **Record cash found**: the amount to the cent, both counters, and the number printed on the bag. The app refuses it without the amount, without **two different people** (the same name twice, however it is spelt, is one person) and without the bag number.
2. It reads red (*Cash held by Havellin*) until the hand-over is recorded. *Handed to* is filled in with the representative. Anybody else is recorded and flagged *not a fiduciary recorded on this job*, and so is a hand-over on another day.
3. Press **Print Cash Count and Receipt**: both counters sign, and the fiduciary signs for the bag with its seal intact. Then **File signed receipt** with the PDF or a photo, and the line goes green.

> A record made in error is never edited or deleted: press **Void** on it, give the reason, and record it again. It stays on the card, struck through, with the reason. A record made on the other device shows after a reload. Both receipts are drafted for counsel and not yet reviewed.

## Step 11 & 12 · Invoices — Client Dashboard

Open the client. **Each invoice has its own row on the timeline** — *Deposit invoice sent*, *Midpoint invoice sent*, *Final invoice sent* — so there is no stage to pick any more: the row you are on *is* the stage, and the lit one is the invoice due. Each sends exactly like the estimate did (**✉ Send…** → **✓ I've sent it**), and each is filed to Drive as it goes out.

| Stage | What it bills |
| --- | --- |
| **Deposit** | 50% on the estimate basis. No actuals exist yet. |
| **Midpoint** *(Second on Home Prep)* | Brings what has been collected up to 75% (a short deposit is carried into it) and trues the 30% prep fee to the *Quote $* figures recorded on the Job Plan. Labour stays on estimate until the hours are complete. **On Home Prep it is the _Second invoice_ on every screen and document** (2026-09-29): that engagement has no project midpoint, and its estimate bills the second 25% once the vendor schedule is booked. |
| **Final** | Hourly: labour trued to the logged hours (a change order's hours are logged and billed like any other) and the prep fee on the Job Plan figures. Fixed price: the flat fee with its rush and discount lines, the prep fee, and each accepted change order at the price it was signed at. Either way it bills the whole total less every payment received. |

> **The midpoint invoice waits for the halfway point (2026-09-29).** The estimate tells the client the midpoint is due at project midpoint, so the band no longer asks for it on the day you activate. Until the plan's halfway day, until half the work is done or until every room is locked, whichever comes first, the band says *Do the work — the midpoint invoice is due at the halfway point* and has **no filled button**; the send sits beside it as an outline, *✉ Send midpoint invoice — due around <date>*. Press it early only if the client asked for it. On the halfway day, or the day the last room is locked, it is the filled button again.

> **The final does not wait for the midpoint money (2026-09-29).** Once the job is closed (Step 13) the final is the band's filled button, even with the midpoint unpaid. It bills everything not yet paid, the midpoint's share included, against the payments actually received, and says on its face that the outstanding amount is carried into its balance. If the midpoint invoice never went out, do not send it after the close: the final carries it.

### What needs a PIN — and what doesn't

**Most invoices need no PIN.** Deposit and midpoint follow the approved estimate's schedule, so they print and send with no approval step (the *No approval required* badge went with the Invoices tab on 2026-09-11). Only the **final** asks for a manager PIN, and only when it lands more than **±15%** away from the estimate — the refusal names the percentage. Accepted change orders are left out of that comparison, since the client already agreed to those separately.

> **⚠** **A PIN approves one client's final at one figure (2026-09-29).** Press **🔑 Manager approval** on the client whose final it is — the approval is recorded on that client and no other, and it survives a reload. If more hours are logged, a change order is accepted or a payment comes in after the PIN, the balance moves and the final asks again, naming who approved it and at what figure. That is the app asking a manager to look at the new number, not losing the old approval. Until 2026-09-29 a PIN typed for one client released *every* client's final until the page was reloaded, and the reload forgot it. The green *Approved for Release* band you see when you view an approved final is for us: it never prints and never goes in the client's PDF.

> **⚠** **No hours logged = no final invoice.** No PIN, no PDF, no email, and no manager can unlock it. This is the one thing most likely to bite a practice run: skip the daily logging and the final simply will not issue. Go log the hours, then reprint. (Fixed-price jobs are exempt — their total doesn't come from the log. **A Home Prep job is exempt only if it quoted no declutter hours;** one that did is gated like any other, and rightly, because the final bills what is logged.) **From 2026-09-29 the timeline stops offering View and Print on that final** — both used to be there and then refuse on the press — and once the job is closed the row says *No hours are logged yet — log them on the Job Plan and the final can be priced*. Pressing **🔑 Manager approval** on it says the same thing the send does (since 2026-09-30).

### Things you'll see on an invoice

- **A rush job carries its 20% expedited-delivery line on all three stages.** If you invoiced a rush job before August 2026, re-print its midpoint and final and check the totals against what the client agreed — the premium used to be dropped after the deposit.
- **There is no "job ran over estimate" line, in either direction — removed 2026-09-11.** It used to say *"client was notified per T&Cs"* on any overage at all, which the app has never had a record of. An estimate is an estimate. And **an accepted change order moves the figure the variance is measured against**, so a job run properly cannot show a real overrun here. The document still prints *Original Estimate* and *Actual Havellin services total* on adjacent rows — nothing is hidden. **Nothing about what the client pays changed.**
- **A final more than ±15% off the estimate still stops for a manager PIN.** That is internal and did not go with the line above. If it fires, the question to ask is whether a change order should have been raised and accepted while the extra hours were still ahead of you.
- **A $1 credit line on a client who paid correctly is gone.** **Fixed 2026-09-11.** Where a stage payment landed on a part dollar — 25% of $25,715 is $6,428.75 — the summary printed *"Received ahead of the invoiced schedule — credited in the balance below ($1)"* over a difference of fifty cents. **The balance was always right**; only the line was wrong.
- **A final on a job closed before its midpoint invoice went out says so (2026-09-29).** Its midpoint row reads *25% midpoint — billed on this invoice*, and any gap names *the deposit invoice* alone. It used to name a midpoint invoice that never existed. A midpoint counts as invoiced once the timeline has it sent, or once a midpoint payment is recorded. **The balance was right either way**; only the words changed.
- **A negative final invoice is not a bug.** If the job ran far enough under estimate, the 75% taken by midpoint overcollected. It renders as a green Credit, and since 2026-09-29 the covering email says *Credit to you* and that nothing is due — it used to read *“Balance due: $-2,741 … due within 7 calendar days”*. That's correct — issue it, and tell the client the credit is coming.
- **The final’s *Original Estimate* row is the estimate the client signed (since 2026-09-29).** Accepted change orders sit on their own line directly under it. The row used to add the change orders in, so it no longer matched the deposit and midpoint printed beneath it.
- **Every invoice you send files itself to Drive**, one file per stage, so the three never overwrite each other. Sending a stage again replaces its own copy — which is what you want when you correct a figure and reissue. Printing files nothing (it did on the Invoices tab, retired 2026-09-11); if a send's filing did not land, **📁 File … to Drive** appears beside that invoice.
- **On a fixed-price job** the services total is the flat fee **plus the 30% prep fee as its own line** when the job has prep vendors (2026-09-23) and, on an estimate saved from 2026-09-30, **plus the rush premium and less the preferred-client discount as lines of their own**; the stages split that sum and labour is never trued to hours. The prep fee is on the estimate's quotes at the deposit, and on each prep vendor's *Quote $* figure on the Job Plan at the midpoint and final — so put the vendor's actual bill in that box before you send the final. It moves by exactly 30% of the gap between a quote and a bill. **A fixed-price job saved before the 2026-09-23 change and never reopened** still carries the prep fee inside its flat fee, prints no second line and bills nothing extra — that is correct, not a missing fee; one saved before 2026-09-30 and never reopened carries its rush premium and discount inside the fee the same way.
- **Every figure prints its cents when it has any (since 2026-10-01).** A $21,679.50 estimate asks $10,839.75, then $5,419.88, then $5,419.87, and the three add up to it to the cent (re-measured 2026-10-02 on a Premium Estate Settlement with 3% off; this example was $22,071.25 on P17's quarter hours); *$900* still reads *$900*. Until then everything was rounded to the whole dollar. The ACH payment link asks for exactly what the invoice says is outstanding, cents included.
- **A Home Prep final names what its total is made of (2026-09-30).** It reads *Services total (Home Sale Preparation Fee on actual vendor spend + logged concierge hours)*, or just *(Home Sale Preparation Fee on actual vendor spend)* when you logged no concierge hours. It used to say *logged hours + actual fees* on every prep job, hours or not. Every other service keeps *Actual Havellin services total (logged hours + actual fees)*.

## Step 13 · Close the job — Client Dashboard

Work handed over → close the job, with **■ Close job** on the band. It is there from the day the job is active (2026-09-29): an outline button beside whatever step is lit, and the filled button once the midpoint invoice is recorded as sent and its payment recorded. The app stamps the delivery date, the time and the job's assigned concierge (since 2026-09-30; it used to record the manager who approved the price). Then the **final invoice** is the filled button (Step 12).

> **⚠** **⚠ Close when the work is handed over, and never record a payment that has not arrived to get there.** Until 2026-09-29 a finished job with its midpoint unpaid had no way to close and no way to send the final — the band read *Collect the midpoint payment* and nothing else. That is gone: close it, and the final bills the midpoint's share with everything else. With no midpoint payment on file, *Close job* asks *Close this job now?* first — press **Cancel** if the work is not finished. On an estate whose Disposition Ledger has no signed copy filed it asks too, and says so (since 2026-10-03): OK still closes, but get the ledger signed and file the copy (Step 10e). Closing also files the ledger to Drive by itself; if that fails the sync badge says why, and **File to Drive** on the ledger card does it. Closed it by mistake? **↺ Re-open job** undoes it until the final invoice goes out or a final payment is recorded (below).

On the Job Plan, a closed job loses the red *you're at the project midpoint* banner, and *Midpoint invoice sent* reads *not sent — the job is closed, so the final invoice bills it* with no button to press.

After the close the midpoint rows wear an **amber ring** until they are settled: *Unpaid — the final invoice carries it*, or *Not sent — the final invoice bills it*. A midpoint cheque that turns up later is recorded as normal (**✓ Record midpoint payment** is in the strip at the foot of the timeline); once the final is paid the row reads *Paid with the final invoice*. If you had drafted the midpoint invoice and not sent it, delete the draft in Gmail. If you had sent it from Gmail, press *I've sent it* **before** you close — or, if you have already closed, press **Re-open job**, confirm it, and close again.

### Closed it by mistake? Re-open job (new 2026-09-29)

On a closed job, **↺ Re-open job** sits beside the lit step as an outline button, where *Close job* was. It asks first: *Re-open this job?*, with the day it was closed and the name on that close. OK puts the job back to *Active*, clears the handover date and lands you on the Job Plan. The timeline goes back to the step the job was on, *Close job* is back, and *Work complete* reads *Re-opened — the earlier close was undone*. Close it again when the work is handed over; that day becomes the handover. Nothing else moves: the hours, the payments and anything already sent stay as they are.

> **⚠** **⚠ Re-open is gone once the final invoice has gone out, or once a final payment is recorded.** The client is then holding a bill that says the job is finished. If the job needs anything after that, tell Anthony.

> **If you drafted the final before you noticed**, the question says so, with the day and the Gmail account: that draft billed the job as it stood at the close. **Delete it in Gmail** — the app cannot reach your mailbox. The *Final invoice sent* row keeps reminding you until a final actually goes out. When you close again the band offers a fresh *Send final invoice*; once you have drafted it, the row says to send that one and delete the older.

### Before you close it — the Close-out card (new 2026-09-22)

On the Job Plan's Close-out stage and on Job Admin & Inv, on **every** job, Home Prep included. Four steps, in order:

1. **Call the client.** A call, not a text. Tick *Satisfaction call* once they have told you they are happy.
2. **Ask for the Google review.** Press *Draft review request*: a short thank-you with our review link lands in **your** Gmail drafts. Read it, send it, tap *I've sent it*. When the review shows up, tick *Review posted on Google*.
3. **Ask for a referral.** Who else could use us? Tick it.
4. **Rate every vendor.** One to five stars and a note on each vendor you used — prep trades and haulers included. The star saves itself to the Vendor Directory.

> **⚠** **The review button stays grey until the call is ticked, on purpose.** Never send a review request to a client you have not spoken to first — if something went wrong, you want to hear it on the phone, not read it on Google.

> **⚠** **You cannot close the job until every vendor you used is rated.** *Close job* will refuse and name who is left. It takes a minute, and it is the only way next month's concierge knows which painter to call.

### If the job dies instead

The **✕** on the client's row in the client list — or **Mark lost** beside the lit *Client accepted* step — opens closeout, and what it does depends on whether *any* money arrived:

- **Nothing received** → *Lost*, with a reason (price, scope, timing, unresponsive, competitor, other) and an optional note — competitor name, price gap. A job marked Won that then withdraws flips back to not-won: a win that produced nothing isn't a win.
- **Anything received, on any stage** → the box names the amount and it closes as *Closed — Deposit Retained*. It still counts as won, at what it kept rather than what it was quoted (2026-09-29). Until then a client who had paid *part* of the deposit and walked was recorded as a plain loss and the money they had paid appeared nowhere. **On a fixed price** the button reads **Close — Retain $2,000** (whatever came in): we keep it.
- **On an hourly job, since 2026-10-01: we keep the deposit or the work done, whichever is more, and the rest goes back.** The box shows three rows — *Received*, *Earned* (the deposit, or the work done as the final invoice would bill it: the hours logged at the job's rates, with materials and fees) and ***Refund due*** — and the button names both halves, for example **Close — Retain $6,125 · refund $3,062.50** on a $12,250 Home Cleanout (re-measured 2026-10-02; it read Retain $6,006.25 · refund $3,003.13 on P17's quarter hours). Make sure every hour worked is logged on the Job Plan *before* you close it out: the work done is read from the log.

**Then record the refund once it has gone (since 2026-10-01).** The client's card shows a **Walkaway settlement** above the payments list: received, earned, anything refunded, and the *Refund due*. Send the refund the way Anthony says (cheque, wire or bank transfer), then press **Record refund**: it opens on the amount due and today; pick how it went, add the cheque number or wire confirmation and who it was paid to, and press **Record refund →**. The refund goes on the payments list as its own line, and the amount the job records as retained drops to what it earned. More than is due asks you first; more than the job holds is refused. If you recorded one by mistake, **Void** it with the reason — it is never deleted.

> **⚠** Both of those are **terminal**. Nothing in the app moves them. If a lost client comes back, start a new job.

> **⚠ The ✕ disappears once the job is finished (new 2026-09-22).** It is there for the whole engagement, because a client really can cancel mid-job — and it goes the moment the work is **delivered** or a **final payment** is recorded. Either one on its own is enough. **Nothing else about the row changes:** the client is still listed and **Open →** still opens them.
>
> **If the final invoice has not been paid, that is chasing money — not a lost job.** The work was done. Record the payment when it arrives; never close a delivered client out as lost to tidy the list. One press used to turn a fully paid $19,940 job into *Closed — Deposit Retained* with *Price / estimate too high* against it.
>
> A job already marked *Lost* or *Closed — Deposit Retained* **keeps** the ✕, so you can still correct the reason. A lost job is not in the default list — press the **Lost** filter to find it.
>
> **Re-opening a closed job brings the ✕ back**, because the work is no longer recorded as delivered.

## Where the job sits — the status list

| Status | Means | What's unlocked |
| --- | --- | --- |
| New | Intake done; no estimate submitted yet (a saved draft, or one a manager denied, still reads New) | Build estimate, on the client's timeline |
| Pending Approval | Estimate submitted for a manager PIN | — |
| Approved — Awaiting Client | We approved our own figure. **The client has not answered.** | Email the estimate. Nothing may be staffed. |
| **Won** | The client accepted | Agreement, staffing, the Job Plan staffing section |
| **Won · Pending Re-approval** | The client accepted, and a revised price — a discount, or an edit before the packet went out — is waiting on a manager PIN | Everything Won unlocks: the client is still won. The PIN or a deny returns it to **Won**. |
| **Won · Awaiting Re-acceptance** | The client accepted, and a manager has since approved the estimate at a **higher** price | Staffing and the Job Plan carry on. The agreement, the deposit invoice and the payment link wait for their yes to the new price (step 5). |
| Active | Activated: signed, deposit in and, on a probate matter, the Letters received (on a trust, since 2026-10-03, the Certification of Trust) | **Close job** beside whichever step is lit; the schedule counts from this day. (Hours open once the deposit is recorded and the team confirmed, and photos once the client is Won; neither waits for Active.) |
| Closed | Work handed over; delivery stamped | The final invoice, and any payment still owed. **Re-open job** puts it back to Active until the final goes out or a final payment is recorded. |
| Lost | Died before any money arrived | Terminal |
| Closed — Deposit Retained | Died after any money came in, a part deposit included. On a fixed price we keep what was paid; on an hourly job, the deposit or the work done, whichever is more, and any refund due is recorded on the card when it is sent (2026-10-01). The amount is named. | Terminal. Still counts as won, at the amount kept: what came in, less any refund recorded. |

## Home Prep for Sale — the short version

A sell-side, show-ready service, mostly through Douglas Elliman referral agents. Havellin manages every trade — paint, repairs, landscaping, deep cleaning, staging — and charges a flat **30% Home Sale Preparation Fee** on the managed vendor spend — its name on every document and screen since 2026-10-01, where it used to be the *GC / Site Management Fee* or the *site management fee*; nothing about it changed but the name. No room scoring, no crew. **Since 2026-09-14 it can also carry concierge declutter hours** where the house needs clearing as well as trades — see step 3 below.

> **⚠** **The agreement is with the homeowner.** The referring agent is a referral channel, never our client. No document should suggest otherwise.

1. **Intake** with Service Type = *Home Prep for Sale*. No property value needed.
2. **Build Estimate** empties out — rooms, crew, labour, collections, vehicles and moving materials all disappear, and so do the five vendor cards that aren't Property Preparation. **Two cards are left**: *Home Prep for Sale — Managed Vendors*, sitting beside the Job details, and *Pre-Prep Declutter — Concierge Hours* below it (step 3). Add each trade with an estimated cost; the footer shows the vendor total and the running 30%. **Fill in the scope note on every line** while you're on the walkthrough: exactly what you'll tell that trade ("5 bedrooms + hallway, walls & ceilings; front and back landscaping"). It carries through to sourcing.
3. **The house is a mess as well? Type the hours.** Anthony's case exactly: you were called out to prep for sale and the living room, the master, the dining room and the kitchen all need clearing first. Put the hours you think it takes into *Pre-Prep Declutter* — four, five, six, whatever you are standing there looking at, in whole hours. Since 2026-10-02 a part hour is rounded up: type 5.5 and the line under the box says *Estimates round up to whole hours: 5.5 is priced as 6*, and it is saved as 6 — 6 × $150 = $900 on the client's page, and the total counts exactly that. Nothing is refused (for one day, 2026-10-01, the box took quarter hours and Save refused anything else). They bill at your concierge rate **on top of** the 30%, and the line under the box prices them as you type. **Leave it at 0 if the house only needs the trades.**

   > **⚠ Do NOT re-type the job as Home Editing to get the hours.** That was the obvious move and it was measured: the price is driven by the whole house's square footage, not by the rooms you tick, so marking the other rooms ✕ out of scope changes almost nothing. On a 3,500 sqft house, scoring four rooms and scoring all twelve both come out at **$10,650** (re-measured 2026-10-02 on whole hours; $10,475 on P17's quarter hours, and about $8,950 when this was first measured). You would be quoting ten thousand dollars of crew labour for half a day of your own time — and the client's agreement, invoice and Drive folder would all change name. Type the hours instead.

   > **Typing hours changes what the client signs, so re-read it.** The estimate Terms stop saying "not billed hourly" and start saying Havellin is paid on two bases; the agreement's §3.3 does the same; and the **15%** over-estimate notice now applies to your hours. That is all correct — just do not send a document you have not looked at since you changed the number. **And a prep estimate still needs at least one vendor:** hours alone will not save, and the refusal tells you to use Home Editing instead. It is right.

4. **Client estimate** reads vendors-first: itemised vendor estimates at cost, then the 30% fee, then one total. Same 50 / 25 / 25 schedule on the Havellin fee. No completion date is projected — the schedule is confirmed once vendors are booked, and the estimate says so.
5. Approve → send → Mark Won → agreement → deposit, exactly as above. **Find the house needs clearing after they signed with vendors only?** Raise a change order for your hours before you do the work — it prints your rate, the client accepts it, and the hours log opens (step 10d). **Found a trade nobody quoted?** Since 2026-09-30 a change order can add a prep vendor too, with or without hours: pick it under **Add a prep vendor** and type **Its estimated cost**. Once the client accepts it, the vendor joins the prep vendors on the Job Plan to be booked and quoted like the others, and the final bills the 30% on its *Quote $*. An unaccepted change order adds nothing.
6. **Job Plan** is stripped down to three things (four if you quoted declutter hours or a change order added some): *Budget & Fee* (estimated spend, quoted-to-date, the running 30% on actual quotes, with an over-budget flag), *Home Prep Vendors — Sourcing & Status* (assign the vendor, set status, log the actual quote against the scope note; a vendor an accepted change order added is listed with the rest), and a *Coordination Checklist* — scope confirmed → quotes collected → vendors booked → work underway → completed/inspected → final invoices and fee billed. Top to bottom it runs: hours log (if hours were quoted or added by a change order), checklist, then *Budget & Fee* and *Sourcing*, which fold — tap the ▸ on the left to open or shut them — and the Close-out card last.

   > **⚠ If you quoted declutter hours, or a change order added some, the hours log is there — use it.** It opens on a prep job that quoted hours, or once the client accepts a change order that adds them (step 10d), and it shows your concierge row alone (there is no crew on this engagement). **The final invoice bills what is in the log, not what you quoted**, so hours you worked and never recorded bill nothing at all. On a job that quoted hours the app will refuse the final until something is logged, which is the right answer; on one whose hours came only from a change order it warns instead, and then bills the fee alone. The Budget & Fee card shows the hours authorised — quoted, plus any a change order added — against *logged to date* and turns red if you are more than 15% past — at that point ring the homeowner: the estimate promised them that call, and more hours need another change order they accept.
7. **Job Admin & Inv on a prep job** is short on purpose: the client line, the brown Next band, the desk paperwork (open), and the Close-out card. No inventory, no appraisers, no approval request — a prep job records nothing about the contents of the house. Close out exactly as any other job: call, review request, referral, rate every trade.

## Quick reference

### What we bill the client

| Item | Standard | Premium Estate |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Transition Concierge | $150/hr | $185/hr |
| Property Specialist | $100/hr | $125/hr |
| Hours and money | Estimates in **whole hours**, rounded up; hours logged in **half hours** and billed as logged; change orders in whole hours; money to the **cent** (since 2026-10-02) | Same |
| Service Management Fee (third-party vendors) | **None — billed at cost** | **None** |
| Vendor coordination, per line you add | **touches × 0.5** — online auction 6 hrs · mover 3 · auction house 3 · appraiser 2 · estate sale company 1.5 · hauler / dumpster / end-of-job cleaning 1 · shredding 0.5 (online auction and estate sale since 2026-10-01; they were 2 and 4). Prep trades (painting, staging, a GC) book none: the 30% covers them | Same hours, premium rate — the appraiser included (it booked none on a Premium Estate for one day, 2026-09-30) |
| Home Sale Preparation Fee (Home Prep) | **30%** of prep vendor spend — standalone *or* bundled, hourly *or* fixed price, always its own line | 30% |
| Home Prep **declutter** hours (new 2026-09-14) | Concierge rate, **on top of** the 30% — typed on the estimate or added by a change order, billed as logged | Premium rate |
| Home Prep *coordination* hours | **None** — they are inside the 30% *(changed 2026-09-10; this row used to say “None — at cost, plus 0.5–2 coordination hrs per trade”, which contradicted the row above it)* | **None** |
| Moving Materials | **Fixed package price** — you pick a tier on the estimate ($200–$1,500). *Not* cost-plus, no handling fee, no receipts *(corrected 2026-09-11)* | Same packages |
| Preferred Client Discount | Havellin labour only (plus the rush premium charged on it) · **max 15%**. On a fixed price, its own line on the fee less the moving-materials package (2026-09-30) | max 15% |
| Expedited delivery (rush) | +20% of Havellin services, never on the 30% home prep fee (2026-09-30). On a fixed price it is its own line: 20% of the fee | +20% |
| Fixed-price contingency | The suggested fee is the services priced as the job would run with two specialists, plus **20%** (Probate **25%**, Contested Probate **35%**). No rush premium or discount inside it — those are lines on the fee — and the home prep fee sits on top | Same |

Living-client services open on **time-and-materials**; the three estate services open on a **firm fixed price** (since 2026-09-30), and you can switch either way — every service but Home Prep can be quoted fixed (probate since 2026-09-20). Home Prep has no fixed price: it bills the 30% fee on vendor spend, plus any declutter hours quoted or added by a change order, as logged. Vendor work is pass-through — the vendor bills the client directly at cost and Havellin takes only the fee on top.

**Order on the price:** services total → plus 20% expedite on it, leaving out the 30% home prep fee → less the discount (labour and the premium charged on it) → plus vendors at cost. On a fixed price the premium and the discount are lines on the fee. *(Corrected 2026-09-23 — this line had the discount first, which was the order before 2026-09-11. The prep fee came out of the premium on 2026-09-30.)*

### Manager PIN required for

- Approving or denying an estimate
- A **final** invoice more than ±15% off the estimate (deposit and midpoint need none)
- Deleting a client, vendor, partner or contractor. A vendor, partner or contractor with history is retired instead, never deleted: a vendor rated or used, or a partner with referrals attributed, goes to *Do Not Use*; a contractor named on any job goes *Inactive*. The app asks when you press delete (*Delete this … permanently* in a vendor's or partner's edit form, the ✕ on a contractor) and again after the PIN (2026-09-30).

**Not** required for the **agreement** — that second PIN was removed 2026-09-10 (step 6), and not for a Change Order — that one is settled with the client, not internally. Everyone has their own PIN and the approval is recorded under whoever typed it, so use yours.

### The margin panel

**It is not shown anywhere in the app now** — it came off the Build Estimate screen as too much detail for the screen you build the estimate on, and nothing else draws it. Nothing about margin changed, and the **15% discount cap** is the guardrail that's left. If you're weighing a discount bigger than the app will let you give, that's a conversation with a manager, not a workaround.

What it was: an indicative profitability readout with a flag on it, **not a discounting tool**. *Price at 30% Margin* was a reference line at an admittedly arbitrary margin and *Above Reference* simply the distance to it. Neither was an allowance to spend. In practice, a job needing that much discount is one to walk away from rather than price down to.

### If something won't let you proceed

| Symptom | What's missing |
| --- | --- |
| **A red line on the Found on site card: _Original will held by Havellin_ or _Cash held by Havellin_** | Nobody has recorded the hand-over. Hand it over today against the printed receipt, then press **Record the hand-over** on it (Step 10g). The line leads the Job Admin card at the desk until you do (2026-10-03). |
| **_Original will deposited with the clerk_ has gone red** | More than ten days since the hand-over and no deposit recorded. Ask whoever holds it, usually the estate attorney, whether it went to the clerk, and record the day with **Confirm the deposit with the clerk**. |
| **_Record cash found_ says _Two different people count the cash_** | Both counters are the same person, however the name is spelt. Two of you count it together: name the second. |
| **A find has no _File signed receipt_** | It appears once the hand-over is recorded, because the receipt is signed at the hand-over. Press **Record the hand-over** first. |
| **The _Form 706_ chip is red** | The 706 question is unanswered (the chip ends _…, if one is filed_), or the due date has passed. Ask the estate attorney and answer _Form 706 being filed?_ on Edit Client; once the date has passed, confirm with them that the return or an extension went in. We never file it (2026-10-03). |
| **A trust job says _Chain of custody is mandatory_** | Correct (2026-10-03): it is mandatory on every trust administration, as on probate. Log a custody event on every item that leaves. |
| **_Activate job_ refuses on an Estate Settlement: _Executor authorization must be received_** | Correct (2026-09-29). The estate is going through probate — you answered *Probate* or *Both* to how it is administered — and a probate estate cannot start without its Letters, whatever the service. When the certified copy is in hand, set it to *Received* under **&#9998; Edit Client**, with the date the Letters issued. If the estate is really a trust matter, correct the matter there instead: since 2026-10-03 (P19) it then waits on the Certification of Trust (next row). |
| **_Activate job_ refuses on a trust: _The successor trustee’s Certification of Trust must be received_** | Correct (2026-10-03, P19; Anthony). A trust job starts once the successor trustee's proof of authority is in hand, the way a probate job waits on its Letters. Ask the trustee or their attorney for a copy of the Certification of Trust, then set it to *Received* under **&#9998; Edit Client** (the control is named *Certification of Trust* on a trust). The red chip on the client card and on the Trust card goes with it. |
| **An Estate Settlement now shows _Probate Details_ — case number, Letters, §733.604** | Correct (2026-09-29). Its matter is recorded as probate, so it has a court case like any Probate job. Nothing in the block is required on an Estate Settlement; fill in what you have. It disappears if the matter is changed to trust or neither. |
| **An amber box: _No court on this matter … The Probate Estate Settlement service prices court work that will not happen_** | The service is Probate and the matter is _Trust_ or _Neither_ (2026-10-05). Unless Anthony wants the court work priced, switch the service to _Estate Settlement_ before the estimate is approved, on **✎ Edit Client** or Build Estimate's service dropdown; the estimate reprices. Nothing is refused if you leave it. Once the estimate is approved the box goes and the price stays: a change then goes through **✎ Edit estimate** or a change order. |
| **The agreement or the estimate says _Estate Settlement_, but the job is a Probate service** | Correct (2026-10-05): on a matter recorded _Trust_ or _Neither_ no client document says Probate (_Contested Estate Settlement_ on Contested Probate). The price is still the Probate service's. The client list and the dashboard keep the service's own name. |
| **The trust reads _dated March 3, 2015_ on the agreement and the Trust Schedule, _Mar 3, 2015_ on the dashboard** | Correct (2026-10-05): a document cites the trust with the month spelled out; the dashboard's cards keep the short date every screen uses. |
| **The _Record payment_ box opens on a figure that is not the cheque in your hand** | It opens on what that stage's invoice asks for, less anything already recorded (2026-09-29). Type what actually arrived — the box is a starting point, and the money is what you record. Only the deposit asks before accepting a short amount. |
| **The ACH link button says _"This ACH link asks for $…, but only $… is outstanding … Do not send it"_** | That link was made before a cheque came in. A Stripe link cannot be edited: switch it off in the Stripe Dashboard under *Payment links*, and take the balance by cheque or wire. A link made after a part cheque asks only for the balance. |
| **A client paid part of the deposit and walked away** | Press **✕**. With any money received, on any stage, it closes out as *Closed — Deposit Retained* naming the amount (2026-09-29) — the button reads *Close — Retain $…*. It used to be recorded as a plain loss and the money appeared nowhere. On an hourly job that paid more than it earned the box also shows the *Refund due*; record the refund on the card once it has gone (Step 13). |
| **_Save & Confirm Job Team_ refuses: _the second slot has no name_** | The estimate staffs two concierges (its *second concierge* box is ticked). Pick someone on the *Transition Concierge 2* row, or *Contractor — TC* if you have not picked yet (2026-09-29). The estimate priced two, and on a rush job promised them to the client; if the job really needs only one, raise it with the manager rather than confirming with the slot empty. |
| **A date entered one evening reads as the next day** | Recorded before 2026-09-29: after 8pm Eastern (7pm in winter) every date the app filled in read tomorrow. Correct it where it was entered — a payment in its record, an hours line on the Job Plan. Anything entered since is today where you are. |
| **The final invoice row has no View or Print, and _Send_ refuses** | An hourly job with no hours logged: the final cannot be priced yet (2026-09-29). *Send* says *No hours have been logged for this job, so the final cannot be issued*, and no manager PIN can release it. **🔑 Manager approval** says the same since 2026-09-30 (it used to answer *inside the ±15% tolerance — no approval needed*, which was wrong: the hours are what is missing). Log them on the Job Plan and all three come back. |
| **The *Final invoice sent* row says crew hours were logged with no concierge hours** | The log has specialist hours and none of yours. You are on site for every crew hour, so the log is almost certainly missing your hours, and the final would under-bill by about the figure it names (the concierge time the estimate priced). Log them on the Job Plan before sending. It is a warning, not a block: if the log really is right, the final still sends (since 2026-09-30). Never on a fixed price. |
| **Home Prep's middle invoice says _Second_, not _Midpoint_** | Correct (2026-09-29). Home Prep has no project midpoint; its estimate bills the second 25% once the vendor schedule is booked, and every screen and document now names it the way the estimate does. |
| **_Offer discount_ or _✎ Edit estimate_ has gone from the timeline** | The signing packet has been sent. The client is being asked to sign that price, so it cannot be re-priced any more, by a discount or an edit: a change now goes through a change order (step 10d). Build Estimate says the same in its banner. |
| **A won client reads _Won · Pending Re-approval_** | You offered a discount (or resubmitted an edit) and it is waiting on a manager PIN. The client is still won — staffing and the Job Plan are unaffected — and the PIN returns it to *Won*. Before 2026-09-29 it read *Pending Approval*, then *Approved — Awaiting Client* or *New*, as if the client had never said yes; it reads *Won* now with nothing to re-do. |
| **A won client reads *Won · Awaiting Re-acceptance*, and the agreement will not send** | A manager approved the estimate at a higher price than the one the client accepted. Send the revised estimate, get their yes to the new figure, and press **Client accepted the revised price** (step 5). The job stays won in the meantime. |
| ***Estimate sent to client* is lit again and the button says *Send revised estimate*** | The approved price is now above the one on the estimate the client has. Send them the revised one and press *I've sent it* (step 4). This happens before they say yes, too. |
| ***Edit estimate* is missing while the estimate is waiting on a manager** | By design (2026-09-29): nothing can change the estimate while the manager has it. The PIN or a deny brings it back. |
| **The discount box says “between 0% and 15%”** | You typed more than 15, a decimal or nothing. Whole numbers 0–15 only. **0 takes the discount off.** |
| **The signing packet says PREVIEW and has no Print button** | The client is not marked Won yet — or the price has gone up since they said yes and they have not accepted the new one. Read it with them all you like; Print and Send come back after step 5. |
| **The rush line no longer says we add a second concierge** | Correct. The 20% buys priority scheduling. The estimate names a second concierge or a bigger crew only when you have actually staffed one. Do not promise extra people for the premium. |
| **The job is finished, the band says _Collect the midpoint payment_, and there is no way to close it** | Fixed 2026-09-29. **■ Close job** is beside the lit step from the day the job is active. Press it: the final invoice becomes the filled button and bills the midpoint's share with everything else. **Never record a payment that has not arrived** to move the band. |
| **Activation day: the band says _Do the work_ and has no filled button** | Correct (2026-09-29). The midpoint invoice falls due at the plan's halfway day, once half the work is done, or once every room is locked. It waits beside the band as an outline button reading *due around <date>*; press it early only if the client asked. |
| **_Close job_ asks _Close this job now?_ and mentions the midpoint** | No midpoint payment is recorded. That is fine: the final bills it. OK closes and stamps today as the handover date; *Re-open job* undoes it until the final goes out. Cancel if the work is not finished. |
| **_Close job_ asks _Close this job now?_ and mentions the Disposition Ledger** | An estate whose ledger has no signed copy filed (since 2026-10-03). OK closes anyway and files the ledger to Drive; have the representative sign it, then press **File signed copy** on the Disposition Ledger card (Job Admin & Inv). Cancel if you would rather get it signed first. |
| **A closed job's Job Plan has no red midpoint banner, and *Midpoint invoice sent* says *not sent — the job is closed*** | Correct (2026-09-29). The final invoice bills the midpoint's share, so there is nothing to send. Do not send the midpoint now: it would bill that share twice. |
| **The midpoint rows have an amber ring on a closed job** | Open, not lit: the midpoint was not paid (or not sent) before the close. The row says what settles it. Record a midpoint cheque from the strip if one arrives; paying the final settles it too. |
| **You closed a job by mistake** | Fixed 2026-09-29. **↺ Re-open job** is beside the lit step of a closed job. It asks first, then puts the job back to *Active* and clears the handover date. Close it again when the work is handed over. |
| **A closed job has no _Re-open job_ button** | On purpose: the final invoice has gone out, or a final payment is recorded, so the client holds a bill that says the job is finished. Tell Anthony. |
| **_Re-open_ says the final invoice drafted on a date no longer applies** | You drafted the final before you noticed the close was a mistake, and it billed the job as it stood then. Press OK, then delete that draft in Gmail. A fresh one goes out when you close again. |
| **The final invoice row says a Gmail draft _was made before the job was re-opened_** | That is the final you drafted before a Re-open. It is still in Gmail at the old figure. Delete it; do not send it. If you have since drafted a fresh final, send the fresh one. The line goes the moment a final is sent. |
| **_Work complete_ says _Re-opened — the earlier close was undone_** | Somebody closed this job and then re-opened it. The earlier close is kept on the record. Close it again when the work is handed over. |
| **The final invoice says _25% midpoint — billed on this invoice_** | Correct (2026-09-29). No midpoint invoice went out before the close, so the final bills the midpoint's share itself. The balance is right either way. |
| **You drafted the midpoint invoice, closed the job, and the draft link is gone** | On purpose: the final bills that share now, so sending the midpoint would bill it twice. Delete the draft in Gmail. |
| **_Close job_ says it cannot close the job yet** | A vendor you used is not rated. The message names them. Open the Close-out card (Job Plan or Job Admin & Inv), give each a star rating, and close again. |
| **_Draft review request_ is greyed out** | The satisfaction call is not ticked. Call the client first, tick it, and the button comes on. |
| **A vendor is missing from the scorecard** | It is not marked *Confirmed* on the sourcing card. Only vendors you actually used are rated — set the status and it appears. |
| **A prep job's Job Admin & Inv tab has no inventory** | Correct. A prep job records nothing about the house's contents; the tab is the paperwork and the close-out. |
| **The *Name N shots* button is not there** | Either every shot already has a name — it withdraws itself — or the unnamed shots have not reached Drive yet (press **Retry** on them in the room). With no Apps Script URL on this device the button is still there, and pressing it says so. If the out-of-date banner is up about the deployment, the naming service is part of what it is telling you. |
| **You press it and it says the key is missing** | Setup, not a fault. `ANTHROPIC_API_KEY` goes in the Apps Script project's Script Properties. Anthony does this once. |
| **Rows say *agent* or *agent · unsure* in bronze** | Working as intended — it named them and nobody has checked them yet. Read it, correct it if it is wrong, and the badge clears. Start with the *unsure* ones. |
| **It named something wrongly — a maker it got wrong, a reproduction it called original** | Correct it on the row. That is the job, and it is why every row comes back unchecked. If it is getting a whole category wrong on one house, tell Anthony — that is worth knowing. |
| **One photograph turned into four rows** | Correct, and not a duplicate. Four objects in one frame is four inventory lines sharing one picture — the row says *1 of 4 in this photo*. Do not delete three of them. |
| **An amber *Possible duplicates* block, or a bronze *possible duplicate* chip on a row** | The agent gave the same name to two lines in one room, **off different photographs** — usually because the object was shot twice on the Items pass. Open both pictures. If it is one object, press **Remove** on the line you do not want; if it really is two, press **Not duplicates**. Nothing is merged or deleted for you. |
| **It flagged two matching nightstands, and there really are two** | Expected — it cannot tell a duplicated frame from a real pair, so it asks. Press **Not duplicates** and it stops. Correcting either name or ticking the row reviewed does the same thing. |
| **You pressed Remove on a duplicate — is the photograph gone?** | **No.** Only the line comes off; the photograph stays in Google Drive, and *Removed items* at the foot of the tab gives the line back whole. That is different from binning a shot in the room, which does trash the picture. **Remove line** in a line's panel works the same way on any line (2026-09-30). |
| You want one line off the desk — a mis-split, a line for something that is not there — without deleting the photograph | Open it (▾) and press **Remove line** (new 2026-09-30). It asks first, takes the line and never the photograph, keeps the number spent, and *Removed items* at the foot of the tab gives it back. |
| **Thirty books came back as one line** | Correct. Ordinary household goods are recorded as a lot with a quantity; art, antiques, silver, jewellery, rugs, firearms and anything with a readable mark get their own line. Split it by hand if this particular lot warrants it. |
| **A row came back with no name at all** | The agent looked and found nothing inventoriable — a wall, an empty room, a shot that did not come out. It deliberately does not invent a line. Name it or bin the photo. |
| **A *Must find* notice appeared** | Go and look. It saw the item, or somewhere one would be kept, in a photograph. It has not ticked *Found it* and it is not telling you the thing is definitely there — you confirm. |
| **Where did the Client Intake tab go?** | It is not a tab any more (2026-09-23). Press **+ Add New Client** at the top of the Client Dashboard — the same form, as a full screen. Saving drops you on the new client's dashboard. |
| **Where did the Build Estimate tab go?** | Not a tab any more either. Open the client and press the brown **Build estimate** button above their timeline; once an estimate exists, **✎ Edit estimate** beside it opens the same screen and un-approves it first if it was approved (until the signing packet goes out). There is no job dropdown on it — it is always for the client you opened it from. |
| **The brown button says *Set the walkthrough date*, not Build estimate** | No walkthrough is booked, so booking one comes first. **Build estimate** is the outline button right beside it — press that if you are already in the house. |
| **You pressed ← on Build Estimate, came back, and the rooms were gone** | Unsaved scoring is kept only while the page stays open *and* you come back to the *same* client. Opening a different client's estimate, or reloading, starts from the saved copy. **Press Save Estimate before you leave the screen.** |
| **The tabs are split, with a gap in the middle** | On purpose (2026-09-23). Left is the work on a client — Client Dashboard, Job Plan, Job Admin. Right is the people we call — Contractors, Vendors, Referral Partners. |
| **Where did the Win / Loss tab go?** | It is a row of four figures on the Client Dashboard now, straight under Total Jobs (2026-09-23). Press **Won** or **Lost** — the ones with the little arrow — to list the clients behind the number, and press a name to open that client. Nothing the tab showed is missing, and there is now a list of the wins as well as the losses. |
| **The Probate, Estate Settlement, Home Prep (or any other service) filter button is gone** | Press the **Service** heading on the client table instead. Every service's clients sit together, in the order the service list uses — Home Prep, Home Editing, Home Transition, Home Cleanout, Estate Settlement, Probate, Contested Probate. Press it again to reverse it, a third time to go back to newest first. |
| **The *New* or *RE Potential* filter is gone** | Press the **Status** heading — the new clients come first — or the **RE** heading — the ★ clients come first. |
| **The client list is in an order you do not recognise** | A heading is sorting it — it has a bronze arrow. Press that heading until the arrow goes faint, and the list is back to newest client first. Reloading the page clears it too. |
| **Conversion Rate shows a dash** | Nobody has been marked won or lost yet, so there is no rate to show. It fills in the moment one client is. (It used to read **0%** here, which looked like nobody had ever said yes.) |
| **A client who paid the deposit and then walked is on the Won list** | Correct. *Closed — Deposit Retained* counts as won because it produced revenue, and the list says so beside the name. It is under the **Closed** filter too now — it used to be reachable only through All. It counts at what it kept: what came in, less any refund recorded (2026-10-01). |
| **You want to see the rooms you ticked, or what you wrote in them** | Client dashboard → **🔍 Walkthrough**, beside *Edit Client*. Private notes, every room with its scores and note, the collections, the vehicles. You do not need to go near Build Estimate. |
| **There is no ‹Walkthrough› button on this client** | No estimate has been saved for the job yet — there is nothing to read. If you know there is one, the estimates may still be loading on this device; press it again in a moment and it will tell you which it is. |
| **The walkthrough page has no Print button** | Deliberate. It carries your private notes word for word, so there is no way to print, send or file it — a printed copy of that is exactly what must not exist. Read it off the screen. |
| **The walkthrough shows different scores from the ones you remember typing** | You are reading the frozen copy taken when the estimate was approved — the page says *Locked* and names who approved it. That is the record of what was quoted. The live scores only show while an estimate is still unapproved, and then the page says so in amber. |
| **A Home Prep job needs a few rooms cleared and you are about to re-type it as Home Editing** | Don't. Stay on Home Prep and type the hours into *Pre-Prep Declutter* on Build Estimate. Re-typing prices off the whole property's square footage, not the rooms you tick — on a 3,500 sqft house, four rooms and twelve rooms both come out near **$8,950**, so you would quote a full editing job for half a day of your own time. Re-type only when it really is an editing job that also needs trades. |
| **A prep estimate won't save** — "add at least one Home Prep vendor with a cost" | Declutter hours alone are not a prep engagement. If there are genuinely no vendors and the job is only decluttering, re-type it as **Home Editing**; the refusal says so. Otherwise add the trades. |
| **A prep job's final invoice is blocked and you thought prep was exempt** | It is exempt only when it quoted **no** declutter hours. You quoted hours, so the final bills what is in the log — and an empty log would bill nothing for work you did. Log the hours on the Job Plan, then reprint. |
| **The prep Job Plan has an hours log now and it did not before** | It opens when the estimate quoted declutter hours, or once the client accepts a change order that adds concierge hours (2026-09-25). A change order that only adds a vendor opens nothing. Your concierge row is the only one — there is no crew on this engagement, so no specialist rows and no specialist names to fill in at team sign-off. |
| **Budget & Fee on a prep job has gone red on the declutter line** | Logged hours are more than 15% past the hours authorised — what was quoted, plus any a change order the client accepted added. Ring the homeowner before going further — the estimate's own Terms promise them that call at exactly that point, and more hours need another change order they accept. |
| **A prep agreement says "No Transition Concierge or Property Specialist hours are billed" and the estimate prices some** | The agreement was generated before the hours were typed. Re-generate it — §3.3 states both bases once the estimate carries hours, and a contract that disagrees with its own Exhibit A must not go out. |
| **A Home Editing job is pricing concierge hours you did not expect** | Not the declutter box — that reads zero on every service but Home Prep, even with a number left in it. Check the room scores and the crew size instead. |
| **Every room is cleared and the strip still read under 100%, or said the job would run past its planned end** | It measured the work against the whole estimate, which also carries coordination and move-day hours no room holds, so it could never reach 100%. **Fixed 2026-09-29**: work done counts the rooms only, and every room cleared reads 100%. A "running late" flag you saw before then on a job with every room done was wrong. If a finished job still reads short, a room status has not been set — open the Job Plan and check each room reads Cleared. |
| **The dashboard says "no room marked done yet, so there is no progress reading"** | Hours are going in and nobody has set a room status on the Job Plan. Set each room to **Locked** once it is sorted and shot, and **Cleared** once the specialists have emptied it — the two taps in the room's workspace. The percentage appears as soon as one room is done. It withholds the figure rather than printing *0% of the work done*, because that would be a much stronger claim than the app can actually make. |
| The schedule line shows no percentages, or no verdict about the pace | It stays quiet until at least one crew-day of hours (7 combined) is logged *and* a fifth of the work is done. On the first morning the rate means nothing — one room of twelve projects to a thirteen-day job. Keep logging; it fills in. |
| A job on a **fixed price** shows no hours and no progress | Nothing about fixed price hides the hours log — it opens once the deposit is recorded and the team confirmed. Either no hours are logged yet or the team is not confirmed; the *Hours & daily close* fold says which. (Home Prep is never on a fixed price.) |
| The dashboard says the job is *tracking to* a date past the target end | At the rate it is actually going, it finishes late. That is a conversation with the client **now**, not on the target end date — re-plan, or raise a change order if the scope grew. |
| You want to know when the midpoint invoice is due and the strip says *Halfway* | That date is when (2026-09-29): the midpoint falls due on the plan's halfway day, once half the work is done, or once every room is locked, whichever comes first. Until then the band holds it as an outline button reading *due around <date>*. |
| **You hit Save Estimate and the form went blank** | Fixed 2026-09-13 — and **nothing was lost**. Save now lands you on the Client Dashboard, on the client you just priced. Before that it was trying to open a tab that had been retired, so it blanked the form and went nowhere. Open the client; the estimate is there. |
| **Save Estimate refuses and names a room** | That room's volume or complexity box is blank (or 0). Score it 1 to 5. Until 2026-09-30 a blank was quietly priced and saved as a 3. |
| **An older fixed-price estimate reopened with a different fee** | Expected (2026-09-30). Its fee carried the rush premium or the discount inside it; both are lines now, so the fee was restated — down where the premium was inside it, up where a discount was — and the client's total is unchanged. The fixed-price panel names the old fee, the new one and what the fee comes to with its lines (rounding can leave that a dollar above the old figure). Re-send the estimate if the client has seen the old split. |
| **The Pricing Reference Check says "Scored lighter than Normal"** | Information, not a warning. It is this house priced at Normal and at Full, and your scores put it where it says. Do not re-score to move it. |
| **There is no "Record the signed agreement" button and the row says *DocuSign is watching for it*** | Working as intended (new 2026-09-17). That agreement went out through DocuSign, so the signature comes back on its own and the manual button is deliberately withdrawn — two records of one contract must not be able to disagree. The button on that row is **↻ Check DocuSign now**, and opening the client checks too. |
| **The client says they signed and the app still says waiting** | Press **↻ Check DocuSign now** on the *Agreement signed* row. If it answers *held until 4:55 PM*, that is the twenty-minute limit and there is nothing wrong — come back then. If it answers that DocuSign *does not report it complete*, **the envelope is genuinely still out**: open it in DocuSign and see who has not finished — on a two-signer agreement that is often **our own countersignature**. **Do not try to record it by hand — the app will refuse.** |
| **The agreement came back signed through DocuSign and the job still sits on *Signing packet sent*** | **Fixed 2026-09-18, and it repairs itself — reload the app, then open the client.** Every agreement sent through DocuSign before that date is in this state. The send went out, the client signed, we countersigned; the app simply stopped watching the envelope and never asked. **Nothing was lost and nothing needs re-sending.** On opening the client it asks DocuSign, records the signature, and files the executed copy and certificate to Drive. If it still sits there after that, it is one of the two rows below. |
| **You pressed "Check DocuSign now" and it said it is held until a time** | That is the limit, not a fault. DocuSign allows **one status check per agreement every twenty minutes** and the penalty for asking more often is **losing our API access**, so the app holds the request and tells you when it can go. **Nothing is lost by waiting** — the signature is recorded in full the moment it is asked for. Pressing the button again in the meantime sends nothing. |
| **You completed the signature and the app did nothing, and there was no button either** | Fixed 2026-09-18. The app was inside its twenty-minute window and said nothing about it, and the row carried no control to ask with — so a completed envelope and a broken integration looked identical. The row now always carries **↻ Check DocuSign now**, and it answers either way. |
| A job that was stuck may have **two envelopes** in DocuSign | While a job was stuck the band went on offering **Send signing packet**. Pressing it again minted a second envelope for one agreement. **Check DocuSign for a duplicate on that client and void the one nobody signed**, then tell Anthony. Do not delete anything that carries a signature. |
| DocuSign says the client signed but the job is not marked signed | **Havellin countersigns second.** The envelope is not *completed* until we have signed too, and a half-executed contract is not a contract. Check the DocuSign inbox — it is usually sitting with us, not the client. **On an estate, every co-representative signs too** (since 2026-10-05): open the envelope in DocuSign to see who is still outstanding. |
| **Send for signature stopped: *"Daniel Adler has no email recorded. Add it on Edit Client, or send the PDF to sign by hand."*** | New 2026-10-05. A co-representative recorded on the estate has no email, and DocuSign cannot send them their part of the envelope. Nothing was sent. Add the email on **✎ Edit Client** and press send again, or use **📄 Send as PDF to sign by hand**; then they sign the printed page and you file it (Step 8). |
| ***Agreement signed* says *Not on record: the signature of …* in amber** | New 2026-10-05. The client has signed and a co-representative's signature is not on record: the agreement went by hand, or the envelope did not carry them. Get the page they signed and press **File the page signed by …** under the timeline (Step 8). Nothing waits on it, but do not leave it. |
| The send's notice says a co-representative *was not put on the envelope* | The Apps Script deployment is older than 2026-10-05, so DocuSign sent to the client and Anthony alone. Tell Anthony it needs redeploying. Meanwhile that co-representative signs a printed copy, and you file the page once the client has signed. |
| The send's notice says two people *are on the envelope at one email address* | New 2026-10-05. The client and a co-representative (or two co-representatives) are recorded with the same email, so both signing requests went to that inbox. Nothing was refused. If it was a slip, correct it on **✎ Edit Client** and call them; if they share an inbox, make sure each signs for themselves. |
| A notice says the signature status could not be checked | It could not reach DocuSign; the reason is in the notice. It says outright *"this is not a statement that it is not [signed]"* — because it is not. Open the client again later. Nothing was marked unsigned. |
| **An amber notice says the certificate of completion could not be retrieved** | The executed agreement *was* filed; the audit trail was not. On a probate matter that certificate is the evidence of who bound the estate, so **do not let this one slide**: press **📁 File signed copy**, in the strip under the timeline, and the app fetches it from DocuSign and files it. If it is refused, the notice names the time DocuSign can be asked again; press it then. Re-opening the client does **not** try again. If it still will not come, tell Anthony. (This row said to download it from DocuSign by hand until 2026-10-01.) |
| A client will not sign on a screen | Use **📄 Send as PDF to sign by hand** on the *Signing packet sent* row — it is offered beside the DocuSign button on every job and changes nothing for any other client. **Choose before you press:** once one route is used the other is withdrawn for that job. If you are handing them the printed packet yourself, press **🤝 Handed over in person** on the same row instead (2026-10-01). |
| You sent it the wrong way | **Tell Anthony; do not work around it.** The route is fixed once you press, on purpose. Recording a DocuSign agreement by hand, or chasing a paper one through DocuSign, is how the app and the contract end up saying different things. |
| An older job still has the old *Record the signed agreement* button | Correct. A job papered before DocuSign was switched on keeps its manual route permanently — turning DocuSign on does not strand work already in flight. |
| **A client asks what the marketing tick box is** | It is how they decline. Havellin may use photos or video from their job for marketing **unless they tick it** — leaving it blank is the yes. Ticking it changes no term of the agreement and costs them nothing. Say plainly: **no address, no names, no faces, ever**, and they can decline or change their mind later in writing whether or not they ticked it. |
| **A client signed without ticking anything and now says they did not want photos used** | Take it in writing — an email is fine — and that is as good as the box. §10.2 gives written notice exactly the same effect, and withdrawal is forward-looking, so nothing already posted has to come down. **Do not argue that they should have ticked the box.** |
| **A representative asks whether your crew sign NDAs** | Yes, and it was signed before this job existed: every person who works a Havellin engagement, staff and 1099 contractors alike, is under a **signed written agreement carrying confidentiality obligations** before they work any job at all. **Nothing gets signed on site — do not offer it.** Until 2026-09-20 §7 of the estate agreement promised a per-job NDA *before accessing the property*, which we never took; it states the standing obligation now. An agreement signed before that date needs no re-issuing — what the crew is bound by is broader than what that clause described. |
| **A representative on an estate job asks about the marketing box** | Same box, same answer as a living client — it is §7.2 on that form. It was a flat prohibition for a few hours on 2026-09-18 and is not any more. **If you are looking at an estate agreement whose §7.2 reads *Not Permitted*, it was generated in that window — regenerate and re-send it.** |
| **You want to post an estate job you have the signed authorization for** | **Two checks the contract does not make for you.** Has the house closed or been cleared? If not, wait — posting the contents of an empty estate is a targeting risk. Is the matter contested? Then no, whatever the box says. Everything else: no address, no names, no faces, no paperwork in shot. |
| **An agreement has two marketing boxes and an extra signature line** | It was generated before 2026-09-18 and DocuSign did not render those fields. **Regenerate and re-send it.** It is one opt-out tick box now. |
| **This agreement looks different from the last one I sent** | Three things came off on 2026-09-18: the boxed *IMPORTANT: Read carefully* banner at the top, the repeated *Exhibit A* paragraph at the very end, and the signatures sharing a page with the last clause. **The signature page is its own page now.** Nothing legal changed — the Exhibit A incorporation is still in §1.1. |
| **An older agreement had an initials box for photography and this one does not** | Removed 2026-09-18, deliberately. We do not ask permission to photograph a house we have been hired to inventory — the agreement states what we do with the media instead. **Marketing use is the separate question**, and it is the one optional box on either form. |
| **A client asks why the estate agreement still has a "Role / Authority" line** | Because on an estate matter that line is the **capacity that lets them bind the estate** — the same reason our side of the block says *Managing Member*. It prints the role recorded at intake, and nothing asks them to fill it in. What came off was the four-option menu it used to show when nothing was recorded. |
| **You are looking for the Signature provider setting and cannot find it** | Removed 2026-09-18. DocuSign is the firm's route on every device now; it used to be a per-device dropdown that quietly defaulted to the old email route, so an agreement once went out the old way from a browser nobody had configured. **The per-job choice is still there** — **📄 Send as PDF to sign by hand**, on the row. |
| **The button says "Send signing packet" and you expected DocuSign** | It reads **✉ Send for signature — DocuSign** when DocuSign will take it and **✉ Send signing packet by email** when it will not. If it reads *by email*, DocuSign is not configured for the firm — tell Anthony. The button naming its route was a fix: it used to read the same either way. |
| **The executed agreement did not arrive in `agreements@`** | It is copied at the end, after **every** signature (on an estate, each co-representative's too) — so nothing arrives until Havellin has countersigned. If all are done and it still has not, check that the group accepts mail from outside the organisation; that is what blocked it the first time. |
| You are looking for View / Print on a document and the strip at the foot is empty | The document for the stage you are on is in the **brown band at the top**, and its **View / Print / Edit sit on the same line as the one brown button**, to the left of it (changed 2026-09-20 — they used to be a separate row above it). The strip at the foot is the archive — documents from *earlier* stages only. On a job whose estimate is still a draft it is legitimately empty. |
| **The final invoice is not in the links at the foot of a finished job** | It is in the **big buttons at the top**. The band reads **COMPLETE** and names *Last sent — Invoice — Final*, with *View final invoice*, *Print final invoice* and *Filed final invoice*. The big buttons are always the most current stage; the links at the foot are the four documents before it, so nothing appears twice. |
| **There is no Print on the estimate** | It has not been approved yet, so the tray titles it *Client Estimate — DRAFT* and offers View and Edit only. A draft may be read on our screen and must not reach paper. Get it approved (Step 3) and Print appears. |
| The Hard target on the client details shows a plain dash | No hard target was set at intake — that is fine and not a warning. Until 2026-09-13 the app painted that dash **red and bold** on every job that simply had no hard date, which looked like a problem and was not. Set one in Edit Client if there is one. |
| **A payment you recorded is not on the client any more** | Fixed 2026-09-12, and it needs the Apps Script redeploy of that date. A client used to sync as *one record*, so the last device to save anything on that client overwrote everything the other one had done — including a recorded cheque, and the record that an invoice had gone out. It now merges payment by payment. **Record the payment again**; nothing is recoverable from before the redeploy. |
| **You deleted an hours entry and it is back** | Same date, same redeploy. The deletion never reached the sheet at all, so the line returned on the next page load — on your own device as well as everyone else's. Those hours bill the client, because the final invoice trues the labour charge to the log. **Check the hours total on any job where you deleted a line before 2026-09-12**, then delete it again. |
| The other device locked rooms and you cannot see them on the Job Plan | The tab re-reads every 20 seconds now, so give it a moment. If it still does not move: the **red unsaved-changes chip** is showing, which stands the refresh down until your own work is saved; or your cursor is in a box on the plan, which holds it off so your typing is not wiped. Click away and wait one pass. Failing both, reload the page. |
| **A vendor, a quote or the team you set on the Job Plan has gone back to how it was** | Fixed 2026-09-29, and fully from the Apps Script redeploy of 2026-09-30 — reload the page to pick up the build, then **set it again once**. The other device’s next save used to write its earlier copy of the client back over yours; an edit to a *different* client was enough, and so was merely opening the client while the agreement or a payment link was out. Once that redeploy is live, each vendor line, each part of the team, the ratings and each house-checklist row merges on its own, so two people changing different ones both land. If it still happens, the redeploy has not been done yet: the out-of-date banner at the top of the app says so. |
| The chip's detail (tap it), or the badge that flashes bottom-right, says *busy* | The sheet was holding another save for 20 seconds, so this one was not written. **Nothing is lost** — it is sent again on its own, and meanwhile the chip itself reads *N unsaved changes — retrying…*. It clears within a minute or two. This answer comes with the Apps Script redeploy of 2026-09-30, so you will not see it until that is live. |
| The chip's detail (tap it), or the badge that flashes bottom-right, says *STORE_UNREADABLE* | One of the spreadsheet's store tabs holds damaged data, so the sheet refused to read it or write over it. **Your change is held on this device and keeps retrying — do not reload or close the tab.** Tell Anthony: the named tab is restored from **File → Version history** in the spreadsheet, and the waiting saves then go through by themselves. This refusal comes with the Apps Script redeploy of 2026-09-30; before it, a damaged tab is emptied by the next save instead, and the out-of-date banner says so. |
| The other device set a vendor or confirmed the team and you cannot see it on the Job Plan | The 20-second refresh brings rooms, notes, ticks and hours — not vendors or the team, which are saved on the client. **Reload the page.** Do it before you change either yourself: until the Apps Script redeploy of 2026-09-30 is live your older copy takes the whole client, and after it the line you change still starts from your older copy. |
| The Job Plan redrew itself while you were looking at it | That is the 2026-09-12 refresh, and it only happens when something actually changed on the other device. It will not do it while you are typing. Other tabs do not do this at all. |
| Half the tabs are missing and the nav is at the bottom | You are in **field mode** — the **📱 Field** button in the header is filled bronze. It keeps three tabs (Clients · Job Plan · Vendors) and hides the three that are desk work (Job Admin & Inv, Contractors, Referral Partners). Intake and Estimate are not tabs at all any more — **+ Add New Client** and **Build estimate** on the Client Dashboard reach them. Nothing is lost; press it again to come back. |
| You scored the walkthrough on the phone and cannot find Save | It is at the foot of Build Estimate, under the rooms, beside **Reset**: press **Save Estimate** there. It saves everything, the totals the phone hides included, and a refusal (a room left unscored, a contract question unanswered) prints right under it. Since 2026-09-30; before that field mode hid Save with the totals, and a walkthrough scored on a phone stayed unsaved until **Field · exit**. |
| **The Client Estimate / Agreement / Invoices tab is gone** | Retired 2026-09-11. Everything they did is on the **job timeline** — open the client on the Client Dashboard and look at the row for the step you want. Nothing was removed. |
| You sent the email but the step has not gone green, and the row says *"Drafted — read it, send it, then confirm"* | You have not pressed **✓ I've sent it**. The app can only put a draft in your Gmail; it cannot see that you sent it. Press it. On the estimate this is what **Mark Won** is waiting for. |
| The row says *"Opened as a plain email — send it from your mail app, then confirm"* | Gmail failed, so the app opened a plain email instead. It carries no attachment: attach the printed PDF, send it from your mail app, then press **✓ I've sent it**. (Before 2026-09-30 a plain email recorded nothing and there was no tap to press.) |
| **✓ I’ve sent it** disappeared after a discount or after *Edit estimate* | Correct (2026-09-29). The draft it would have confirmed has the old price in it, and one tap used to record that old-price document as sent. The row names the draft — the day and the Gmail account. Delete it in Gmail and press **Send** again once the manager re-approves. |
