| The dashboard names a Gmail draft and says to delete it | The price changed after that draft was made. **Delete it; do not send it.** The app never touches your Gmail, so it cannot do this for you, and the draft may be in a colleague’s account. If it already reached the client, tell them a revised one is coming. |
| There are two drafts of the same document in Gmail | The older one was made before a price change. The notice after the fresh send says which to delete (*“Delete the older Gmail draft from …”*), and the row says it again until you confirm the new one. Delete the older, send the newer, tap **✓ I’ve sent it**. |
| A message above the timeline flashed and vanished after *Submit for approval* or a discount | Fixed 2026-09-29. Both start the manager-approval check, which refreshed the dashboard a second later and took the message with it. It stays now until you press or open something else. |
| The row of buttons across the top of the Client Dashboard has gone | Changed 2026-09-11, and nothing was lost. Five of the seven were already further down the same screen. **Estimate · Submit for Approval · Mark Won · Activate Job** are rows on the **timeline** — open the client and look at the lit row. **Change Order** is the **+ New** in the Change Orders card. **✎ Edit Client** and **📁 Drive** are on the *Job Timeline & Payments* heading. |
| You are looking for **Activate Job** | It is a row on the timeline (*Job active*), and it only shows a button when the job can actually be activated. If there is no button there, the row says what is blocking it — usually the signed agreement, the deposit, or executor authorisation on a probate matter. The old top-of-screen button offered itself even when the job was blocked, which is why it went. Pressing it takes you to that client's Job Plan (2026-09-23). |
| A job will not activate, and you want to start the pre-job work | You already can. **Marking the client Won** is what opens staffing, the Job Plan and photo capture — none of that waits for money. Only **logging hours** waits for the deposit, and that gate has no override. *Active* is a status marker, not a permission. |
| When does a client's Drive folder get created? | **At the moment you save a new client (+ Add New Client → Save Client)** — the folder and all seven subfolders in one go. Not at approval, not at activation. **📁 Drive** is only ever a link to it; there is nothing to press to create one. |
| **The ✕ has gone off a client row** | That job is finished — the work was delivered or the final payment is recorded — and the ✕ is the *mark lost* button. A delivered job cannot be lost, so it is withdrawn from 2026-09-22. The client is still listed and **Open →** still works. If the final invoice is outstanding, that is collections: record the payment when it arrives. |
| **A client you marked Lost is not in the list** | Every other filter excludes a lost job. Press the **Lost** filter. The ✕ is still on the row there, so the reason and note can be corrected. |
| You cannot find a button you are sure exists | Open the client and look at the **lit row**. Only one step is live at a time and its buttons are drawn in the band, once — not on every row. If nothing is lit, the job is finished or lost. |
| The agreement is waiting for a manager PIN | It is not — that PIN was removed 2026-09-10. If the packet will not send, one of three things is outstanding: the estimate is not approved, the client is not marked **Won**, or the price went up after their yes and they have not accepted the new figure (step 5). The lit step is the one to do, and a refused send names which. |
| The red **⚠ Agreement not signed** chip by the status says to approve the agreement | It said that until 2026-09-30, on every job, for a step that does not exist (the row above). Its tooltip now names what is actually missing: the estimate or the client's yes, then sending the packet, then recording the signature — or that DocuSign is watching for it. The lit step on the timeline says the same thing, on screen. |
| A final you had approved asks for the manager PIN again | The balance moved after the PIN — more hours logged, a change order accepted, or a payment recorded. The refusal names who approved it, at what figure, and what it asks for now. A manager looks at the new figure and enters the PIN again. If the change was a mistake, correct it and the approval on file counts again. |
| **🔑 Manager approval** says the final is already approved | It is, at today's figure, and it names who approved it and when. Send it. |
| The *Approved for Sending* band on an agreement names somebody else | Fixed 2026-09-29: it used to name whichever agreement the open page had approved last. Reload to pick up the current build and regenerate the agreement; the band names that client's own approval now, and it is kept out of the printed copy and the PDF. |
| *Who signed it?* — do you put your own name in | **No.** It is the **client** who signed the contract; it prefills the authorised representative. Who recorded it is captured separately, automatically. It will not accept a blank. |
| An old job shows *"Signed before the signature record — who signed it is not on file"* | Correct, and nothing to fix. Before 2026-09-11 the app never recorded the signer. **Do not type a name in from memory** — an unknown signer is an honest record. |
| There is no *Save to Drive* button on a document | There is nothing to press — documents file themselves. **📁 File … to Drive** (*File estimate to Drive*, *File deposit invoice to Drive*) only appears when the automatic filing did *not* land, and it goes away once it works. It is not offered on an estimate back with the manager after a discount or an edit: approve it first. **📁 Filed estimate**, *Filed packet* and the rest open what is already there, each naming its document (2026-09-30; until then they all read *Filed copy*). |
| A document in a client's Drive folder names a different client | A bug fixed 2026-09-11: the estimate and agreement used to be filed by reading whatever the old tab was showing, so filing from the dashboard with another client loaded wrote *their* document into *this* folder — and reported success. Re-file it from the timeline and delete the stray. Worth a look in any folder used before that date. |
| An invoice you emailed before 2026-09-11 had no attachment | It never did. The old invoice email was plain text and said *"Please find attached"* with nothing attached. Re-send it from the timeline; it carries the PDF now. |
| A date on the timeline reads *Invalid Date* | A display bug fixed 2026-09-11. Reload the page to pick up the current build. The underlying date is fine — nothing was lost and nothing needs re-entering. |
| Somebody found cash, a gun or a safe that intake never mentioned | Nothing moves. Put it on the record through **Edit Client** — tick the row, write what you found and where — and it appears on the Job Plan brief for everyone else. A firearm additionally waits for **written authority** naming who may take possession — and, if it is going to travel in one of our cars, naming it by serial. The app will not release one without the authority, and neither should you. |
| A flag on the crew brief reads *"Ticked at intake, no detail recorded"* | Somebody ticked the box on the intake call and never wrote the answer down. Ring the client and get it **before Day 1**, then fix it on Edit Client. The app prints the row rather than hiding it precisely so this is visible while it is still cheap. |
| The Job Plan shows no Standing job flags panel at all | Either nothing was ticked and neither question was answered, or the job predates 2026-09-10, when the questions were added. Nothing is broken — an empty panel would just be noise. Fill it in on **Edit Client** and it appears. |
| The Standing job flags panel on the Job Plan no longer lists firearms | Correct since 2026-09-20 — the red banner above it *is* the firearms row, with the note, the rule and the protocol link, so the panel stopped repeating it. The client dashboard's panel still lists it. If firearms was the only flag, the plan shows the banner and no panel. |
| You are looking for the old free-text *Notes* box on intake | Still there, at the bottom of the first card, under the two questions and the checklist. It is for what the questions do not cover — family dynamics, urgency, who really decides. What used to go in it about the *house* now has proper rows of its own. |
| A client asks whether you mark up the vendors | Straight answer, and it is on every document: **no — the vendor bills them at the vendor's own price and we add nothing to that invoice.** On home prep vendors we charge a separate, clearly-shown **30% Home Sale Preparation Fee** for sourcing, scheduling, on-site oversight and quality control. Both things are true at once; do not say only the first half. |
| The job is not the service type intake said it was | Change it in the **service type** dropdown on Build Estimate (from 2026-09-10). Everything you have scored is kept and the price re-runs. It offers living services on a living job and estate services on an estate job, and never both. |
| The service dropdown does not offer the type you want | You are trying to cross the living/deceased line — a living client cannot become an Estate Settlement here, because nobody has been asked for a representative or a date of death. That is a different client: **+ Add New Client**, which asks for them. Edit Client refuses the switch too (*Create a new client for this*, since 2026-09-30). |
| Edit Client says *Create a new client for this* | You changed a living client to an estate service, or the reverse. They are different people: the estate has a representative and a date of death, the living client has their own phone and email. Use **+ Add New Client** (2026-09-30). |
| Edit Client says *Please complete: …* and nothing saved | The save would have cleared a required field. Put it back (or correct it) and save again. A field that was **already** blank on an older client never stops a save: it is named after the save instead (*Saved. Still missing on this client: …*). |
| A red line under the dates, on intake or Edit Client | The start is after the hard target, or before the walkthrough. Both dates are kept; move one, or agree a new date with the client. Until 2026-09-30 the app cleared one of them without a word. |
| Intake says *Already on file at this address* | Somebody at that street is already a client. If it is the same person, go back and open them from the dashboard; a second job at the same house is fine and saving goes ahead. |
| The service dropdown is greyed out | Either the estimate is out for manager approval, or it is approved, or the agreement is signed. Out for approval: wait for the manager's PIN or a deny — *Edit estimate* is withheld until then (2026-09-29). Approved: press **✎ Edit estimate** on the timeline and it opens again (you will need to re-approve). Once the signing packet has been *sent* it is a **Change Order**, not an edit. |
| You changed the service type and the total jumped | Expected — that is the point. The services price differently, and coming off Home Prep in particular switches the job from a fee on vendor spend to billed concierge and specialist hours *plus* that fee. Read the Pricing Reference Check before you submit. |
| A prep vendor you entered before today is now earning a fee it did not before | Changed 2026-09-10 — bundled prep carries 30% now and books no coordination hours. If the client is holding an estimate issued before that date, it was quoted the old way and reopening it moves the total up. Re-send after re-approval rather than letting the invoice arrive higher than the quote. |
| The *Coord hrs* box is gone from your prep vendors | Deliberate, 2026-09-10. Prep books no coordination hours on any job now, so there is no estimated figure for a recorded one to be checked against. The box is still there on every other vendor. |
| You switched a job to Contested Probate and the case fields vanished | Fixed 2026-09-10 — a real bug. On **Edit Client**, switching to Contested Probate used to hide the case number, the deadline, the attorney *and* the authorised representative, while still saving whatever was in them. Nothing was ever lost; you just could not see or correct it. They stay on screen now. |
| Your mobile is not on the estimate or the email you just sent | It comes from the phone field on your row in the **Contractors** tab. Add it there and it appears on every client document and email from then on. Until you do, the client gets the office line only — which works, it is just less direct. |
| A client document shows a phone number that rings nowhere | The old office line was *(561) 370-4700* and it is retired. The app refuses to print it as anybody's mobile, so if you are seeing it, you are looking at a document generated before 2026-09-09 — regenerate it. The office is now **(561) 652-5522**. |
| There used to be a *Plain email* button and now there isn't | Deliberate. It carried no attachment, and having it beside the real button invited sending the wrong one. The app still falls back to a plain email on its own if Gmail cannot be used, and tells you that is what it did. |
| agreements@ never got a copy of an agreement you sent | Before 2026-09-08 there was no working agreement email at all — the button was in the app and did nothing. Nothing was lost on your side; the copy simply never went. From now on both the Gmail draft and the plain email CC it automatically. |
| The worksheet in a Home Prep client's Estimate folder is blank | Fixed 2026-09-08, and on 2026-09-30 for a prep estimate with declutter hours, which until then still filed an empty room table with no vendor list. A prep job scores no rooms, so the old worksheet rendered an empty table; it now lists the prep vendors, with the declutter hours on a line under them. Re-approve the estimate and it re-files with the vendor lines and the fee on it. (And check the folder holds the client estimate too — see the row below about a table of rooms and hours.) |
| **✉ Send…** opened a plain-text email instead of the formatted one | The app fell back, and it said why in the notice above the timeline. Almost always the Google sign-in window was closed before it finished — press the button again and complete it. (Gmail is set up in the app already; nothing to configure on your device.) The plain email works; you just have to attach the PDF yourself. |
| The Gmail draft was created but the PDF is not attached | The PDF conversion failed and the app told you so rather than dropping the email. The body still carries the summary. Press **🖨 Print …** on that document, save it as a PDF, attach it to the draft by hand, and send. |
| The Gmail draft opened in the wrong Google account | Gmail opens the first account signed in on that browser. Switch account in Gmail and the draft is there — it was created in whichever account you picked in the Google window, and that is the one it will send from. |
| Google says the Gmail API is not enabled | A one-time setup step on the Google Cloud project, not something on this device. Send it to Anthony — it is in §2 of the manual. Until it is done, **✉ Send…** falls back to a plain email on its own, with nothing attached: print the document and attach it by hand. |
| The *Signing packet sent* row will not let you send | The client has not been recorded as accepting. Press **✓ Client accepted — mark won** on the *Client accepted* row (or **✓ Client accepted the revised price**, after a raise; step 5). Until then the packet is a PREVIEW: you can read it with them, but not print, send or file it, and nothing is filed until the first print or send. |
| The agreement was approved and now says the approval was withdrawn | Somebody edited the estimate, or a discount changed the price. The agreement attaches the estimate as Exhibit A, so it cannot stay approved against a version that is changing. Settle the estimate and get it re-approved, then send a fresh packet — there is no separate agreement approval to press (the first print, send or filing stamps it), and Drive is refreshed for you. |
| The estimate PDF in a client's Drive folder is a table of rooms and hours | That is our internal worksheet, and before 2026-09-08 it shared a filename with the client estimate so it sometimes won. They are separate files now: approving or sending the estimate files the client version again. |
| A Home Prep estimate talked about hours, or a 15% overrun | Fixed 2026-09-08. A vendors-only Home Prep estimate bills no hours — the fee is 30% of what the vendors actually invoice. If you are holding a prep estimate issued before that date, re-open it and re-approve so the client gets the corrected terms. |
| The Appraisal Worklist says nothing is flagged — is the estate clear? | Not necessarily, and this is the trap. That line answers the *per-item* test only. Read the **§20.2031-6(b)** box underneath it: art, jewellery, silver, antiques, rugs and collections are added together, and over $3,000 in total the estate owes an expert appraisal under oath with its federal return — even when no single piece was ever flagged. |
| The aggregate box says "cannot be tested yet" | Some MAIV articles still have no value in them. The total you can see is a floor, not a total, so the app will not give you a verdict. Get values against them; that is the whole job at that point. |
| You found a fur coat / a wall of old books and there is no category for them | There isn't one, deliberately. Leave the category as General/Household, set **MAIV** to *Yes*, and pick *Furs* or *Books & manuscripts* as the class. The regulation names both by name. |
| Trust items are on the aggregate but not on the Court Inventory | Correct, and both are right. The Court Inventory is the probate schedule and trust property is outside it. The federal aggregate counts the *gross estate*, and a revocable trust's contents are in the gross estate. Two documents, two questions. |
| A firearm you flagged isn't on the Appraisal Worklist | Correct, and deliberate. Nothing goes on that list until the representative has authorised the transfer to a **named** dealer in writing. Select the line and press **Record approval** when the signed authority is back (it writes *Authorized By* and *Approval Date*), with the dealer in *Channel / Recipient* (on a firearm going to a named person, on its dealer route), and it appears. A name left in *Channel / Recipient* from when the line went *To a person* is not a dealer (since P22): the row reads *still names …* until you type the dealer or press **Keep the name** in its record. The worklist prints a note saying what it is holding, so a held item never reads as a forgotten one. |
| A Job Plan stage will not stay open — it shuts again within seconds | Fixed 2026-09-11; **reload the page** to pick up the current build. The tab was redrawing itself over and over because it mistook "the sheet has photos for this job" for "something changed", and every redraw shut every phase. Nothing you did was lost — room statuses, notes and ticks were all saving normally underneath it. Phases now stay as you left them for the rest of the session. |
| Somebody else locked rooms on this job plan and you cannot see it | Until 2026-09-11 the sheet kept only the *newest whole plan*, so whoever saved last wiped the other person's rooms. It now merges room by room. **If it happened before that fix, the locks are gone rather than hidden** — re-check the rooms and lock them again. Reload the page first so you are looking at the current copy. |
| A shot in the room's strip says *Uploading…* and never finishes | It gives up after 90 seconds now and turns into *⚠ shot N not saved* with a **Retry**. The photo is still on the device — it is the connection to Google that failed, usually the same cause as a red sync chip bottom-left. Fix that, then press Retry. On a build before 2026-09-11 it would sit there for the rest of the job; reload to pick up the current one. |
| Red *not saved* flags on a room you have already re-photographed | Each flag is its own shot, so a new photo does not clear an old failure. Press **Retry** to send it, or the **🗑️** on its thumbnail to delete it. **🗑️ throws the image away** and asks first — only use it once you have re-taken the room. An as-found shot cannot be deleted at all: retry it, or shoot another beside it. *(Until 2026-09-20 the strip had a **✕** beside the flag instead.)* A failed **detail** shot has its own tile under *Detail shots not saved* since 2026-09-30, with Retry and 🗑; before, it had neither and its flag could never clear. |
| The chip reads *check the Apps Script URL and its access* | The save reached the script but not the part of it that writes — so **a re-deploy will not fix this one**, whatever it may have said before 2026-09-11. It is the **Settings → Google Sheets Sync** URL or the deployment's access setting. Pass it to whoever manages the Apps Script; your work is held on the device until it clears, so **do not close the tab**. |
| The chip reads *Apps Script needs redeploying* | Different thing, and this one is real: the deployed script is older than the app and is missing the line that handles this save. Somebody needs to paste `apps-script/main-sync.gs` **from the `main` branch** into the editor and do **Deploy → Manage deployments → New version**. Saving in the editor is not deploying, and *New deployment* mints a different URL that Settings is not pointed at. |
| A red chip bottom-left reads *N unsaved changes — retrying…* | A save did not reach the sheet. **Nothing is lost** — it is held and re-sent on its own, and the chip goes when it lands. **Tap the chip** to see which save it is and what the server said. Carry on working; it usually clears itself within a minute. |
| The chip reads *still failing after N attempts* | Then it is not a blip, and it will not fix itself. Two things cause it: the **Apps Script URL in Settings is stale**, or the script was deployed with anything other than **Who has access: Anyone**. Tell whoever manages the Apps Script, and **until it clears, that work exists only on the device in your hand — do not close or reload the tab.** Retrying carries on in the background the whole time. |
| The browser asks whether you really want to leave the page | You have a save that has not reached the sheet yet — look bottom-left for the red chip. **Stay**, and wait for it to go. Closing the tab throws that work away: the queue lives in the page, and the next load takes the sheet's copy over this device's. |
| You open an older job and its inventory list is *empty* | This device ran out of room and cleared that job's list to make space for the one you were working on — you would have seen a badge saying so. **Nothing was lost:** it is on the sheet. Get a connection and open the job again and it comes back. Offline, it will keep reading empty until you have signal. |
| A badge reads *cleared N KB of other jobs off this device* | That is the app making room so the inventory you are working on could save. It only ever clears jobs the sheet already has, and it never touches a photo that failed to upload. Carry on. If you see it often, this device is holding several finished estates — **Settings → This Device → Clear** tidies it properly. |
| Photographs fail on site — every shot says *"not saved"* | The client has no Google Drive folder. It is made when the new client is saved, and very occasionally that one attempt does not land. Open the client on the Client Dashboard and press **Create Drive folder**, then press **Retry** on the shot in the room's workspace — the photos are still held on the device and go up as soon as the folder exists. |
| You can't find the **NFA Item** tick box | It only appears on rows whose category is *Firearms*. Set the category first. On anything else the column shows blank on purpose — an NFA tick on a sofa describes nothing. |
| A cheap gun never showed up anywhere on the worklist | Anything under $3,000 with a value recorded isn't flagged for a specialist, so it was never worklist-bound. It *is* still listed on the awaiting-authority notice — that notice covers **every** firearm without written authority, not just the expensive ones. |
| You found a metal tube in a gun safe and don't know what it is | Treat it as a firearm. Photograph it, touch nothing, flag it, tell your TC the same day. It may be a suppressor, which is an NFA item and a different transfer path entirely. Nobody is ever criticised for over-flagging this. |
| A firearm going to a family member reads *Going to a named person* and will not clear | Record its dealer route: open the line (▾), and in its *Firearm* block press **Through a licensed dealer…** and name the dealer who will make the transfer (new 2026-09-30). It then needs written authority and a serial like any other, and Anthony alone carries it to that dealer. An NFA item is offered no route — the dealer collects it. |
| A firearm reads *cleared to carry* and you are not sure what that means | New on 2026-09-13. It has written authority, a serial and a named dealer (on one going to a named person, the dealer on its route), and it is not NFA — so **Anthony** may drive it to that dealer in a locked case. Nobody else at Havellin transports firearms, and the old rule that the dealer always collects no longer applies to ordinary guns. If you are not Anthony, the answer is still that the dealer collects. |
| The worklist blocks a firearm and says *no serial number recorded* | Fill in **Serial Number** on the inventory row. The authorisation names each gun by serial, so an item without one cannot have been named in the document that makes carrying it lawful — and the dealer's receipt comes back serial by serial and has to match the manifest. Photograph the serial in the house; once the gun has gone nobody can read it again. |
| An NFA item has authority, a serial and a dealer and the app still refuses to release it for transport | Correct, and that arm has **no override and never will**. No representative can authorise it — the registration is federal and not theirs to assign. The dealer collects it from the property. Do not look for a way round this one; there is not one, and the penalty is not a fine. |
| Somebody suggests taking a cut of what the guns sell for | No, and this is the thing that would genuinely get Havellin in trouble — far more than the drive. **Never buy an estate firearm and never take a percentage of firearm proceeds.** Bill the hours, like everything else. Buying-and-reselling or earning on the sale is what turns a concierge into an unlicensed dealer. |
| A representative asks how long the NFA item will take | Don't give a number. This guide said "months" until 2026-09-13 and that is now wrong — a Form 5 clears in about two days electronically, while a Form 4 to a dealer does not. Say the item is on a separate federal path and let the dealer give the date. |
| You want the whole firearms procedure, standing in the house | Open the Job Plan and tap **Read the firearms protocol →** on the red firearms row; the Appraisal Worklist carries the same link. It opens in a new tab, needs no sign-in and works on a bad signal. Six stages, the NFA check, the seven nevers and the kit. |
| You took photos and nothing happened — no count, no flag, no message | Fixed 2026-08-03. The app required the job to be *Active* and it was only *Won*, so it discarded the shot silently. It captures from *Won* now, and says so out loud if it ever does refuse. **Photos taken in that window before that date are not in Drive** — re-shoot if you still can. |
| A room's strip shows a red **⚠ not saved** | That upload failed and the photo is not in Drive. Hit **Retry** on that shot — it works across sessions now, so yesterday's failure can still be sent today. |
| Retry says the photo can no longer be retried | The device had no room to hold the image. It's gone — take the shot again. |
| A job's photo counts all read zero when you first open it | Fixed 2026-08-03. The cards were drawn before the photo records loaded. Everything was in Drive the whole time; switching away and back used to show the real counts. |
| A client's *Estimates* folder has the same estimate in it several times | Pressing the old *Save to Drive* button repeatedly, before 2026-09-09, added a copy each time instead of replacing one. **Approve the estimate once more and the folder collapses back to a single file** — the extras go to Drive's trash and are recoverable for 30 days. Tell Anthony if it does not, because the Apps Script may not have been redeployed. |
| After **✉ Send…**, Gmail shows an error — *"account is not available"* or *Temporary Error (404)* | Fixed 2026-09-09 (second attempt). **The draft was created every time** — only the link opening it was wrong. Reload to pick up the current build. The notice above the timeline names the mailbox the draft is in (*Draft created in …*); open it with **↗ Open the … draft** beside the document, and if Gmail opens a different account, switch with the avatar top-right. |
| Gmail opened, but it is the wrong Google account and there is no draft | The link can only open whichever Google account signed in first in that browser. Switch accounts with the avatar top-right — the notice above the timeline names the mailbox the draft was created in, and it is in **Drafts**. |
| The Gmail draft has no PDF attached | First check you are not looking at an **old draft** — a draft is a snapshot and never updates itself, so delete it and press **✉ Send…** again. If the new one still has none, the notice above the timeline says so: press **🖨 Print …**, save the PDF and attach it by hand before sending. |
| The *Print Job Plan* button has disappeared | Retired on 2026-09-11, deliberately. It printed **nine Letter pages** on an 18-room job — every room card twice, once for the sort grid and again for the pack grid — and those cards are status and Take Photo *buttons*, which do nothing on paper. Nothing on screen changed; the plan, the ticks and the statuses are all still there. If it ever returns it will be a one-page brief. |
| You want the Standing job flags on paper for the crew | There is no paper route now — it went with the print. The panel is the first thing on the Job Plan tab, so open the job on your phone in the driveway and **read it aloud**. If somebody genuinely needs it on a sheet, write the firearms line out by hand; do not send a crew in without it having been said. |
| A non-estate job's plan looks much shorter than an estate one | Correct, and by design. Every labour service runs the **same stages in the same order**; the estate rigor is *added* on top, not a different plan. Estate Settlement and both probates add the in-house documentation boxes under the room grid (firearms photographed where they lie, each checked for NFA, documents sequestered, cash logged) and the *written release authority* line under Midpoint & pickups; probate adds the Letters and §733.604 deadline chips at the top and the *Florida court & legal compliance* list under Job Admin on the Job Admin & Inv tab. Measured on one six-room house after the 2026-09-19 cut: Home Editing 9 boxes, Home Cleanout 13, Estate Settlement 17, Probate 18 — Home Transition 28, because Move Day is a real list — and most of what used to be a box is now a line the app reads for itself. **Home Prep for Sale is the one genuinely different plan** — no stages, no room list, and no hours log unless hours were quoted or a change order added some (see *Home Prep for Sale — the short version*, step 6). |
| A room reads *Pending* though you have finished it | Nothing marks a room done by itself. Open the room and tap **Lock** (sorted and shot) or **Cleared** (emptied, and the After shot taken). Rooms are drawn once now, between *Before Day 1* and *Hours & daily close* — there is no Phase 2 grid to look at. **⚠ One real exception** still stands: anything locked on the OTHER device before the Apps Script redeploy of 2026-09-11 is genuinely gone — re-lock it once and it will hold. |
| **Lock** is greyed out and the room says *Shoot the room as found first* | It is an estate or probate job and the room has no as-found shot. Open **📷 As found** and shoot it as it sits — from the door, then every drawer and cupboard open — and Lock comes live. On a living-client job the same case only flags in amber. |
| There is no **Cleared** button | Lock the room first. Cleared is only offered on a locked room, and it flags you if there is no After shot yet. |
| The camera closed after one shot, or the phone's own camera opened | The site was refused camera access, so the same screen fell back to the phone's camera one shot at a time. Everything still files. To get the stay-open viewfinder back, allow the camera for this site in the browser's settings and reopen the room. |
| The *Florida court & legal compliance* boxes are not on a probate job | The matter type is recorded as **Trust** or **Neither**, or the tier is **None**. Read the two lines at the top of the Job Admin card: *Matter type recorded* and *Contracted to produce*. If the matter really is a trust, the list is correctly absent — the trustee's schedule is a separate build and the desk is not asked to verify a document that does not exist. If nobody answered it, the line says so; fix it on Edit Client. |
| An Estate Settlement has suddenly grown a court checklist | Somebody recorded the matter type as **Probate** or **Both**, which is what it keys on since 2026-09-21. That is the fix, not a fault — an Estate Settlement administering a probate estate was getting no compliance list at all. *Personal Representative authority confirmed* appears on the Job Plan for the same reason. |
| A probate job lost *All professional appraisals attached* | The engagement tier and the estimate decide it. A job recorded before 2026-09-21 reads as *Inventory with values*, which does not make appraisals ours. Nothing is wrong: the box comes straight back the moment an appraiser is on the roster or on the approved estimate (a line under Vendors, since 2026-09-30), and it is always there at *Inventory + appraisals*. |
| A values-tier agreement says appraisals are *not within this engagement* | Correct, from 2026-09-24. On *Inventory with values* counsel arranges the appraisals; the agreement and the estimate used to promise we would, because both top tiers priced the same. If the attorney wants us to coordinate one or two, add each appraiser under Vendors on Build Estimate: since 2026-09-30 the agreement then says Havellin coordinates *the professional appraisals listed in Exhibit A* and counsel arranges any other. If they want us to arrange all of them, change the tier to *Inventory + appraisals* in Edit Client. Either way, re-price, re-approve and regenerate. |
| The Estimate Summary shows *Inventory scheduling* hours on an estate job (it used to read *Appraiser & inventory scheduling*) | Expected. Since 2026-09-30 the documentation step's coordination is **inventory scheduling only** (Anthony's decision): full scope prices half of what the old line did, capture scope none of it. An appraiser is priced only by its own line, added under Vendors on Build Estimate, on any tier — no tier requires or refuses one. Do not score the job down to take the line off. At *Inventory + appraisals* with no appraiser on the estimate, the scope hint says so in amber: add each appraiser under Vendors. |
| An appraiser line on a **Premium Estate** estimate books its 2 hours again | Correct since 2026-10-01. For one day (2026-09-30) it booked none, because Premium's flat 25 hours of specialty coordination were taken to cover the appraisers. Those 25 hours are gone — Premium is the higher rates only — so every vendor line books its own hours on every job, the appraiser's 2 at the Premium rate. Anthony: *“if we do use any of these, hours should be assumed and billed for”*. |
| A Premium Estate estimate came out thousands lower than it used to | Correct since 2026-10-01. Premium no longer adds 25 concierge hours of its own; it bills the hours a standard estimate does, at $185 / $125. On a 3,500 sqft Estate Settlement that is $22,350 where it was $26,975 (re-measured 2026-10-02 on whole hours; $22,071.25 on P17's quarter hours). If the job uses an auction house, an appraiser or an online auction, add each as a vendor line: that is where its hours come from now. |
| Where do I type the object's name? | You don't, in the house. Shoot it, chip it, talk the note if there is one. It arrives on the Job Admin & Inv tab under *Unnamed shots*, and naming it there takes seconds with the photo in front of you. |
| The *NDA* box is gone from Before Day 1 | Deliberately (2026-09-20). Havellin does not take a per-job NDA — confidentiality is in the written contractor agreement every crew member signs **before they work an engagement at all**, so there was never anything to collect on the day. Anthony: *"we do not require NDAs for staff on all jobs. confidentiality is baked into our 1099 employment agreements."* The estate agreement's §7 was corrected the same day — it used to promise the representative a per-job NDA. **Nothing else on Before Day 1 moved**; every labour job is simply one box lighter. |
| The *Midpoint invoice sent / received* boxes are gone from the Job Plan | Deliberately (2026-09-19). Both lines are now read off the invoice record and the payments — send the invoice from the band on the Client Dashboard, record the cheque there, and the two lines under *Midpoint & pickups* turn green on their own. A box you could tick without sending anything was the defect. |
| Where did the financial close and the court-filing checklist go? | Under **Job Admin**, folded at the top of the Job Admin & Inv tab. Tap the fold. |
| A big red *FIREARMS IN THIS HOUSE* banner is on the Job Plan | Intake flagged firearms for this client. Read it to the crew, read the red line every time you open a room, and follow Step 10f. Nobody on the crew touches one. |
| Every box you ticked on the plan last week is unticked | Ticks made on a build before 2026-09-19 do not carry: the boxes were numbered by their position in the list and are now named, so an old tick has nothing to attach to. Room statuses, notes, hours, photos and everything on the Job Admin & Inv tab carried. Re-tick what still applies — most of the old list is gone, or is now a line the app reads for itself. |
| There is no **Court Inventory** under *More* on an estate job | Same reason: that schedule states a value on every line, and this engagement does not include valuation. The §733.604 inventory is the estate attorney’s to prepare and file. Hand them the **Contents List**; at *None* they are working from their own schedule already. |
| A Court Inventory prints **DRAFT** and you want to know what to do about it | Read the red block at the foot — it names the reason *and* what clears it, and the two are not always the same thing. *Nothing on the probate schedule* is fixed by the Asset Track, not by valuing anything. *Pending appraisal* on a values-tier job is counsel’s to arrange, not ours — unless the appraiser is on the estimate: then the block says Havellin schedules it where it covers the item and the attorney arranges any other (2026-09-30). On a trust matter **no final copy is coming at all** — that document is the wrong instrument for the estate. |
| You are on an estate job and there is no **Estate Inventory PDF** button | The job is contracted at **Contents list**, so the primary button is **Contents List** instead — a photographed, room-by-room list with no values on it. That is what the client signed for and what counsel is expecting. If you believe the tier is wrong, fix it on *Edit Client*; do not send the valued schedule instead. |
| The **Contents List** shows no values and the attorney asks why | Because that engagement does not include valuation, and the agreement says so in two places. The document states it on its own face. Hand it over as it is. |
| The **Contents List** names a room and says *nothing listed* | Nothing on the manifest carries that room. Either it genuinely holds nothing of consequence, or the shots went in under the wrong room — open it on the Job Plan and check. A room you excluded from the engagement is not in that list; it is reported separately as out of scope. |
| The **As-Found Record** names a room in red that you know you shot | Those photographs did not reach Drive. Open that room on the Job Plan and press **Retry** on the flagged shots, then print it again. A shot that was taken but never saved is also listed on its own, with no link — same fix. |
| A room reads *Pass not confirmed complete* on the As-Found Record | Nobody ticked **As-found pass complete** in that room. If the drawers and closets really were all opened, open the room and tick it — your name goes on it. If they were not, leave it: the line is true and the representative should see it. |
| The representative asks for a photograph of a drawer and you cannot find it | Print the **As-Found Record** (Job Admin & Inv → More). It indexes every as-found shot by room and time with a link to each file. If the room is in the red block at the top, it was never shot — say so rather than hunting. |
| As-found shots are not in the *As-Found Record* Drive folder | That job's folder was created before 2026-09-20 and has no such subfolder, so they are still in **Estate Inventory** with everything else. Nothing is wrong and nothing is lost. To split it, make a folder called exactly *As-Found Record* in that client's Drive folder; the app finds it on the next shot. |
| A saved PDF is called *Havellin Palm Beach — Job Manager* | You saved it before 2026-09-20, when none of the inventory documents named themselves. Print it again and it arrives named properly — the document itself is unchanged. |
| Four rows in the manifest share one photograph | Somebody split that shot into several objects, which is how it is meant to work — the row says *1 of 4 in this photo*. Do not delete the duplicates; they are four different things in one picture. |
| **➕ Another item in this photo** is not offered on a line | Either it is a hand-typed line with no photograph (cash, an account) or the shot has not reached Drive yet. For the second, press **Retry** on it first. |
| A room card says *1 items* when the manifest shows four | Correct. The room card counts photographs taken; the manifest counts objects recorded. One shot of a shelf with four things on it is one and four. |
| A must-find line lost its *Found* tick | Somebody reworded that line on Edit Client after it was found. The tick is keyed to the wording, so it clears rather than moving onto words it was not made for. Press *Found it* again; nothing else is lost. |
| You pressed *Found it* on the iPad and the laptop still shows the line as missing | Reload the laptop. The tick saves to the job and syncs, but nothing polls the client record mid-job; only the device that pressed it repaints at once. Still missing after a reload? The Apps Script is older than `2026-09-19a` and let the laptop's stale copy win (manual §11): redeploy, then press it once more. |
| The brief in the room is long and the cameras are below it | Scroll. It is the intake answers, and the point is that you read them in the room. Firearms stays pinned above the scroll. |
| The brief says *Must find* three times | One line per item on the intake answer, so each line is its own row with its own *Found it*. That is what lets one be ticked without the others. |
| The plan opened with the job showing and both bars at the top shut | That is the layout since 2026-09-20: the job itself never folds; only the two tools at the top do. *Vendors & partners* opens by itself while you are still lining them up on a job not yet active; *Hours & daily close* opens when you tap it. The stage you are in carries a bronze **NOW**. |
| Where did the hours log go? | Under **Hours & daily close**, the second bar at the top of the plan, folded. The bar reads today's hours and the running total; tap it and the entry form, the summary and the projection open under it. Nothing about logging changed. |
| Every stage of the job is open and the tab looks long | By design, since 2026-09-20 — Anthony: *"the main body should just be the job you're working."* Read down the thread: green nodes are behind you, the bronze **NOW** is where you are, grey is ahead. The two things you do not need all day, the vendors and the hours log, are the bars at the top. |
| You want to know where the job stands against the schedule | The strip under the client card on the Job Plan: target start, the day you started, today, *Working day N of M*, days to go, the target end. Same line as the Client Dashboard. |
| The checkbox labels are lower case now, and one box runs the whole width | By design (2026-09-20). A box is a sentence, not a form label. A section with one box spans the row instead of wrapping to four lines in a third of the width. |
| The Move day card has no NOW even though you are moving today | Move day is never marked NOW — the marker follows the rooms and the money. It reads done once Close-out does. Tick its boxes as you go; the count is in its heading. |
| The Hours fold reads *no hours logged today* in amber | The job is active and nothing is logged for today. Open it and log the day before you leave the house — the timesheet is the invoice. On a fixed-price job too: the nag is deliberate there. |
| You logged the day and the fold still reads *no hours logged today* | It should not, since 2026-09-20 — the count on the fold and the schedule strip move the moment the entry saves. On an older build both waited for the next redraw; switch job and back (or reload) and the count catches up. The entry itself was saved either way. |
| The specialist dropdown offers *Other* and no *Contractor TBD* | An older build. Since 2026-09-20 the form offers *Contractor TBD* from the first draw; before that, opening the plan rewrote the list and the placeholder came back only once the form had redrawn (a press of *Save & Confirm* did it). Never pick *Other* — it stores the word as a person. |
| Do I log hours on a fixed-price job? | Yes. Every job, every day — Anthony’s rule (2026-09-20). Fixed price changes what the client is billed, not what we record; the final still issues off the flat fee with nothing gated on the log, and the log is how we find out whether the fee was right. |
| There is no Phase 3, or no phase numbers at all | The numbers went on 2026-09-19. Phase 3 was Move day, which only Home Transition has, so every other job counted 0, 1, 2, 4. The stages are named now, in the order you work them. |
| The room cards are three across and you want the old full-width rows | By design: three on a desk, two on the iPad, one on a phone, so a twelve-room house is four rows rather than twelve. Everything the row carried is on the card — name, status, the three counts, the note. |
| Job Plan rooms are in a strange order | They're in walkthrough order now, matching the estimate. Before 2026-08-03 they were sorted hardest-room-first, which matched nothing. |
| The client estimate has no third-party vendor section at all | Correct when there are no vendors — the empty heading and its $0 lines are suppressed. Add a vendor and it comes back. A vendor with no direct cost (auction house, estate sale company) still shows. |
| A client asks how long each stage of the job will take | The document deliberately carries no dates. Answer from the overall working-day estimate and say plainly that the pace depends on how quickly they make decisions — that is the honest answer and it's also the one that protects you. |
| An heir on site asks you to hand them something | It goes through the representative, in writing. Be pleasant, do not make an exception, and do not take a verbal instruction from anyone who is not the representative — the client document promises this in writing and the estate file has to match it. |
| A client offers you the will, or you are tempted to ask for it | You do not need it and should not hold it. Ask for the *Letters* (the *Certification of Trust* on a trust-only matter, since 2026-10-03) and for the list of designated items. A will you find in the house is sequestered and handed over against a signed receipt. |
| The estimate talks to the client as though they were alive, and they are not | The job is filed under **Home Cleanout** but the owner has died, so it is an **Estate Settlement** — a different client, which Edit Client will not switch to (*Create a new client for this*, since 2026-09-30). Press **+ Add New Client**, which asks for the representative and the date of death, and build the estimate there. Do not edit the wording — it is rebuilt on every render, and the price is wrong too. |
| You are looking for a "client is deceased" tick box | There isn't one, and there should not be. The **service type** is the answer: Estate Settlement, Probate and Contested Probate are deceased-client engagements; Home Editing, Home Transition, Home Cleanout and Home Prep are living-client ones. |
| Intake will not take a client phone or email on an estate job | Correct — the client is deceased, so those fields are disabled and the **Authorized Representative** block below is where the contact goes. That is who every document is addressed to and where the estimate is sent. |
| The agreement is the wrong one of the two forms | Same cause — it reads the service type, exactly like the estimate. Across living and estate it is a new client (**+ Add New Client**); within the family, change the service type on Build Estimate or in Edit Client and re-generate. The routing changed on 2026-08-03; anything issued before then is worth re-reading. |
| The final invoice says the client was notified that the job ran over, and nobody called them | Removed 2026-09-11 — the whole line is gone, in both directions. It printed on *every* overage however small, and the app has never recorded a notification at any size. An estimate is an estimate, and an accepted change order moves the figure the variance is measured against, so a job run properly cannot show a real overrun there anyway. **Re-print any final issued before today.** If you are looking at a genuine overrun, the fix is upstream: raise a change order while the extra hours are still ahead of you. |
| The final invoice shows a $1 credit to a client who paid exactly what we asked | Fixed 2026-09-11. The *"Received ahead of the invoiced schedule"* line was comparing two figures that had each already been rounded to the dollar, so a fifty-cent difference — a client paying the exact 25% of an odd total while we asked for the rounded one — came out as a whole dollar. **The balance due was never wrong**, so nothing was mis-billed. Re-print and the line is gone. |
| The agreement says 15% of vendor invoices, but the estimate charges no vendor fee | Fixed 2026-09-08. The fee came off on 2026-08-02 and the agreement clause was not updated with it, so every agreement generated in between promised a fee the invoice never bills. Re-generate it — §3.5 now reads *Vendor Coordination*, no fee, on the standard form, and the estate form's fee table says *at cost — no fee*. If a client has already signed one, the invoice governs and charges less, not more. |
| There are two agreement files in the client's Drive folder | The folder predates 2026-09-11, when the app stopped filing the bare agreement beside the packet. **Keep the one named *Havellin Services Agreement*** — that is the signing packet, with the estimate attached as Exhibit A. The one ending *_Agreement.html* is the terms without the exhibit and is not signable; never send it. |
| The client asks where Exhibit A is | You sent the agreement on its own. Use **🖨 Print** on the *Signing packet sent* row — one PDF with the approved estimate attached as Exhibit A — and send that. New 2026-09-08. |
| The estate document reads nothing like the home editing one | Correct as of 2026-08-03. Estate and probate stages are written for the representative — catalogue first, nothing removed until authorised in writing, releases receipted. Home Editing keeps the decision-paced language because there the client really is standing in the house deciding. |
| A vendor you entered is not named anywhere in the job plan stages | It is filed under a category the stage bucketer does not recognise. Check its Category in the Vendor Directory — valuers land in the sorting stage, removal and sale in disposition, cleaning and finishing trades in close-out, everything else in disposition. |
| A client asks why the estimate has no dates | Deliberate, and the document does not explain it either. Answer from the overall working-day estimate and say the pace depends on how quickly they make decisions. Do not write dates onto the document. |
| You see the word "licensed" describing Havellin anywhere | Wrong — we are **insured and bonded**, not licensed. Corrected in the app 2026-08-03; take it out of anything you have written yourself. *Licensed FFL* for a firearms vendor is a different thing and is fine. |
| There is no **✉ Send estimate** | The estimate is not approved yet: the send appears on *Estimate sent to client* once a manager approves. Press **Submit for approval**, or wait for the PIN. |
| A client shows **📁 Create Drive folder** and you know the folder is made automatically | Read which of the **three** it says. **Creating Drive folder…** means it is being made right now — wait a few seconds, it is not a button. **Create Drive folder** means the automatic attempt at intake genuinely failed, and pressing it is the right thing to do. **Drive** means it exists. Opening a client within a few seconds of saving intake will normally show the middle one. |
| You pressed **Create Drive folder** right after making the client and it turned into a link | Nothing is wrong and there is nothing to clean up. Before 2026-09-22 the button was offered while the folder was already being made, so pressing it asked for the same folder twice; the server hands back the one that exists rather than making a second. That window no longer shows a button at all. |
| A badge says **CHECK THE FROM ADDRESS before you send** | The app could not reach Gmail and fell back to your computer's own mail program, so the email may be leaving from a personal account rather than your Havellin one. Look at the From line before sending. The usual cause is Gmail not being set up on that device, or the Google sign-in being closed. |
| A manager approval email arrived from somebody's personal address | It was sent before 2026-09-22, when the request was handed to the machine's default mail program. It is fixed — approval requests now draft in the Havellin mailbox. Nothing needs re-sending; the estimate and the PIN are unaffected. |
| Build Estimate is locked | Out for approval: wait for the PIN or a deny. Approved: press **✎ Edit estimate** on the timeline, which un-approves it. Once the signing packet has been sent it is a Change Order. |
| Staffing refuses / Job Plan staffing section is hidden | The job isn't marked Won. Record the client's acceptance on the dashboard. |
| **✓ Record the signed agreement** or **✓ Record payment** is not there | Each appears on its own row once that row is lit, so the step before comes first. A DocuSign agreement has no Record button: its row carries **↻ Check DocuSign now**. |
| Save Hours Entry is greyed out | Either the team isn't confirmed, or the deposit isn't in — the bar says which. |
| A change order isn't on the final invoice | It was never marked accepted. Open the client, find it on the **Change Orders** card, and press **Get Acceptance** on its row. **Every change order raised before 2026-09-29 is in this state** — until then that button was not on the page. |
| The Change Orders card says *Awaiting acceptance* and there is nothing to press | The page is older than 2026-09-29, when the buttons sat in a hidden row. Reload: every row carries **PDF**, and **Get Acceptance** until the client accepts. |
| You pressed *Create Change Order* and nothing seemed to happen | On a page older than 2026-09-29 the message went to a screen you could not see and the card did not redraw — the change order **did** save. Reload and look at the card before raising it again. If there are two, print and accept only one: a change order cannot be deleted, and the one left unaccepted moves nothing and is never billed. |
| The printed change order says the extra hours carry the rush premium or the discount | Correct on an hourly job (2026-09-29): they are billed like every other hour on the final invoice, premium and discount included. On a fixed-price job the change is priced at the hourly rates printed on it, and the discount never applies to it. On an expedited fixed price saved from 2026-09-30 it also carries the 20% expedited-delivery premium, and says so (*priced at the hourly rates shown plus the 20% expedited-delivery premium*); one raised before then, or on an older fixed fee, keeps its plain-rate price. |
| The change order form has no box for a dollar amount | Correct — it carries **hours** now (changed 2026-09-11). Type the extra concierge and specialist hours. You are not billing the client for the change order; you are recording their authority for the extra work, and the hours are billed through your timesheet on the final invoice like every other hour. On a fixed-price job they are converted at the estimate’s rates (plus the 20% premium on an expedited one saved from 2026-09-30) and added to the fee instead. On Home Prep the one dollar box is an added vendor’s estimated cost (2026-09-30). |
| A change order won’t save | It has no hours on it. A change order with no hours would sit on the timeline and the invoice reading as an agreed change while agreeing to nothing, so the app refuses it. On Home Prep it needs concierge hours, an added prep vendor, or both, and an added vendor needs its estimated cost (2026-09-30); on a job with prep vendors on its estimate, the hours, an added prep vendor, or both (since 2026-09-30, the same day). |
| A change order on a fixed-price job shows a dollar amount | Correct, since 2026-09-25. On a flat fee nothing else charges the added scope — the change order’s price is exactly what the final invoice adds, so the client signs the price, not just the hours. On an expedited fixed price saved from 2026-09-30 that price includes the 20% expedited-delivery premium: $2,000 of hours prints as $2,400. On an ordinary hourly job there is still no price on it. |
| A fixed-price job is over the hours and the screen says *margin at risk* instead of *STOP* | That is right. On a flat fee the overrun comes out of our margin, not the client’s bill, so there is nothing to stop for and nobody to notify. Raise a change order only if the extra time is work beyond the agreed scope. |
| A printed change order reads *Reason: scope_add* | It was printed before 2026-09-25, when the reason printed as its internal key. Print it again; it reads *Scope addition — new rooms or services* now. |
| A change order was accepted and the hours still read *OVERAGE* or *STOP* | Check it is really marked accepted (**Get Acceptance**) — an unaccepted change order moves nothing. If it is accepted and the hours are still red, the job is past even the hours the client authorised: that is a real overrun, and the same conversation as before. |
| The estimated hours on the dashboard or the Job Plan are bigger than the estimate | Correct since 2026-09-25: they include the accepted change orders, and the line under the figure says so (*incl. … by change order*). |
| The schedule line says 8 working days and the estimate says 6 | An accepted change order lengthened the plan, and the line says *incl. 2 days by change order*. The estimate still states the length the client first signed against. |
| Change order #2 starts from a bigger number than the estimate | Correct — it starts from the estimate plus the change orders already accepted, so its revised total is the real one. |
| A Home Prep client signed with vendors only, and now the house needs clearing too | Raise a change order for your hours **before** you do the work (2026-09-25). It states your hours and your rate — the rate their agreement’s §3.3 already names — the client accepts it, and the Job Plan opens an hours log. Log the hands-on hours as you work them; the final bills them at the rate on top of the 30% fee. |
| **A Home Prep job, or a job with prep bundled, needs a trade the estimate did not carry** | Raise a change order and pick the trade under **Add a prep vendor**, with **Its estimated cost** (2026-09-30); it can carry concierge hours as well, or the vendor alone. The client sees the vendor, its estimated cost and the 30% fee at that cost, and accepts it. The vendor then joins the prep vendors on the Job Plan to be booked and quoted, and the final bills the fee on its *Quote $*. An unaccepted change order adds nothing. It was standalone Home Prep only until later the same day: a job with prep bundled into the labour has the box too, under its hours (not a fixed price from before 2026-09-23 whose fee carries the prep fee inside it). |
| A Home Prep client asks why their agreement names an hourly rate when they are paying the 30% fee | It is the rate for hands-on work they might ask for **after** signing — clearing a room the painters need empty, say — and it is only ever billed on a change order they sign first (2026-09-25). Nothing is billed at it unless they do. The 30% fee covers coordinating the vendors, as it always has. |
| The specialist box is greyed out on a Home Prep change order | Correct. Prep has no crew — concierge hours only — and Save refuses specialist hours. |
| *Final invoice sent* says a change order added hours and none are logged | Log them on the Job Plan before you send the final. It will send without them, but then it bills the Home Sale Preparation Fee alone and the hours you worked are never charged. |
| The change-order form inside an estate agreement has no dollar lines | Correct on an hourly job since 2026-09-25: the hours are billed as they are worked. On a fixed price it asks for the price of the change and the revised fee; on an expedited one saved from 2026-09-30 the price line adds *plus the twenty percent (20%) expedited-delivery premium*. Regenerate any estate agreement made before 2026-09-25 before it is signed. |
| A client asks for the *receipts* for the moving materials | There are none, and the documents no longer promise any. Materials are a **fixed package price** you picked on the estimate — not cost-plus, no handling fee. Four client surfaces said otherwise until 2026-09-11, two of them contracts. If they are holding one of those, re-send the current estimate and agreement; the price has not changed. |
| A prep client’s agreement quotes $150/hour | It was generated before 2026-09-11. A Home Prep engagement bills **no hours at all** — the fee is 30% of vendor spend — and the contract said both things at once. Re-generate and re-send it; nothing about the price changed. |
| A vendor’s quote is on the wrong trade’s line | Fixed 2026-09-11. Removing a line from an estimate used to shift every quote below it up one, so the cleaner’s row could come back carrying the landscaper’s name and price. If you are looking at a job where that already happened, re-enter the quotes on the Job Plan — they will stay put now. |
| The figures on the Client Dashboard read dashes, or the referral leaderboard is empty | The clients have not finished loading on this device. Both correct themselves when they land. If the list says *Could not reach the client list*, the app cannot reach the sheet — check the connection and reload the page. It shows dashes rather than zeros on purpose, because “0 won, 0 lost” is a claim and it has not read anything yet. *(Win / Loss was its own tab with a ↺ Refresh button until 2026-09-23; both are gone.)* |
| Two tabs are showing on top of each other | You printed a change order on a build older than 2026-09-11. Reload the page. It is fixed, along with the PDF being named after the app instead of the client. |
| A firm does two things and you want it under both | Press **Edit** on its card in the Vendors tab and add the second trade to the Category field after a semicolon — *Art Appraiser; Antiques & Furniture Appraiser*. Do not add a second row for the same firm. |
| Saving a vendor says the name is already in the directory | It is, and that is the guard working. A job remembers its vendor by *name*, so two rows under one name make every later lookup pick between them at random. Edit the existing card instead. |
| The category suggestions go quiet once you type a semicolon | They shouldn't any more (fixed 2026-09-09). If they do, you are on a stale build — reload the page. Note the suggestion list carries what you already typed, so the whole line stays in the box. |
| A firm's second trade is on the wrong Build Estimate card | Cards are driven by the vendor's single *Category group*, not by each trade. File the firm under the group you engage it for most; its other trade appears on that card. Nothing is lost — it is still assignable and the Job Plan finds it by name. |
| An appraiser came back unticked as *Independent* | The directory lists that firm as also buying, or as an auction house or estate sale company. The app unticks it and says which trade. Tick it back if you have a reason; the note tells you what it saw. |
| You have the owner's cell but there is only one phone box | You are on a build older than 2026-09-18 — reload the page. A vendor now has an office line plus two contacts, each with their own mobile and email, and a partner has a direct line, a mobile, the firm's office number with an extension, and the assistant. |
| A vendor card offers no *Text* button | Nobody there has given you a mobile yet. Text is only ever offered on a cell — a switchboard cannot receive one. Put the mobile on the contact slot (**Quick edit**) and the button appears. |
| A vendor card reads *name not recorded* beside a number | Somebody typed the number and not the name, which is the app keeping the useful half rather than refusing the entry. Press **Quick edit** and fill the name in. |
| A partner's card says *⚠ firm main line, not direct* | That number is the firm's switchboard, recorded back when there was only one phone field — it was true of **32 of the 79 partners**. It still dials, so nothing is blocked. Move it into *Office phone* on the partner's card and the flag clears. **To work through all of them at once, search the Referral Partners tab for *main line*.** |
| Partner contact fields will not save | The Referral Partners Apps Script has not been redeployed. That sheet is read by column position, so the new columns need the backend pasted, deployed as a *New version*, and **backfillIds** run once. Vendors need none of this — their sheet creates its own columns. |
| Somebody at the firm calls and the dialer still shows a number | Their mobile is not in the directory yet, or the Quo sync has not run since it was added. Add it to a contact slot, then run **pushQuoAll**. A vendor row now produces a contact for the firm *and* one for each person with their own number, so Andy calling shows as Andy. |
| A vendor isn't in the Job Plan picker | They aren't set to *Active* in the Vendor Directory. |
| Saving a new vendor sits on *Saving…* and the form never closes | The write is still in flight — the sheet's web app can take several seconds to wake up. **Do not press Save again.** The button is disabled while it works, and an add is never retried automatically, so a second press is what writes the vendor twice. If nothing has happened after 45 seconds the app re-enables the button, reloads the directory and tells you to check whether the vendor is already there. |
| The form says the vendor is already in the directory | It is, under that name, and one firm is **one row** — two rows under one name break every later lookup, because the name is what identifies a vendor. Close the form and press **Edit** on the existing card. Genuinely a different firm with the same name? Put something in the name that tells them apart. |
| A crew member isn't in the staffing dropdown | They aren't *Active* on the Contractors tab. |
| The ✕ on a contractor asks for a PIN, or refuses and says to set them Inactive | Both by design (2026-09-30). Deleting takes the manager PIN. Someone named on any job — as its concierge, on its crew, on its estimate or in its hours — is never deleted: set their status to **Inactive** (Full edit → Status). They drop out of the staffing dropdowns and stay on every job that names them. |
| The Contractors columns are in a different order than they were | Alphabetical by name since 2026-09-20, founders included — and the staffing dropdowns read the same way. Nothing else about a record changed. |
| Fixed-price toggle is greyed out | The estimate is out for approval or approved, which locks every control on Build Estimate. Wait for the PIN or a deny, or press **✎ Edit estimate** on the timeline. Every service but Home Prep can be quoted fixed (probate since 2026-09-20); on Home Prep the box is not shown. |
| The estimate won't save | No rooms are scored. Score at least one — and read the coverage badge before you settle for one. |
| "Every included room needs a volume and complexity score" — and it names rooms you deliberately left out | **Fixed 2026-09-10.** Save and Submit were counting out-of-scope rooms as unscored, and an ✕ room has its volume and complexity boxes greyed out, so there was no way to comply. If you see this now, the rooms it names are genuinely still *blank*, not ✕ — tap each one round to ✓ and score it, or round to ✕ to put it out of scope. |
| Red badge: "walkthrough looks incomplete" | Fewer bedrooms or baths accounted for than intake recorded. Either score them, or mark the ones we're not touching **✕ out of scope**. It won't block you; don't ignore it without knowing why. |
| You marked rooms out of scope and the badge still complains | Check they're on **✕** and not blank — the box cycles blank → ✓ → ✕, so one tap from blank only gets you to ✓. Blank reads as "not looked at yet". Also check the room is one intake actually counted: outbuildings don't count toward the main-house bed and bath numbers. |
| The badge says "2 scored, 5 out of scope" — is that a problem? | No, that's the confirmation. It's green. It's telling you the walkthrough is complete and reminding you five rooms are deliberately not ours, so nothing looks like an oversight later. |
| Red badge says "1 of 2 half baths" and you've ticked the only one you can find | There are five *Half Bath* rows, not one — Entry & Living, Kitchen & Utility, both bedroom floors, and *Pool / Cabana Half Bath* under Exterior & Auxiliary. Tick the one matching where the powder room is. If the house genuinely has fewer half baths than intake recorded, the intake figure is wrong — fix it there. |
| You ticked *Additional Bathroom(s)* for a powder room and the badge got worse | That row counts as a *full* bath. Untick it and use a *Half Bath* row instead. |
| The guest house has its own kitchen and bath — where are those rows? | Gone on purpose. Score the whole building on one row, sized by bedrooms. Its kitchen and bath are already in that weight. |
| Can't find the casita | There isn't one any more — the row came out on 2026-09-18. Bedroom and a bath: use the free-text *Additional Outbuilding Room* row in **Outbuildings & Guest Quarters** and type *Casita* over the name. Living space or a kitchenette in it: *Guest House — 1 bedroom*. Don't leave it unscored because the word isn't on the list. |
| Can't find the pool house / used to be two of them | One row now, and it is under **Exterior & Auxiliary**, not Outbuildings: *Pool House — with living quarters*. It assumes a bedroom, a kitchen and a bath, so only tick it for a pool house that actually has them — a bare changing room is the *Pool / Cabana Half Bath* row. |
| Room hours jumped since you last used the app | Fixed 2026-08-03. Rows used to show the packing step only — about a quarter of the work — so every room understated itself by 3.7×. Nothing got more expensive; the row is finally telling the truth. |
| A garage or patio costs far more than you expected | Exterior rooms add load *on top of* the square footage, and they are charged the full estate step set including documentation. A 2-car garage carries the same content weight as the kitchen. Known and queued for a pricing pass — flag it if it looks wrong on a live job. |
| The whole house is packed and setting nineteen sliders is absurd | Use the **How full is this house?** chips above the room grid. Press it when you walk in, before ticking anything — every room you tick then opens at that fullness. Then fix the odd room that does not fit. |
| You set the house to Packed and the rooms you ticked afterwards all opened at 3 | Fixed 2026-09-10 — the chip used to apply once, to whatever was ticked at that moment. On the current build it seeds every room you tick from then on. **Any estimate you priced that way is under-scored on the rooms you did later**, and because volume is averaged across the whole property that moves the price of the entire job. Reopen it and press the chip again. |
| You pressed a chip and one room did not change | Working as intended: you had typed that room's volume by hand, so it is yours and the chip leaves it alone. The line under the grid tells you how many it held back. To hand it back to the chip, untick the room and tick it again. |
| Where did the Third-Party Vendors card go? | Moved below Notable Collections and Vehicles & Watercraft on 2026-09-10. It is the collections and the vehicles that tell you whether this job needs an auction house or an appraiser, so the question is asked after you have walked the house rather than before. |
| A client asks whether the vendor prices on their estimate are guaranteed | They are not, and the document says so above both vendor tables. Each vendor sets its own final price and invoices the client directly, so they see the real bill. Havellin never marks up a vendor's invoice; on home-prep vendors our 30% is charged on what the vendors actually bill. |
| A long-tenured house does not price higher than a new one | Correct as of 2026-08-03. Years in home used to multiply crew hours as well, on top of the volume you had already scored — the same fact counted twice. It now only adds concierge coordination time. If the house really is packed, say so with *volume*: that is the input that carries it. |
| The quote is above the reference range on an ordinary house | There is no hand-typed range any more (2026-09-30): the **Pricing Reference Check** is this house priced by the engine at Normal and at Full, with the same rooms and crew rule. *Scored fuller than Full* is information, like *lighter than Normal* above — do not score down to move it. |
| Crew size keeps changing by itself | Correct — it tracks the recommendation while you score. It stops the moment you set it yourself. On an hourly quote a step up can lower the price a little, because the bigger crew finishes sooner and the concierge bills fewer hours: intended, and the quote bills the hours worked (Anthony, 2026-09-30: *"we don't want jobs stretching over weeks, so we bump up crew size to keep work days down"*). A fixed-price suggestion does not drop with it, and since 2026-09-30 the badge under the crew says so. |
| Crew badge: "2 assigned, 6 recommended — runs 23 days instead of 9" | You've staffed below what the job needs. That's allowed and it is priced as staffed — longer job, more concierge days, higher fee. Go back up unless the small crew is a real constraint. |
| You are on a probate job and want to quote a flat fee | You can, since 2026-09-20 — the toggle used to be greyed out on Probate and Contested Probate and is not any more. The suggested fee carries a wider contingency on those two (25% probate, 35% contested, against 20% elsewhere) because they are the matters most likely to run long. It is still only a suggestion: type your own number or round it down. |
| The suggested fee jumped when you changed the service type | Two things moved, both correctly: probate and contested probate price heavier work per room *and* carry a wider contingency. The dialog tells you the contingency changed. A fee you typed by hand is never repriced underneath you — the panel flags that the suggestion moved and leaves your figure alone. |
| A client on a fixed price asks what happens if they cancel | The deposit is earned at signature and does not come back; 75% is earned at the midpoint; the whole fee at completion, plus any vendor booking we cannot cancel. Hours never enter into it. Read them the clause or call Anthony — see Step 6. Do not offer to refund the deposit. |
| A client on an hourly job asks what happens if they cancel | They owe the hours worked and the materials used up to the day they stop. The deposit is earned at signature and does not come back, even where it is more than the work done (since 2026-09-30; until then the agreement refunded the unused part). Anything they paid beyond both the deposit and the work done goes back to them: the app works it out when the job is closed out (since 2026-10-01; Step 13). Read them the clause or call Anthony — see Step 6. Do not offer to refund the deposit. |
| A client asks whether they can pay by card | No. Since 2026-09-30 the agreement agrees: §3.9 names only an ACH transfer through our payment link, a wire, or a cheque (until then it offered a card, against the rule). If a client insists on a card, the answer is a cheque or a wire. |
| A fixed-price final invoice shows no hours anywhere | Correct. A flat fee does not vary with the time spent, so the final states the fee and nothing else — and it issues even with an empty timesheet. That is not permission to skip the log: you still record every day (Step 10b), it is simply not what the client is billed from. |
| Fixed price warns *the estimate has moved since you set this fee* | A room score, the crew or an adjustment changed after you typed the fee, so the suggestion is no longer what it was — the warning names both figures. *Use $X* takes the new suggestion; *Keep $Y* stands by yours and clears it. It no longer fires when you merely round the fee down (before 2026-09-20 it did, and called the rounding a +17% markup). |
| The suggested fixed fee is an awkward number ($25,275) | Type the figure you want in the box, or tap *Round to $25,000* under it. What you type is what prints on the estimate, the agreement and the invoices. |
| You rounded the fixed fee and want to know what the invoices will bill | Your figure, plus the rush and discount lines and any 30% prep fee on it: the deposit is half of that total, the midpoint a quarter, and the final closes it out without reading the hours log. |
| Crew badge warns "N assigned but only M recommended" | You've set a crew bigger than the scope needs, which *lowers* an hourly quote: the concierge is on site fewer hours. A fixed-price suggestion is priced as the job would run with two specialists whatever the crew (2026-09-30), so it does not drop. Drop back unless the extra hands are genuinely required. |
| The timeline planner says a date "is not reachable" | Six specialists is the cap. Counter with the days it quotes, or cut scope. |
