| A vendor category isn't on any of the six cards | It isn't in the Vendor Directory under that Category Group. Add it there and reload. |
| The vendor cards have nothing to pick, or an amber *Vendor Directory not loaded* sits above them | The directory has not loaded on this device — usually its URL is missing from ⚙ Settings, as the warning says; on a slow connection, give it a moment or reload. Since 2026-09-30 the warning shows whenever that is so, on every service; before, it could never show on a labour job, because the End-of-Job Logistics card always lists its own categories. |
| Looking for the Home Prep card and the *Include in estimate* tick box | Both gone as of 2026-08-03. Prep goes in the *Property Preparation* card with every other vendor, and having a line on it *is* including it. Nothing to tick. |
| An old estimate you reopen prices higher than it did | Expected, and it's a fix. Prep lines used to be dropped unless a tick box was set that the card adding them never touched — those lines now price. Third-party vendor lines also book coordination hours now. Re-read it before you send it. |
| Adding a vendor pushed the quote up | Correct since 2026-08-03. Each line books concierge coordination time (a mover 3 hrs, an online auction 6, an estate sale company 1.5, a hauler 1; the online auction and the estate sale company since 2026-10-01, when they were 2 and 4). A line already on a saved estimate keeps the hours it was quoted at. We charge no percentage on vendors, so this is how that work gets paid for. |
| A Home Prep job shows only one vendor card | Correct. A standalone prep job has nothing else to price — the client estimate and the Job Plan only ever carried prep vendors. If it truly needs a hauler, book it as a prep trade or run the job as a Cleanout with prep bundled. |
| A prep cost you typed into the card reads $0 in the totals | Fixed 2026-08-03. It was a parsing bug — the formatted "$8,000" was read as zero on every keystroke while the field kept showing it. If you're still seeing it, you're on a cached build; hard-refresh. |
| You disagree with the hours a vendor books | Say so — that is what the touch count is for. Work out how many separate things you actually have to do with that trade; the number is half of that. Anthony changes one integer and every future estimate follows. |
| Not sure whether to fill in *Coord hrs* on the Job Plan | Optional, always. It bills nothing and blocks nothing — your logged hours already cover that time. Enter it when it's obvious, skip it when it isn't. |
| **There is no "Appraise" in the disposition chips** | There is not meant to be, and it is not missing. Appraise is a separate **⚑ Appraise** toggle just under the chips (new 2026-09-11), because an appraisal is not a destination — it is the step *before* one. Press the toggle *and* chip the disposition: the item goes to the appraisal worklist and still shows as heading for Auction. First appraise, then sell. |
| Something needs valuing but its category is Furniture or General/Household | That is exactly what the toggle is for. Art, jewellery, silver, antiques, rugs, collectibles, firearms, wine and instruments go to the worklist on their own; nothing else does. Press **⚑ Appraise** in the house, or tick **Needs Appraisal** on the row that evening. There was no way to do either before 2026-09-11. |
| You pressed ⚑ Appraise, then photographed the next object and it was not flagged | Working as intended. The toggle resets after every shot; the *disposition* chip deliberately does not. A whole shelf going to auction is one decision, but "send this one to a specialist" is about the single object in front of you. Press it again for each piece. |
| A collection you marked **Appraise** on the estimate is not on the Appraisal Worklist | If you imported it before 2026-09-11 the instruction was lost on the way in — it arrived as *Hold* with no appraisal flag, so unless the guessed category happened to be an intrinsic one it was reported nowhere. Open the row and tick **Needs Appraisal**. Fixed for anything imported since; it now arrives as *Not yet decided* with the flag set. |
| The release approval request says **NOT YET APPRAISED** on a line | That line is flagged for a specialist, no appraiser is linked and no waiver is recorded. **It is not a block** — the representative can properly decide to release it, and the line stays on the request. It is there so nobody initials away an object without knowing the estate will have no independent record of what it was worth. Either get it valued, link an appraiser, or **Waive** with a reason. |
| The Job Admin & Inv tab looks completely different | Rebuilt 2026-09-01. It is grouped by disposition now, with *Not yet decided* at the top as your worklist. The 29-column table and the four column buttons above it are gone; every one of those fields lives in the item panel behind the **▾** on each row. |
| An item jumped to a different section when you set its disposition | Working as intended. The list is grouped by disposition, so changing one moves the item. Find it under its new heading. |
| The Job Admin & Inv tab looks half empty | It opens on **Today** when anything was shot today, once for each client you open. Press **All**, or **Clear filters**; since 2026-09-30 All stays chosen (it used to snap back to Today on every redraw on a day with a capture). |
| Photos show as little symbols instead of thumbnails | Those photos have not been pulled down to this device yet — they are safe in Drive. If every one is a symbol, the tab says why at the top; tell Anthony, the Apps Script needs redeploying. |
| The client schedule prints *IN PROGRESS* | Correct, and leave it. Not every line is ticked *Reviewed* yet. We give clients work in progress on purpose; the stamp is what makes that honest. Finish the review and it prints *REVIEWED*. |
| An item you want approval for is not on the Approval Request | It is set to **Keep** or **Hold** (nothing is leaving, so there is nothing to approve), it has no disposition at all yet, or every fiduciary has already approved it (until 2026-10-03, any approval date took it off, whoever had signed). Set the disposition first. |
| A line you recorded an approval for is back on the Approval Request, or its row reads *approval incomplete* | Not every fiduciary has approved it (new 2026-10-03): the client records a co-executor or co-trustee whose name is not on the approval — perhaps one added to the client since it was signed. Hover the row for who. Get their signature, then select the line and press **Record approval**, ticking whoever signed (since 2026-10-05 it records them as they come in). |
| **Record approval** says *… has not signed yet: these lines stay on the next request until they do* | Working as intended (new 2026-10-05, P20). It recorded the signatures you ticked, each with its date; the lines are approved once every fiduciary has signed. When the rest come back, record them the same way. Until 2026-10-05 it refused instead (*Not recorded: … is not ticked*). It refuses only with nobody ticked. If someone listed on the client is not a fiduciary at all, correct the client record first. |
| A row reads *ratification owed*, or the Approval Request has a section *Already released: for ratification* | The line left the property before every fiduciary had approved it (new 2026-10-05): usually a co-trustee recorded after the hand-over. Nothing is undone. Send the request, get the missing signature, and record it with **Record approval**; the line drops off once everyone has signed. |
| The app refused a sale or a release: *Havellin and its people never buy or receive estate property* | Working as intended (new 2026-10-03). The recipient you typed is one of Havellin's people (anyone in the contractor directory, a manager, the job's concierge). Nothing was changed. Nobody at Havellin buys or is given estate property, at any price. |
| A living client's row reads *⚠ … works with Havellin*, or their approval request has *Property going to someone who works with Havellin* | Working as intended (new 2026-10-05). The line is being sold or given to one of Havellin's people. On a living client's job that is the client's decision, so it is flagged, not refused, and the request names it so the client signs knowing. If the recipient was typed by mistake, correct it on the row. |
| A row reads *no signed receipt*, or Job Admin says *Signed receipt for every item released to a person* with a count short of the total | Those lines are released to a person and no signed receipt filed to Drive lists them (new 2026-10-03). On **Releases & signed papers**, press **Print receipt** for that person, get it signed, then **File signed receipt**. Typing into *Receipt / Doc* does not clear it. |
| A bequest on the desk's card reads *not yet found* | No line is matched to it. Find the item and put its number in the bequest's **Match** box. If it is not in the house, tell the representative: the app does not decide what that means. |
| A value shows *not stated* in red on the client schedule | That line has a value with no **Valuation Source**. Open the item and set one, and put what you used in *Valuation Basis / Comps*. We promise the source on our own website. |
| A red chip bottom-left: *N change(s) not saved* | Tap it. It opens and names which write is stuck, which sheet it was going to, and what the server said back. **Try again** resends. Nothing is lost while it sits there — the work is queued, not dropped. |
| The chip says *Apps Script needs redeploying* | The sheet script answered *Unknown type*: it does not know this save, so it is genuinely older than the app. Anthony redeploys it (Extensions → Apps Script → Deploy → Manage deployments → New version), then you press **Try again**. |
| The chip says *the Apps Script returned an error* | The script crashed on that one write. It is held rather than retried, because the same request would fail the same way. Tap the chip, screenshot the error line and send it on — **do not go redeploying anything on the strength of this**. Until 2026-09-01 the app called this a redeploy, and it was wrong to. |
| "No payment link was created" after **🏦 ACH payment link** | Stripe refused it or the script could not reach it. Nothing was sent to the client. Send the deposit invoice on its own and record the payment by hand when it arrives; tell Anthony what the message said. |
| The link was refused because it "is not ACH-only" | Stripe would have let the client pay by card on it, so the app switched the link off rather than send it. Cards cost us about 45× what a transfer does. Anthony fixes it in the Stripe Dashboard (Settings → Payments → Payment methods: cards **off**, ACH Direct Debit **on**), then press the button again. |
| The client says they paid by bank transfer and the app shows nothing | Normal for about four business days. They have authorised it; the money has not arrived. Open the client again in a day or two. **Do not record it by hand** — the app will record it itself when it settles. (If you already did, it matches Stripe's report to your entry rather than recording it twice, since 2026-09-30.) |
| The deposit shows *uncleared* after a bank transfer you recorded by hand | Correct: a transfer recorded by hand is an authorisation, not settled money — the same treatment a cheque gets — and it funds the job either way. If the client paid through our ACH link, the app clears it itself when Stripe reports it settled, matching the report to your entry (since 2026-09-30; it used to record the money a second time). Otherwise press **✓ Mark cleared** beside it on the payments list once the bank shows it final. A transfer recorded twice before 2026-09-30: press **Void** on the one you typed, reason *recorded twice*. |
| A cheque bounced, or a payment was typed twice or on the wrong client | **Void** it on the payments list under the timeline, with the reason. The dialog says first what it takes off; the payment stays on the record, struck through, and counts toward nothing. Then record the money as it really arrived. A payment is never edited: a wrong amount, date or method is voided and recorded again (decided, 2026-10-01). If it was the deposit on a job already running, the deposit step turns red, *Deposit voided: record the replacement payment*, until the replacement is recorded; the job stays active. (Until 2026-09-30 nothing could take a payment back.) |
| A cheque has cleared at the bank but still reads *Uncleared* | Press **✓ Mark cleared** beside it on the payments list. (Until 2026-09-30 nothing in the app could clear it.) |
| The payment recorder has no *Card* | Deliberate (2026-09-30): Havellin takes no card payments. Record how the money really came in — bank transfer, wire, cheque or cash. |
| A banner says the Apps Script deployment is out of date (*2026-09-30*) | The app now needs the *2026-09-30b* version. Tell Anthony it needs redeploying. Until it is, a payment voided or marked cleared on one device can come back as it was from a device that had not been reloaded and then changed the same payment — so reload before you touch a payment somebody else has just changed. A firearm’s serial, recipient or dealer route can drop off the sheet the same way, until the device that recorded it next saves the inventory. |
| "Could not check the payment status … this is not a statement that it has not" | The status check did not reach Stripe. It is not saying the money is absent — it is saying it does not know. It tries again the next time you open the client. |
| Pressing **🏦 ACH payment link** gives you the same link again | Intended. One link per invoice. Two links against one deposit is two ways to pay it, and a client who pays both has overpaid. |
| The ACH button is not on the deposit row | It rides beside the lit deposit step — *Deposit invoice sent*, then *Deposit received* — so it is not there before the agreement is signed, it goes once the full deposit is recorded, and it is never on the midpoint or final rows. Otherwise this device has no Apps Script URL in Settings. |
| The attorney says their paralegal does the inventory — how do I quote it? | Answer **What are we contracted to produce?** on **✎ Edit Client**: *None*, or *Contents list* if they want our photographs and list but will value it themselves. That is the contract term the agreement promises, and it prices the estimate's **Documentation scope** (*None*, or *Capture only*): an estimate nobody has approved follows it (since 2026-09-30); an approved one is flagged with the route instead. Do not pick a different service type — the voice, the written-authority gate and the disposition rules come from the service type and must stay. |
| Intake will not save and says *How this estate is being administered* | Required on every estate job from 2026-09-21, and it has no default. Ask the attorney or the representative: *is this going through probate, or is it a trust?* A pour-over will is **Both**; a family splitting things up with no court and no trust is **Neither**. Nothing else in the app can answer it — the service type is what we were hired to do, and the representative's role is who signs. |
| The Court Inventory comes out empty, or reads *$0* | It lists **Probate-track and Exempt-track items only**. If the contents are held in trust and the lines are tagged *Trust*, there is nothing for it to list — which is correct, and it is why the document now refuses to finalise instead of printing a signed $0 schedule. Use the Estate Inventory Report or the workbook; the trustee's schedule is not built yet. |
| The Court Inventory prints a red block saying this is not a probate matter | Intake or Edit Client records the matter as *Trust* or *Neither*, so a §733.604 schedule is the wrong instrument — there is no court filing for it to be adopted into. Working record only; it will not finalise and carries no signature line. If the matter type is wrong, correct it in Edit Client. |
| A trust job's items are all tagged *Trust* and you did not do it | Correct since 2026-09-21 — the asset track's **default follows the matter type**. On a matter recorded *Trust* an untouched line reads Trust and lands on the *Trust Schedule*. Setting one by hand still overrides it. **The old instruction to set the track line by line on a trust job is withdrawn.** |
| A *Both* (pour-over) job's items still read *Probate* | Also correct, and deliberate. A pour-over will works by pouring anything *not already titled into the trust* through probate, so an untouched line is probate property until somebody says otherwise. Set the track by hand on whatever the trust already held. |
| Where is the trustee's schedule? | **More → Trust Schedule**, on the Job Admin & Inv tab. It is offered on every estate job, not only trust ones, and refuses on its own face when it is the wrong instrument — so it is never a missing button. Since 2026-10-02 (P18) it also goes out in the trust package (**📧 Send trust package**, on the *Trust Information* card). |
| A trust job has no package button, or no *Trust Information* card | The matter type is not recorded as *Trust*. The card is drawn on a matter recorded *Trust* (and the Probate card on *Probate* or *Both*); an unanswered matter on an Estate Settlement, and *Neither*, get neither. Fix it on **Edit Client** (*How is this estate being administered?*). |
| The trust package went to the trustee, not the trustee's attorney | No attorney's email is on the client. It goes to the attorney whenever one is recorded (**Edit Client → Estate Attorney**), copying the trustee; otherwise to the trustee. |
| The Trust Schedule says *this estate has no trust in it* | The matter type is recorded as *Probate* or *Neither*. Either that is right and you want the *Court Inventory* instead, or the matter type is wrong — fix it on **Edit Client**. |
| The Trust Schedule says *no matter type is recorded on this job* | The document still prints and still finalises — somebody tracked those lines as the trust's, which is an answer about the property. The blank is a gap in the job record. Answer it on **Edit Client**. |
| A trustee or their lawyer asks us to prepare the trust accounting | **No, and it goes to Anthony.** The schedule supports their accounting; preparing one is fiduciary work. The document says so on its face and so does the agreement. |
| An agreement on a trust matter is headed *Florida Probate Compliance* | It was generated before 2026-09-21, or the matter type was not recorded when it went out. Fix the matter type on **Edit Client** and regenerate. A trust matter now signs *5.2 Florida Trust Administration Support*, with no §733.604 and no 60-day Letters deadline. |
| A trust matter's agreement still says *Estate of*, *Letters of Administration* or *Client / Personal Representative* | It was generated before 2026-10-03, or the matter type was not answered when it went out (an unanswered matter signs the probate form on purpose). Fix the matter type on **Edit Client** and regenerate: a trust-only matter's form names the trust, the Certification of Trust and the trustee's attorney, and the successor trustee signs. |
| A co-trustee or co-executor asks where they sign the agreement | On their own *Co-Signer* block, named, once they are recorded on **Edit Client** (since 2026-10-03). Since 2026-10-05 they sign it in DocuSign, beside the client, in the same envelope; on the sign-by-hand routes they sign the printed page and you file it with **File the page signed by …** (Step 8). (Until 2026-10-05: always a printed copy, put in the Drive folder by hand.) |
| The Trust Schedule or the Court Inventory has one signature line, and there are two trustees or executors | Record the second one on **Edit Client**; each recorded one then gets a named line (since 2026-10-03). On a pour-over (*Both*), those recorded with a trustee's role sign the Trust Schedule and the others the Court Inventory. |
| A pour-over will — which answer? | **Both.** Some assets probate *into* the trust, so the probate schedule is genuinely right for that half; the app still finalises it and notes that anything tracked Trust belongs on the trustee's schedule. Do not pick one side to keep it simple. |
| The *Documentation level* dropdown is greyed out and you cannot change it | The two questions above it have already decided the level, and you may only ever make a job **stricter**, never looser. The box under them says which answer did it. If it reads *"The 706 question is unanswered, and unknown counts as yes"*, that is the one to go and ask the attorney — answering it *No* lifts the job back to Standard. **From 2026-09-21 you can answer it later**, in Edit Client, on every estate service; before that the intake form was the only place and there was no way out. |
| An estate settlement is in Strict Mode and you never asked for it | Correct behaviour, not a fault: the 706 question defaults to *Unknown* and unknown counts as yes. Ask the attorney and record the real answer in Edit Client. **It does not add manifest work** — that claim was wrong and came off on 2026-09-22. What Strict Mode really does is hold the Court Inventory and the Trust Schedule at DRAFT until every flagged item is appraised or waived, turn the appraisal panel red, make chain of custody mandatory, and add the court-grade records list to the client estimate. Worth getting right before you start booking appraisers, not after. |
| A collection you are adding says *above the $100 grouping cap* | The app has divided the value you recorded by the number of pieces and the answer is over $100 an article. On a 706 estate the federal rule (§20.2031-6(a)) wants each of those articles named, not bundled. **Switch *Add as* to Itemize** — the selector is immediately to the left — or, if they genuinely are one lot, add them anyway and note on the line why. It never stops you: at that moment the value is a walkthrough estimate and the count is often a guess. |
| A **Lots to split** chip has appeared on Job Admin & Inv | One or more lines group several articles under one value and average more than $100 each. Tap the chip to filter to them. Either split the line into named articles, or leave it and let the Appraisal Worklist disclose it — the decision is counsel's, the record is ours. |
| The grouping cap is not showing on an estate you expected it on | It is gated on the **706 question**, not on Strict Mode. If the estate is recorded as filing *No* return, there is no federal itemisation rule to apply and Florida sets no floor of its own, so the app correctly says nothing. Check the answer in Edit Client before assuming it is broken. |
| The Strict Mode box used to give you two dollar figures and now gives one | Corrected on 2026-09-22, and the figure that went was the one that meant nothing. It read *"items are listed individually above $100 instead of $1,000"* — and **nothing in the app ever itemised anything above either number**; there was no control that applied it and no document that used it. **Do not go looking for the setting.** The figure that stayed, the **specialist threshold**, is real: it decides what gets flagged for an appraiser, and it is moved by a recorded *dispute* or a contest — **not** by the documentation level. So a Strict estate and a Standard one both route at $3,000 unless there is a dispute. |
| You cannot find the date of death / the estate attorney / the 706 question on an estate settlement | They are there from 2026-09-21, under **Estate Details**, **Estate Attorney** and **Documentation Standard**. Before that they rendered on probate only, which is why every estate settlement in the app is missing them. The court record — case number, Letters, §733.604 deadline, property sale — is still probate-only, and correctly so. |
| Intake will not save an estate settlement and says *Date of death* | It is required on all three decedent services now, not just probate. It is on the death certificate and counsel has it. Every value on that estate — the schedule, the 706, a trustee's opening carrying value — is a value *at* that date, so there is nothing honest to put in its place. |
| The Job Plan says *Estate attorney on file — not recorded* and you cannot find the field | Fixed 2026-09-21. The line fires on every documentation job, Estate Settlement included, and the field was on the probate form only — so it told you to correct something in Edit Client that Edit Client did not offer. Both forms carry it now, required on probate and optional on an estate settlement. |
| The Documentation scope dropdown isn't there | The job is a home editing, home transition, home cleanout or home prep. Those price no documentation step, so there is nothing to scale and the control hides. If the owner has died the job is an Estate Settlement, and correcting the service type is what brings it back. |
| **Save says "Property value is required"** | The approximate home value was left blank at intake. Open the client on the Client Dashboard, press **✎ Edit Client**, and set *Approx. Home Value* — it sits under Job Details, beside Property Type. Save, press **Build estimate** on the timeline to go back, and press Save Estimate again. **Nothing you scored is lost**, and you must not press **Start over**, which is what would clear it. A Zillow number is fine; it prompts the Premium Estate decision (a hint on a high-value home) and does not move the price. |
| **A new client's estimate already has a discount, a collection, a car or a private note on it** | That is the last client's, carried over by a build older than 2026-09-29. **Reload the page** and open the client again; it will start clean. Then check any estimate priced straight after another before that date: the discount, move styling, the private note, *Walkthrough by*, the collections and the vehicles. |
| ***Walkthrough by* is blank on a client, or Save suddenly asks for a home value you never had to give before** | That client has none on file. Until 2026-09-29 the screen quietly kept the *last* client's — the wrong walker on the estimate, and another house's value letting Save through. Pick yourself in *Walkthrough by*; set the home value on **✎ Edit Client** as in the row above. |
| **The *client needs it sooner* date is empty when you reopen an estimate** | Correct — no estimate records it; it is a question you ask on site. Type it again if you want the planner's advice. |
| **Reset turned Premium Estate off, or left the discount and the private note behind** | It did until 2026-09-29, when both were fixed. It now asks first and gives a blank estimate starting from the client's own record, premium and the documentation scope included. **If you pressed Reset on a premium or estate job and saved before then**, reopen the estimate and check Premium Estate and the scope against intake before anything goes out. |
| **Start over brought the old estimate back** | On a client whose estimate has been saved, that is what it does, and the question says so before you answer: it throws away what is on the screen since the last Save, not the saved estimate. On a client with nothing saved it starts from the intake answers. |
| **After Start over or Reset, the rooms you had just cleared came back under *Offline — restored an unsaved draft*** | Fixed 2026-09-29. The device keeps a copy of unsaved work so a page that drops mid-walkthrough can be recovered with no connection, and both buttons used to leave that copy behind: on a client with nothing saved, opening it offline put the cleared rooms straight back. Both now throw the copy away when you press OK. **If a device still brings back rooms you cleared before then**, press Start over (or Reset) once more and answer OK; nothing saved is touched. |
| **Save says "New home sq ft is required"** on a Home Transition | The new home's size was not recorded. Same route: **✎ Edit Client**, then *Move Destination → New Home Sq Ft*. Move day is priced off the **new** home — the supervision day and the unpack crew's hours — so it genuinely cannot be quoted without it. Your walkthrough is kept. **⚠ If the estimate is already approved this field is frozen**, like square footage and the premium toggle; that needs a Change Order, not an edit. |
| **The Letters chip is red and you cannot find where to record it** | **✎ Edit Client → Authorized Representative → Letters of Administration**, set to *Received* when the certified copy is in hand (or *Not Required* on a matter with no Letters). On a trust the same control is named **Certification of Trust** (since 2026-10-03, P19), and on *Neither* it is not shown at all. **Intake asks it too from 2026-09-22** — but by the time you are reading the chip the client exists, and Client Intake only creates new ones, so Edit Client is the route. Every estate job starts *Pending*. Before that date the question was on neither form: the app read a field that was not there, so it was always Pending and the chip told you it had been recorded at intake. |
| **Save says "Not ready to quote"** and names a contract question | The client is missing *What are we contracted to produce?* or *How is this estate being administered?*, or both. Press **← <client>**, press **Edit Client**, answer it, then press **Build estimate** on the timeline to come back. **The walkthrough on your screen is kept** — nothing you scored is lost, and the refusal says so. Ring the attorney if you do not know the answer; do not guess it to get past the message, because it is what the agreement promises in writing. |
| A line above the fee panel says *Not a quote yet* but there is still a total | That is correct and it is deliberate. The estimate prices while the contract questions are open so you can tell the attorney what each answer costs — what it will not do is **commit** the number. Read the total, have the conversation, answer it on Edit Client, and the line clears. |
| An estate job priced far higher than you expected and nobody chose a tier | A blank tier prices at the **top** of the scale, not at nothing — roughly $6,300 more than *None* on a 3,500 sqft Estate Settlement, and $8,600 on a Probate (measured 2026-09-30). Answer it. Before 2026-09-22 that quote could be saved, sent and papered with nobody having said a word about it; it cannot now. |
| The estimate opened at *None* and I didn't set it | Intake did. **What are we contracted to produce?** on the intake form seeds the estimate's scope. If the tier itself is wrong, correct it on **Edit Client**: an estimate nobody has approved follows it (since 2026-09-30), the open one at once. If only this estimate should differ from what the client was told, change the scope on the estimate — the hint under the dropdown will name the tier intake recorded. |
| You saved the tier on Edit Client and the notice says the approved estimate *is priced at …, and the app does not reprice it on its own* | Correct, from 2026-09-30: a price a manager approved never moves by itself. The notice names the route — **✎ Edit estimate** until the signing packet goes out (the estimate reopens following the new tier; save it and send it for approval again), a change order after. |
| Submit for approval refuses: *the documentation tier on this client changed … after this estimate was saved* | The tier was changed on Edit Client and this saved estimate has not been opened since. Press **✎ Edit estimate** beside the estimate on the dashboard (the refusal names it): it reopens following the new tier, with a line under the Documentation scope saying so. Press **Save Estimate**, then submit. |
| You cannot find the *Documentation level* dropdown any more | It moved on 2026-09-22. It used to sit in the property details on the right; it is now on the **right-hand side under the grey box that explains it**, beside *What are we contracted to produce?* — which is where it belongs, because the two of them are the same conversation. **Nothing was removed.** They ask different things: the tier is *what we hand over*, the level is *how strictly we document it*. |
| Why are there TWO documentation dropdowns? | Because they answer different questions and only one of them is yours to set on most jobs. **What are we contracted to produce?** is the deliverable — it is what the agreement promises and what the estimate prices, and you always answer it. **Documentation level** is how strictly the job is documented, and on an estate the answers above it almost always decide it for you and grey it out. The place you will genuinely use it is a **living-client job** — a downsizing or a cleanout where you want chain of custody made mandatory. There it is the only switch for that. |
| You answered the 706 *No* and the box now says *Formal documentation, set by hand* — you didn't set anything | Fixed 2026-09-22. When a question forces the level to Formal the app writes *Formal* into that dropdown; until this was corrected, answering the question afterwards released the control and left that value sitting there, so the box reported it as your choice. It hands the blank back now. **On a job created before 2026-09-22, open Edit Client and check the level is what you meant** — a stray Formal makes chain of custody mandatory and turns the appraisal panel into a block. |
| You set the level to Formal by hand and the box underneath still says *Standard documentation* | Also fixed 2026-09-22 — the dropdown never told the box it had changed, so it only caught up when you touched one of the questions above it. It repaints as you change it now. **The job was always saved correctly**; it was the explanation that was stale, not the setting. |
| The *Documentation level* dropdown is greyed out and the reason names a *tier*, not the 706 | Somebody answered **What are we contracted to produce?** with *Inventory + appraisals*. That tier sets the floor to Formal, because it is the tier where the client is paying for the appraisals the Formal standard enforces. If that is genuinely what was sold, leave it. If it was picked because it sounded thorough, correct it in Edit Client and the dropdown releases. |
| An old estate shows a tier nobody picked | It is being read off its old *Documentation scope*: *Full* shows as *Inventory with values*, *Capture only* as *Contents list*, *None* as *None*. Nothing was changed and nothing was repriced. Correct it in Edit Client if the attorney actually asked for something else — and note it never reads as *Inventory + appraisals*, so an estate that really was sold appraisals has to be set by hand. |
| The tier menu is empty, or the question is not there at all | Not there at all: the job is a home editing, home transition, home cleanout or home prep — those produce no documentation, so there is nothing to be contracted for. Empty: reload the page. The menu is filled from one catalogue when the app loads. |
| The attorney has not decided what they want yet | Leave it blank and save. It is the one estate question with no default and no refusal. **Come back to it before Step 6** — the agreement is the document that promises it, and it must not go out blank. |
| We agreed a contents list and the agreement promises valuations | The tier was set after the agreement was generated, or never set. Fix the tier in Edit Client; on an approved estimate press **✎ Edit estimate** (since 2026-09-30 the estimate reopens following the tier, and Edit Client's notice names this route), save it, re-approve, and regenerate the agreement. Read §2 and §5.2 of it before it goes out. Once the signing packet has gone out the route is a change order. |
| The client estimate still promises an itemised inventory with values after I set the scope to None | The scope is saved with the estimate. Re-open it on Build Estimate, confirm the dropdown reads *None*, and re-submit for approval — the client estimate and the agreement regenerate from the approved copy. |
| The service dropdown says *Home Editing* and *Home Transition* and I am looking for Downsizing | Renamed 2026-09-08. *Downsizing* is **Home Editing** — with no move to manage, we are editing what the couple takes to the next home — and *Downsizing & Move Management* is **Home Transition**. Same two jobs, same prices, same documents; only the names moved. A client saved under the old name shows the new one everywhere. |
| Clients you deleted are back on the list — or you added one and three old ones came with it | A device that still had the app open from before the deletion saved its whole list. Since 2026-09-08 the sheet refuses those ids and the device drops them itself — you will see *N clients deleted elsewhere — removed from this device too*. If they are genuinely back in the sheet, the Apps Script has not been redeployed since that date; tell Anthony. |
| "N clients deleted elsewhere — removed from this device too" | Not an error and not data loss. Somebody deleted those clients (in the app, or by clearing the sheet) while this device still held them, and this device has just caught up. If one of them was deleted by mistake, Anthony can allow it back from the Apps Script editor before you save again. |
| A custody entry you logged is not there any more | If the job was worked from two devices before 2026-09-11, it was destroyed by the merge — an edit to any field on that row on the other device took the whole newer record and the log with it. Fixed now, but it cannot be recovered; re-enter what you know from the receipt. |
| You removed a custody event and it came back | Only possible on a build before 2026-09-11, or on a device that has not reloaded since. Removal marks the event rather than erasing it now, and the removal is what wins the merge, so it holds everywhere. Reload and check again before re-removing. |
| A tab in the sync spreadsheet is empty and you expect data in it | `Estimates` and `Hours` are dead tabs from an older build — nothing writes to them and nothing ever will. The real estimates are in `EstimateStore` and the real hours in `LogStore`, both as one long line of JSON in column B, which does not look like data at a glance. A tab that is missing altogether (no `LogStore` at all, say) means no hours have ever been logged; the tab is created by the first save. |
| A line on the release approval request is badged *SPECIFIC BEQUEST* or *DISPUTED* | Working as intended, and it is telling you something. The item is flagged on the manifest, the request names it above the table with its item number, and the representative decides — on counsel's advice — whether to initial it. Do not push for the signature and do not remove the flag. If they want it off the request, set its disposition to **Keep** or **Hold**. |
| A line on the request is badged *BEQUEST TO* and a name, or its row reads *⚠ bequest to … · going elsewhere* | New 2026-10-03. The line is matched to a bequest on the desk's list and is proposed to go somewhere other than to the person it names. Usually a mis-sort: set it to *To a person* with that person as the recipient, or to Keep or Hold. If it is deliberate, leave it: the request names the person and the proposal, and the representative decides on counsel's advice. |
| The bulk bar says "2 of them are flagged specific bequest" after you set a disposition | You have just proposed selling or donating property the will or the trust designates for a named person. Usually a mis-selection — check the item numbers it names and put those rows back to Keep or Hold. If it is deliberate, leave it: the request will name them again for the representative. |
| An approval request printed before 2026-09-11 said nothing about a bequest | Correct — it could not. Those two flags reached no printed document until that date, so a bequest could be listed for auction with nothing on the page saying so. Re-read any request issued before then against the manifest's *Specific Bequest* and *Disputed* columns, and re-issue if a flagged line was approved unknowingly. |
| The sheet shows more estimates than you have clients | Records for clients deleted before 2026-09-09 were left behind. Harmless — nothing reads or bills off an estimate whose client is gone. Anthony clears them with `previewOrphanRecords()` then `pruneOrphanRecordsConfirm()` in the Apps Script editor. From 2026-09-09 the sheet tidies itself, so this is a one-off. |
| **You are on a Home Editing job and the estate documents are gone** | Correct as of 2026-09-21, and they were never meant to be there. A living client gets the **Contents Record** instead — the same button, in the same place. Court Inventory, the §733.604 schedule and date-of-death valuation are decedent-only; the Disposition Ledger is on every job since 2026-10-03, on its own card under the inventory. |
| **There is no _Remove_ on a snapshot** | Correct since 2026-10-03: a snapshot is never deleted. Press **Void** and say why; it stays on the list marked *VOIDED*, with your reason, and still prints. |
| **The Disposition Ledger is not under _More_** | It has its own card under the inventory since 2026-10-03, on every job but Home Prep: Print, File to Drive and File signed copy. |
| **The ledger has no signature block** | A line has no disposition. The page names it above the table; decide it, then print the ledger again. |
| **A proceeds statement shows a red ⚠ with two figures** | The statement and the lines it covers disagree by that much, to the cent. Check the statement against each line’s *Gross Proceeds* and *Fees* and correct whichever is wrong; a statement typed wrongly is voided, with the reason, and recorded again. |
| **_Donation receipts on file_ says 1 of 3 although you filed a receipt** | Every donated line needs a receipt now (since 2026-10-03). A charity’s receipt covers the lines going to it when it was filed; file each charity’s on the Donations card, and name the charity on any line that has none. |
| **The Contents Record says *N items left the property with nobody recorded*** | Those lines have a disposition that takes them off the premises and a blank **Channel / Recipient**. Filter the tab on that disposition and fill the names in. That gap is the one thing the document exists to close. |
| Where do you put the name of the person taking something | **Channel / Recipient**, on the row itself. Set the disposition to **Distribute** first — that is the one that means *a named person is taking it*. |
| You are looking for *Gift* in the disposition list | It is called **Distribute**. Same action; the word has to be right on an estate as well, where handing a beneficiary something the will leaves them is a distribution and not a gift. |
| **A firearm marked for a family member reads as blocked** | Working as intended, and fixed on 2026-09-21. A beneficiary is not a licensed dealer, and the transport gate used to accept any name in *Channel / Recipient* as one — so a shotgun set aside for a son read **CLEARED TO CARRY**. Nothing moves until the dealer route is used. |
| A living client’s Drive workbook says *Date of Death* and *§733.604 Inventory Deadline* | The Apps Script has not been redeployed (2026-09-21a). The app is right and the sheet is stale. Once it is deployed, open the client on the Job Admin & Inv tab and make any edit; the sheet is rewritten. |
| A **trust** estate’s workbook says *Letters Issued* or *§733.604 Inventory Deadline* | Either the matter type was never answered, or the Apps Script is on 2026-09-21**a** and needs deploying again (**-b**). Check Edit Client first — a job recorded before 2026-09-21 has no answer, and the app deliberately keeps the court rows when nobody has said otherwise rather than stripping a real deadline off a real probate matter. Answer it, then make any edit on the Job Admin & Inv tab and the sheet is rewritten. |
| A trust estate’s workbook still says *Date of Death* and *Total Estimated FMV* | Correct, and do not ask for it to be removed. Only the **court** rows come off a trust matter — **⚠ the valuation figures come off for a different reason entirely, and only at the *Contents list* or *None* tier** (next row). Everything valued at death is valued the same way whether a personal representative or a successor trustee is administering it — the date-of-death figure is the beneficiaries’ basis, the Form 706 number and the opening carrying value of the trust accounting all at once. |
| An estate’s workbook has **no** *Total Estimated FMV* and the category block counts instead of pricing | Check the tier on **Edit Client**. At *Contents list* and *None* the agreement says **counsel** values the property and Havellin states no opinion of value, so from 2026-09-22 the sheet counts rather than prices — on the screen too. It is the contract, not a fault. The valuation *columns* on the item sheet are still there; what is gone is the headline total, the awaiting count and the FMV rollup, because all three were $0 over columns we were paid to leave empty. |
| You were expecting the valuation figures to be gone and they are still there | Three things to check, in order: the tier is *Inventory with values* or *Inventory + appraisals* (both state values, correctly); **nobody answered the tier at all**, which reads as *Inventory with values* on purpose so that no estate priced before the question existed loses its figures; or the Apps Script has not been deployed since 2026-09-22 and only the screen has moved. Answer the tier on Edit Client, then make any edit on the Job Admin & Inv tab and the sheet is rewritten. |
| *Items Awaiting Valuation* reads the whole item count and never falls | On a *Contents list* or *None* engagement that was correct and unfixable — nobody enters a value on those jobs **by contract**, so the count could only ever equal the item count. It is gone from both surfaces from 2026-09-22. On a *values* engagement it is a real worklist: those items genuinely need a figure, and *No value yet* at the top of the tab filters to them. |
| The MAIV *§20.2031-6(b)* line is still on a capture engagement | Correct, and deliberately. That is a **federal filing requirement on the estate** — an expert appraisal under oath filed with the Form 706 — and it does not stop applying because counsel rather than Havellin is doing the valuing. With nothing valued it reads *“the $3,000 aggregate cannot be tested yet … value them before the return is prepared”*, which is exactly what counsel needs to see. Pass it on; do not ask for it to be hidden. |
| A *Both* (pour-over will) matter keeps every court row | Correct. There really is a probate estate beside the trust, so Letters, the §733.604 deadline and the exempt allowance all apply to the probate half. Nothing is withheld on *Both*. |
| Their workbook is still called *Estate Inventory* and the client is alive | Deliberate — Anthony’s call, and the file is looked up by that name, so renaming it would create a second one. What came off is everything that *claims* a death: the dates, the court deadline, the exempt-property flag and the valuation block. |
| A client asks whether the Contents Record proves they gave something away | No, and the agreement says so in terms. It is our record of what we did at their direction — not an appraisal, not evidence of ownership or title, not a tax or insurance record. If they need any of those, they obtain them. |
| **Document Shredding** (or a dumpster, a hauler, a clean) is no longer on the Job Plan | Correct as of 2026-09-23. The Job Plan lists the end-of-job vendors **the estimate carried**, and nothing else — it used to add all five to every job whether the job needed them or not. Need one after all? **+ Add an end-of-job vendor…** under the list, in *Vendors & partners*. |
| The schedule line no longer shows the target start or the target end | Correct once the job is active (2026-09-23). Every date is counted from the day it was activated, and the target dates it replaced are not printed beside them any more. The estimate and the agreement still carry the dates the client signed against. |
| Pressing **Activate job** asks *"Activate this job today?"* | You are ahead of the target start, and activating fixes the start date for good. **Cancel** unless the crew really is starting today, and press it again on the day they do. |
| Activate job took you off the Client Dashboard | By design (2026-09-23): it lands on that client's Job Plan, which is what you work from once the job is running. The dashboard is one tap away in the nav. *Close job* does not move you. |
| You cannot put the same specialist in two slots | Correct (2026-09-23): one person, one slot. The app refuses it the moment you pick and names the slot they are already in. Only *Contractor TBD* and *Contractor — TC* may repeat. |
| The Job Plan named the walkthrough person as the concierge, not the one assigned at intake | Fixed 2026-09-23: the concierge row starts on the intake assignment. On a job whose team was never confirmed it corrects itself when you open the plan. **If the team was already confirmed**, that was a decision and the app will not overrule it — press **Revise team**, pick the right concierge, and confirm again. |
| A red *Job team confirmed* chip at the top of the Job Plan | Either nobody has pressed **Save & Confirm Job Team** yet, or one person is in two slots — the line under the chips says which. Both are fixed in the *Hours & daily close* bar. Hours cannot be logged until it is green. |
| The brown band says *Send the midpoint invoice* where it used to say *Midpoint invoice sent* | Same step, better words (2026-09-23): the band names what to do next, and the timeline rows below keep the milestone names, because there they are the record of what has happened. |
| You changed the target start in Edit Client and the Job Plan still showed the old dates | Fixed 2026-09-23: the Job Plan redraws every time you open it. Walk back to it and the new dates are there. |
| The rows in *Havellin Services* on a client estimate do not add up to its total | An estimate with home prep vendors on it from before the 2026-09-23 change. The 30% prep fee was *in* the total but printed underneath it, under the prep vendors. It is a row in the services table now. Reopen the estimate, check the rows reach the total, and re-send it. |
| A fixed-price estimate prints a second line under the Fixed Project Fee | Correct (2026-09-23). It is the 30% home prep fee, which sits on top of the flat fee and is charged on what the prep vendors actually bill. The flat fee itself is firm. The agreement states both figures and the invoices bill the fee on the vendors’ real bills. On an estimate saved from 2026-09-30 the expedited-delivery premium and the preferred-client discount are lines under the fee too, when the job carries them. |
| You reopened an older fixed-price estimate and the fee in the box went down | The prep fee was inside that flat fee. Reopening moves it out and puts it on top, so the client’s total is exactly what it was, and the panel says how much moved. Re-send it if the client has already seen the old split; otherwise save and carry on. |
| Can I log the afternoon I spent with the painter? | No, on any job (2026-09-23). Time on the prep trades is what the 30% pays for. On an hourly job logging it bills the client twice; on a fixed-price job it makes the log say the flat fee took longer than it did. |
| A client asks why the prep fee on the final is not the figure on the estimate | The estimate charged 30% of the prep vendors’ quotes; the final charges 30% of each vendor’s *Quote $* figure on the Job Plan, which should be what they actually billed by then — put the real bill in that box before the final goes out. The estimate and the agreement both say the fee is on actual spend. The difference is exactly 30% of the gap between the quotes and the bills. |
| A client asks why the discount on their rush job is more than 10% of the labour | Because on a rush job it comes off the labour *and* the 20% premium charged on it: $100,000 of labour rushed is $120,000, and 10% off that is $12,000. The discount line names its base. Nothing is wrong. |
